--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] ARM-software/ComputeLibrary
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A production multi-ISA CPU/GPU kernel library (MIT) whose CPU core is `arm_gemm`: a bank of
             hand-tuned NEON/SVE/SVE2 micro-kernels selected at runtime by a CpuInfo
             feature-detection layer, including int8 GEMMLowp kernels that use SDOT/UDOT/I8MM
             dot-product instructions with int32 accumulation and an asymmetric (scale +
             zero-point) requantization output stage.
innovation:  Decouple "which SIMD instruction set the binary was compiled with" from "which kernel actually
             runs": a single fat binary carries multiple micro-kernel variants and a runtime
             CpuInfo probe (getauxval(AT_HWCAP)/AT_HWCAP2 on aarch64, CPUID leaves on x86) picks
             the best one per call. The int8 path then exploits hardware dot-product instructions
             (SDOT/UDOT, AVX-VNNI vpdpbusd) that compute 4 int8 MACs into one int32 lane in a
             single instruction, with int32 accumulators to avoid overflow and a fixed-point
             (scale + zero-point) requantization stage instead of float.
techniques:
  - Runtime ISA detection: getauxval(AT_HWCAP/AT_HWCAP2) bits for FEAT_DotProd, FEAT_I8MM, FP16,
    SVE/SVE2 on aarch64; CPUID leaf 7 for AVX2/AVX-512F/VNNI on x86_64
  - Multi-ISA fat binary: compile all kernel variants, set an 'arch floor' minimum, select optimal
    variant at runtime via a function-pointer table keyed on detected features
  - int8 dot-product micro-kernels: NEON vdotq_s32 / SVE svdot / x86 _mm256_dpbusd_epi32 doing 4
    int8 MACs/lane into int32 accumulators (GEMMLowp), avoiding int16 overflow
  - Asymmetric quantization: per-tensor/per-row scale (f32) + zero_point offset, with the
    (a-zp_a)*(b-zp_b) expansion folded into precomputed offset-correction terms
  - Fixed-point requantization output stage: int32 accumulator * int32 multiplier + rounding shift
    instead of float divide, for deterministic integer-only scoring
  - Kernel naming convention encoding ISA+dtypes+tile (e.g. sve_hybrid_u8u32_dot_6x4VL) plus
    microarch-specific variants (a64fx.cpp) for issue-width/cache tuning
  - MxN register tiling/blocking with weight pre-packing so the inner loop streams contiguously
    and reuses loaded vectors across accumulators
nest steals: Two concrete steals into nest-runtime's simd dispatcher (crates/nest-runtime simd module + search
             hot path). (1) Replace nest's compile-time cfg!(target_arch) dispatch with an
             ACL-style runtime feature probe: a std::sync::OnceLock<Cpu> populated via
             std::arch::is_x86_feature_detected!("avx512vnni"/"avx2") and
             is_aarch64_feature_detected!("dotprod"/"neon"), holding a fn pointer to the chosen
             dot kernel. ONE shipped nest binary then uses AVX-512/VNNI or NEON-dotprod when
             present and falls back to AVX2/NEON/scalar, all behind a typed never-panic
             dispatcher. (2) Add a dedicated int8 cosine kernel for the 'tiny' preset (int8
             embeddings, encoding id already reserved): mirror GEMMLowp by accumulating int8*int8
             into i32 with _mm256_dpbusd_epi32 (AVX-VNNI) / vdotq_s32 (NEON) / scalar i32
             fallback, storing per-row scale (and optional zero_point) alongside the int8 block so
             cosine is reconstructed from the i32 dot + scales. This makes the smallest preset
             (0.283 ratio) also the fastest to score directly off mmap, and integer accumulation
             keeps scoring bit-deterministic across machines (a nest hard requirement) where float
             SIMD reduction order is not. The scale/zero_point metadata is additive in the int8
             codec header, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.
compression: Directly serves compress-more-than-everyone: nest's int8 preset is its most compact embedding
             encoding (0.283 size ratio, recall@10 0.992). ACL's GEMMLowp proves you can score
             that int8 representation natively with dot-product instructions instead of
             dequantizing to f32 first, so the smallest-on-disk format also becomes the
             cheapest-to-search. Asymmetric scale+zero_point quantization packs more fidelity per
             int8 than a symmetric scheme, enabling more aggressive quantization (toward int4)
             without recall loss, pushing the compression frontier while keeping search fast and
             deterministic.
perf:        SDOT/UDOT (FEAT_DotProd) and AVX-VNNI vpdpbusd give ~4x the int8 throughput of a naive
             multiply-then-widen-add loop by fusing 4 MACs/lane into one instruction with free
             int32 accumulation; I8MM/SME push further with outer-product matrix instructions.
             Integer accumulation is reduction-order-independent, so it is reproducible across
             CPUs unlike float horizontal sums (matters for nest determinism). MxN register tiling
             + weight pre-packing keeps the inner GEMM loop bound by dot/FMA issue rate rather
             than loads; for nest's single-query dot products the win is mainly the per-element
             instruction fusion + runtime dispatch, not the heavy tiling. int8 embeddings are 4x
             smaller than f32 in cache, so more vectors stay resident on the mmap hot path.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] linebender/fearless_simd
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust portable-SIMD library that encodes "this CPU feature is available" as a zero-sized
             type-level witness token (implementing the Simd trait), so write-once generic kernels
             monomorphize per instruction-set level (Fallback/Sse4_2/Avx2/Neon/WASM-simd128) and
             call intrinsics with no unsafe at the call site.
innovation:  The witness-token + inline-always dispatch pattern lets one generic SIMD kernel safely compile to
             optimal per-arch intrinsics, eliminating the unsafe NxM kernel matrix.
techniques:
  - Witness/token safety pattern: ZST Level/Simd-trait markers as type-level proof of enabled
    target_feature, making core::arch intrinsics safe to call (relies on target_feature 1.1 + safe
    arch intrinsics, MSRV 1.86/1.88)
  - dispatch!(level, simd => kernel(simd, ...)) macro: one runtime Level::new() detection, then
    monomorphized #[inline(always)] specializations per SIMD level so target_feature propagates
    via inlining
  - Portable fixed-width vector types f32x4/f32x8/f32x16, f64x2/4/8, i8x16/u8x16, i16x8, i32x4
    plus mask8x16/16x8/32x4 with overloaded +,*,FMA lowering to optimal intrinsics
  - Trait stack: SimdBase (load/store/arith), SimdFloat, SimdInt, SimdMask,
    Select(if-mask-then-a-else-b), SimdCombine/SimdSplit, SimdCvtFloat/SimdCvtTruncate for
    int<->float quantization
  - fearless_simd_gen code generator stamps the low-signal repetitive per-arch impls so the
    hand-maintained core stays tiny
  - kernel!() macro + safe_wrappers feature: token-gated escape hatch to arch-specific intrinsics
    beyond the portable set, still safe under the witness
  - WASM strategy: build two bundles (+simd128 vs scalar) and select at load since wasm lacks
    runtime feature detection
nest steals: Target crate nest-runtime, module crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/. nest today hand-rolls a 3x3
             matrix of unsafe #[target_feature] kernels (scalar.rs + avx2.rs + neon.rs, each with
             dot_f32 / dot_f32_f16 / dot_f32_i8) behind an OnceLock+enum dispatcher in mod.rs
             (lines 86-132 each match on detect_backend() then call an `unsafe fn`). Adopt the
             witness-token+dispatch pattern (vendored, not the unstable crate) to replace those ~5
             files with ONE generic dot_f32<S: Simd>, dot_f32_f16<S: Simd>, dot_f32_i8<S: Simd>
             kernel set. This: (1) removes unsafe from the hot path, satisfying nest's
             never-panic/typed-error and clean-core constraints; (2) keeps the single up-front
             detection (matches nest's OnceLock model) so per-query cost stays one branch; (3)
             unlocks f32x16/AVX-512 and WASM simd128 lanes for free, widening portability of the
             i8/f16 quantized scoring loop (score_int8_section, search.rs exact-cosine rerank)
             which is THE compression-critical path; (4) the SimdCvtFloat/SimdCvtTruncate traits
             map directly onto nest's int8 dequant (v ~= i8*scale) and float16 unpack, letting the
             dequant be vectorized portably instead of the current portable-unpack-only f16
             fallback. Determinism is preserved because the math is identical per lane and the
             dispatch is deterministic; the kernels stay well under the 300-line file cap.
compression: Indirect but real. fearless_simd is not a compressor, but nest's compression wins (tiny preset:
             zstd/int8/hnsw at 0.283 size ratio) hinge on scoring quantized int8/f16 embeddings
             directly off mmap on the hot path. The witness pattern lets nest add wider/portable
             SIMD lanes (f32x16, WASM, future AVX-512) and clean vectorized dequant for int8/f16
             WITHOUT growing the unsafe surface, so nest can push to more aggressive quantization
             (int8, and later int4/binary-packed) confident the scoring loop stays fast and
             portable across the sovereign/offline targets. It removes the SIMD-portability tax
             that would otherwise discourage harder compression schemes.
perf:        Per-query cost target identical to nest now: one indirect/branch at dispatch, then fully-inlined
             target_feature code (FMA-fused dot products: _mm256_fmadd_ps on AVX2, vfmaq on NEON).
             The pattern's whole point is that monomorphization + #[inline(always)] yields machine
             code byte-comparable to hand-written intrinsics (it powers Vello's Sparse Strips
             renderer and is philosophically the pulp/faer approach). int8 path widens
             i8->i32->f32 in-lane (matches nest avx2.rs _mm256_cvtepi8_epi32 +
             _mm256_cvtepi32_ps). No heap, ZST tokens carry no runtime cost. Caveat: 128-bit
             minimum vector width; relies on aggressive inlining (a missed #[inline(always)]
             silently de-optimizes).
license:     Dual MIT OR Apache-2.0 (Linebender standard), permissive - safe to vendor/adapt into nest. Caveat:
             crate is explicitly "extremely early experimental, no stability guarantees," so
             vendor the pattern rather than depending on the published crate to protect nest's
             frozen-format and determinism guarantees.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] rust-lang/stdarch
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        stdarch is the implementation home of Rust's `core::arch` / `std::arch` vendor intrinsics plus
             `std_detect` runtime CPU feature detection, organized as one
             `#[target_feature(enable=...)]`-gated intrinsic per machine instruction, with
             detection cached lock-free in an atomic bitset and verified by an `assert_instr`
             macro that disassembles the compiled shim to confirm the expected instruction was
             emitted.
innovation:  The two-layer "safe runtime dispatch" contract: each architecture-specific kernel is an `unsafe
             fn` carrying `#[target_feature(enable = "feat")]` (which lets LLVM emit that ISA's
             instructions for just that function without -C target-feature), and it is reached
             only behind an `is_x86_feature_detected!`/`is_aarch64_feature_detected!` guard whose
             result is computed once and cached in a lock-free atomic bitset, so a portable
             least-common-denominator binary picks the fastest available kernel at runtime with
             zero per-call CPUID cost. The matching trick is per-instruction granularity: one
             intrinsic == one named instruction, so capability can be detected and dispatched at
             the finest possible level (f16c, avxvnni, dotprod separately from avx2).
techniques:
  - #[target_feature(enable="avx2,fma")] per-function ISA enablement (no global -C target-feature
    needed)
  - is_x86_feature_detected!/is_aarch64_feature_detected! macros over std_detect; detection cached
    in a lock-free atomic-usize bitset with an initialized sentinel bit so
    CPUID/getauxval(HWCAP)/sysctl runs exactly once
  - fine-grained gates: f16c (_mm256_cvtph_ps -> vcvtph2ps) for hw f16->f32, avxvnni/avx512vnni
    (_mm256_dpbusd_epi32 -> vpdpbusd) for single-instruction int8 dot, aarch64 dotprod (vdotq_s32)
    for NEON int8 dot
  - _mm256_fmadd_ps / vfmaq_f32 fused multiply-add accumulators (the core of a dot product)
  - #[assert_instr(vfmadd)] proc-macro emits a #[test] shim, compiles it, and greps the
    disassembly to prove the intended instruction was generated
  - static vs dynamic dispatch duality: same kernel reachable via #[cfg(target_feature)] or
    runtime guard
  - repr(simd) Simd<T,N> newtype over [T;N] with simd_fma/simd_cast LLVM intrinsics as the
    portable lowering substrate
nest steals: nest-runtime/src/simd already copies the stdarch idiom (OnceLock-cached is_x86_feature_detected
             dispatch + #[target_feature(enable=\"avx2,fma\")] kernels calling
             _mm256_fmadd_ps/vfmaq_f32), so the steal is three fine-grained-feature upgrades the
             code itself flags as gaps. (1) In mod.rs the f16 path dot_f32_f16_bytes (lines
             107-111) explicitly bails to scalar to avoid the F16C detection branch -- add an
             is_x86_feature_detected!(\"f16c\") tier in avx2.rs using _mm256_cvtph_ps (vcvtph2ps)
             to convert 8 f16 lanes to f32 in one instruction, giving the compressed/tiny presets
             a real AVX2 f16 dot path. (2) The int8 path dot_f32_i8_avx2 (lines 36-60) widens
             i8->i32->f32 then FMAs -- gate an avxvnni/avx512vnni tier using _mm256_dpbusd_epi32
             (vpdpbusd) for int8 accumulate-to-i32 in one instruction, multiplying by the
             per-vector scale once at the end, accelerating the tiny preset (zstd/int8/hnsw, 0.283
             ratio) hot path. (3) In neon.rs add an is_aarch64_feature_detected!(\"dotprod\") tier
             using vdotq_s32 for the same int8 win on Apple silicon. Adopt stdarch's verification
             discipline in simd/tests.rs by vendoring the assert_instr disassembly check so a
             refactor that silently drops vfmadd/vpdpbusd is caught. Add
             Avx2F16c/Avx2Vnni/NeonDotprod variants to the SimdBackend enum so nest stats reports
             the active tier. All additive, offline, deterministic (exact rerank stays ground
             truth); no format change.
compression: Indirect but real: extreme compression in nest comes from the int8 and float16 codecs (tiny =
             0.283 ratio), but those quantized formats are only viable on the hot path if they
             decode/score fast off mmap. stdarch's f16c (vcvtph2ps) and VNNI/dotprod
             (vpdpbusd/vdotq_s32) single-instruction kernels remove the widen-and-convert overhead
             that currently makes the compressed dtypes slower per query than f32, so the
             most-compressed presets stop paying a latency tax. This lets nest push quantization
             harder (e.g. default tiny) without losing the serve-from-disk speed promise,
             advancing compress-more-than-everyone while keeping fast search.
perf:        target_feature enablement is per-function, so kernels emit AVX2/FMA/F16C/VNNI/NEON-dotprod
             instructions while the rest of the binary stays portable (no -C target-feature, no
             per-CPU recompilation). Detection is one atomic bitset read after first call; nest
             already amortizes via OnceLock + function-pointer dispatch (one indirect call per
             query). _mm256_cvtph_ps converts 8 f16->f32 in one vcvtph2ps; _mm256_dpbusd_epi32
             does 32 int8 MACs into 8 i32 lanes per vpdpbusd (~4x the current
             cvtepi8_epi32->cvtepi32_ps->fmadd chain); vdotq_s32 does 16 int8 MACs into 4 i32
             lanes per instruction on aarch64 dotprod. assert_instr adds zero runtime cost
             (test-only shim).
license:     MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual, same as the Rust project)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] BurntSushi/memchr
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust crate providing CPU-feature-dispatched SIMD byte-search (memchr/2/3) and substring search
             (memmem) built on a write-once `Vector` trait abstraction, a frequency-ranked "packed
             pair" rare-byte prefilter, the Two-Way string-matching algorithm as a linear-time
             fallback, and a SWAR (SIMD-within-a-register) scalar path.
innovation:  The "packed pair" rare-byte prefilter combined with a write-once generic Vector trait: choose the
             two statistically-rarest needle bytes from a 256-entry background frequency rank
             table, broadcast and SIMD-scan both positions, AND the movemasks to get
             near-zero-false-positive candidates, verify with Two-Way, and dynamically
             self-disable the prefilter when it stops being selective -- all written once over a
             tiny Vector trait with thin AVX2/NEON/SSE2/wasm/SWAR shims.
techniques:
  - Packed-pair prefilter with two rarest needle bytes + AND of movemasks
  - 256-entry HeuristicFrequencyRank background byte-frequency table
  - PrefilterState dynamic false-positive-driven disabling
  - Two-Way O(m+n)/O(1) verifier backbone
  - Rabin-Karp small-haystack fast path
  - Write-once generic Vector trait with per-arch shims
  - SWAR has_zero_byte bit-twiddle + splat broadcast
  - Runtime AVX2/SSE2 feature dispatch with reusable amortized Finder
nest steals: Two concrete wins, both in nest-runtime. (1) bm25_index (0x08) and search-text: the bm25
             tokenizer/term scan and any substring/term lookup over chunks_canonical can use a
             memchr-style packed-pair prefilter to scan mmapped text without allocation; this
             directly attacks the known CJK/Thai degradation by letting a future codepoint-aware
             scanner stay vectorized. Practically, depend on the `memchr` crate (zero-dep,
             no_std-capable) in nest-runtime rather than hand-rolling -- it respects the
             never-open-a-socket / offline constraint and is deterministic. (2) The bigger steal
             is the architectural pattern for nest's simd dispatcher in nest-runtime: memchr's
             write-once `Vector` trait + per-target shim (AVX2/NEON/scalar) is exactly the shape
             nest's dot-product dispatcher already wants; adopt it so the f32/f16/int8 cosine
             kernels are one generic body with trivial AVX2/NEON/SWAR shims instead of three
             parallel copies, keeping files under the 300-line cap and removing duplication. The
             SWAR has_zero_byte/splat trick is a ready-made deterministic scalar fallback pattern
             for any byte-level scan added to nest-format (e.g. validating/section-scanning during
             reader.rs parsing) that must never panic and must run identically on every arch.
compression: Indirect but real. memchr is not a compressor, so it does not shrink the .nest file. Its relevance
             to the compress-more-than-everyone goal is on the SERVE side: faster, allocation-free
             scanning over compressed/mmapped text sections means nest can afford to keep more
             sections zstd-compressed and still hit latency targets, because term/substring lookup
             over decoded text stays in vectorized loops. The packed-pair + frequency-rank idea is
             also directly transferable to building selective prefilters over int8-quantized
             embedding scans (pick the most discriminative dimensions first), which helps keep the
             tiny preset (int8/hnsw) fast at high compression ratio.
perf:        Throughput is dominated by staying in the vector loop: AVX2 processes 32 bytes/iter,
             SSE2/NEON/wasm 16 bytes/iter, scalar SWAR 8 bytes (usize) per iter. The rare-byte
             pair keeps false positives near zero so verification is rarely triggered; the
             PrefilterState self-disable prevents pathological O(n*m)-ish blowups on adversarial
             inputs. Zero heap allocation in the hot path, no_std-capable (SSE2/NEON without std,
             AVX2 gated behind std feature detection). Reusable Finder amortizes searcher
             construction across many calls -- a measurable win over libc memmem and std which
             reconstruct per call.
license:     Dual-licensed Unlicense OR MIT (public-domain-equivalent); safe to vendor or depend on with no
             copyleft obligations.

[HIGH] ashvardanian/StringZilla
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A portable C/C++ string library exposing CPU-feature-dispatched SIMD microkernels (NEON, AVX2,
             AVX-512, SVE, SWAR fallback, CUDA) for substring search, AES-NI hashing,
             Rabin-rolling-hash MinHash sketches, Levenshtein/Needleman-Wunsch alignment, argsort,
             and lookup-table byte transforms.
innovation:  Runtime CPU-capability dispatch over a family of hand-written SIMD microkernels with named
             per-uarch variants (sz_find_skylake / sz_find_haswell / sz_find_westmere /
             sz_find_neon) selected once at load, each kernel built around the principle "you
             already fetch every cache line, so the only win is reducing comparisons per cache
             line": pick rare/anomalous needle bytes at strategic offsets, broadcast-compare them
             with one SIMD op, and only confirm full matches on the survivors.
techniques:
  - AES-NI based non-cryptographic sz_hash: feed bytes through hardware AESENC rounds for a fast,
    well-mixed 64-bit hash (plus streaming sz_hash_state_t and sz_hash_sha256 for crypto
    checksums)
  - Rabin-style 64-bit rolling hash reused for both whole-string and substring (fingerprint)
    hashing, giving O(D*(T-N)) MinHash cost independent of window width N
  - MinHash / Fingerprints sketches: D rolling-hash functions over multiple window widths (e.g.
    4,6,8,10), keeping per-dimension minima as a compact D-dim document signature; 52-bit hash
    variant in v4
  - Substring search via rare-byte + multi-offset SIMD comparison (compare characters at
    strategically chosen needle offsets to cut false positives), Boyer-Moore-Horspool with Raita
    heuristic for long needles, bit-parallel for mid needles, brute SIMD for 1-4 byte needles
  - Offset-based 'tape' layout (Strs): one contiguous byte tape plus a u32 offsets array, ~4 bytes
    overhead per string for sub-4GB datasets, zero-copy mmap and PyArrow-serializable, lazy
    split_iter avoids materialization
  - vpshufb / NEON vtbl lookup-table transform (sz_lookup) mapping bytes through a 256-entry table
    at ~21 GB/s, 80x std::transform, usable as int8 codec / case-fold / dna decode
  - Radix/parallel sz_sequence_argsort returning a permutation over a string collection (beats
    std::sort and 4x numpy.argsort) without moving the underlying bytes
  - Two-tier retrieve-then-rerank architecture: cheap MinHash fingerprints for candidate
    retrieval, expensive exact Levenshtein/NW scoring only on reranked survivors
nest steals: Four concrete steals. (1) nest-runtime simd dispatcher: adopt StringZilla's named-per-uarch
             runtime dispatch pattern (currently nest has AVX2/NEON/scalar) so the dot-product
             kernel picks AVX-512/AVX2/SSE/NEON/scalar once at mmap time behind one fn pointer,
             keeping the never-panic typed-error boundary; optionally vendor StringZilla
             (Apache-2.0) for sz_hash. (2) nest-format hashing: replace or augment SHA-256
             surfaces' hot-path data-integrity needs with AES-NI sz_bytesum/sz_hash for a fast
             non-crypto pre-check while keeping SHA-256 as the canonical content_hash (additive,
             no format bump). (3) nest-format new optional section (id 0x09+, RESERVED today): a
             deterministic MinHash 'sketch index' built from Rabin rolling fingerprints over
             chunks_canonical, giving an ultra-compact pre-filter that shrinks the candidate set
             before the mandatory exact-cosine rerank, mirroring nest's existing HNSW+rerank
             contract and StringZilla's retrieve-then-rerank design. (4) nest-format chunks layout
             + int8 codec: the offset-tape (u32 offsets + byte tape) is exactly nest's
             chunks_canonical/chunks_original_spans shape, and vpshufb/vtbl sz_lookup is a ready
             SIMD kernel for the int8 encoding decode path and for a SIMD bm25 tokenizer that
             fixes the CJK/Thai word-segmentation gap via byte-level scanning.
compression: Indirect but real. StringZilla does not add a new entropy coder, so it does not directly beat zstd
             on ratio. Its compression-mission value is (a) MinHash sketches as a tiny
             deduplication/near-duplicate detector to drop redundant chunks before encoding,
             raising effective ratio; (b) the offset-tape layout minimizes per-string overhead (4
             bytes) in the text sections; (c) vpshufb LUT transform accelerates int8 quantization
             decode so the tiny preset stays fast off mmap. The decisive contribution is making
             the serve path cheaper (AES hashing, dispatched SIMD, fingerprint pre-filter), which
             lets nest keep aggressive compression without hurting search latency.
perf:        sz_find ~10.6 GB/s x86 / 7.1 GB/s ARM (vs libc strstr 7.4 / 2.0); sz_lookup ~21 GB/s (80x
             std::transform); AES sz_hash ~3x OpenSSL hashlib on mmap'd files; argsort 1.91s vs
             std::sort 2.79s and numpy 7.58s on 8M words; Levenshtein 3.4B CUPS x86 / 93.6B CUPS
             CUDA. Kernels are scalar-fallback-safe (SWAR on 64-bit words), header-only C with
             stable ABI, zero-alloc in-place ops, and the per-uarch backends are disablable via
             SZ_USE_X86_AVX512 / SZ_USE_X86_AVX2 / SZ_USE_ARM_NEON compile flags.
license:     Apache-2.0

[MEDIUM] AdamNiederer/faster
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust "SIMD for humans" library that exposes vectorized computation as iterator combinators
             (simd_iter / simd_map / simd_reduce / scalar_collect) over width-agnostic vector
             types (f32s, i8s, etc.), compiling each closure body down to 100% native SIMD via
             thin intrinsic wrappers plus scalar polyfills for missing instructions.
innovation:  see field
techniques:
  - Iterator combinators over SIMD: simd_iter/simd_map/simd_reduce/scalar_collect mirroring std
    iterators, so vectorized math reads like scalar math
  - Width-agnostic vector types (f32s, i8s, u8s): a single source kernel auto-selects f32x8 (AVX2)
    / f32x4 (NEON) / scalar, sized to the largest register the target supports
  - Default-value tail padding: uneven collections are finished by padding the last partial vector
    with a user-supplied identity value (e.g. 0.0) so the full-width SIMD kernel runs end-to-end,
    eliminating the scalar remainder loop (benchmarks: uneven within ~1% of even)
  - Thin-wrapper + polyfill portability: each op is a 1-line wrapper around an intrinsic, with a
    scalar polyfill that only activates on targets lacking the instruction, so the same trait
    method compiles everywhere
  - Accumulator-vector reduction: simd_reduce keeps a full-width SIMD accumulator across the loop
    and does a single horizontal fold at the very end, not per-element
  - upcast/downcast lane-width conversions (e.g. i8 lanes widened to i32/f32 lanes) exposed as
    vector ops, matching mixed-precision dot products
  - Static dispatch with #[inline] + #[target_feature] so the hot loop has zero per-iteration
    CPUID/branch cost
nest steals: Apply to nest-runtime/src/simd/. (1) Steal the default-value tail-padding trick to kill the scalar
             tail loops in avx2.rs/neon.rs dot_f32_*/dot_f32_i8_* (currently lines 18-28 and 52-55
             are hand-written remainder loops): pad the embedding row's final partial vector with
             zeros (a safe FMA identity) so one masked/padded full-width FMA finishes the dim,
             shrinking each kernel and removing a per-query branch - directly helps since nest
             scores EVERY row off mmap on the hot path. (2) Adopt faster's thin-wrapper+polyfill
             discipline as the template for adding NEW quantized dtypes (int4/nf4/binary) to the
             search hot path without exploding the per-arch file count: define one dot_f32_qN
             trait with an avx2/neon wrapper plus a scalar polyfill, keeping files <=300 lines and
             the OnceLock dispatcher unchanged. (3) The upcast/downcast lane-widening pattern is
             exactly nest's i8->i32->f32 widening (avx2.rs lines 45-48) and is the blueprint for
             int4/binary kernels that unpack sub-byte lanes - the enabler for pushing the 'tiny'
             preset's 0.283 size ratio lower with binary/int4 embeddings while keeping a
             real-cosine rerank. Do NOT adopt faster's iterator API wholesale: it is
             compile-time-width-only and nest's OnceLock runtime dispatch is strictly better for
             shipping one portable binary.
compression: Indirect but real: faster's lane-widening (upcast/downcast) and polyfill discipline are the
             practical pattern for adding sub-byte quantized dot-product kernels (int4, nf4,
             binary/Hamming) to nest's hot path. Those new dtypes are what actually push the
             'tiny' preset below its current 0.283 size ratio, since embeddings (the bulk of
             bytes, never zstd-compressed because they are scored from mmap) can only shrink via
             lower-precision encodings that still vectorize. faster shows how to make each new
             precision a thin wrapper + scalar fallback rather than a new per-arch rewrite.
perf:        Hot-loop body compiles to 100% SIMD instructions ordered by LLVM; overhead vs hand-written
             intrinsics is within ~5% worst case, ~1% on the README example. Tail-padding makes
             uneven collections essentially free (reduce_uneven_simd 838ns vs reduce_simd 839ns;
             map_uneven 884ns vs map 879ns). Critical caveat for nest: dispatch is COMPILE-TIME
             only (no runtime feature selection - confirmed by the simdeez comparison), so a
             faster-built binary is pinned to the build host's widest ISA. nest's OnceLock
             function-pointer runtime dispatch already beats this for a single portable offline
             binary, so borrow the kernel/tail tricks, not the dispatch model. Performance can
             degrade only when a slow scalar polyfill is hit on an old CPU.
license:     MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual, standard Rust dual-license; permissive, safe to learn from or vendor
             patterns)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] github.com/google/highway
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A C++ portable-SIMD library exposing a single width-agnostic ops API
             (Add/Mul/MulAdd/Load/Compress/Gather/Mask) that compiles each translation unit once
             per instruction set (SSE4/AVX2/AVX-512/NEON/SVE/RVV/WASM) and selects the best kernel
             at runtime via a function-pointer table, plus contrib kernels for vectorized
             quicksort (vqsort), math, and reductions.
innovation:  The "compile every kernel once per SIMD target, then pick the best at runtime through a cached
             function-pointer table" pattern (foreach_target.h + HWY_DYNAMIC_DISPATCH +
             HWY_EXPORT): one width-agnostic source body, N object-code variants, one CPU-feature
             detection paid once, then an indirect call per hot-path entry instead of a
             CPUID/branch per element. This is exactly the dispatch discipline nest's hot-path
             dot-product loop needs to scale beyond its current hand-written avx2/neon/scalar trio
             without bloating the small rust core.
techniques:
  - foreach_target.h: include a -inl.h kernel body once per target in HWY_TARGETS via controlled
    re-inclusion + per-target namespace (hwy::HWY_NAMESPACE)
  - HWY_DYNAMIC_DISPATCH + HWY_EXPORT: build a per-function pointer table, resolve once with
    GetChosenTarget().Update(SupportedTargets()), then indirect-call (mirrors nest's OnceLock
    function-pointer cache, but table-per-function not enum-per-call)
  - width-agnostic tag types (ScalableTag/CappedTag/FixedTag) so one loop body targets
    128/256/512-bit and scalable SVE/RVV with automatic remainder handling
  - Compress/CompressStore: stream-compaction primitive (store only mask=yes lanes consecutively)
    - the engine of vqsort's branchless 3-region partition
  - AVX2 compress emulation via table-driven permute (precomputed shuffle-index LUT indexed by an
    8-bit mask) when no native VPCOMPRESS exists
  - vqsort: branchless SIMD partition + recursive sorting-network base case (<=256 elts) +
    384-byte robust pivot sampling with heapsort fallback for O(n log n) worst case
  - MaskedLoad/BlendedStore/FirstN for tail/remainder handling without scalar fallthrough
  - SoA + aligned-allocation discipline as the precondition for SIMD payoff (>=100KiB arrays
    before wide vectors pay off)
nest steals: Two concrete steals into nest-runtime/src/simd/. (1) Dispatch refactor: mod.rs currently does
             `match detect_backend()` (an enum compare) inside every dot_* wrapper, and each new
             dtype x target adds a match arm. Adopt Highway's per-function pointer-table idea:
             store `fn(&[f32],&[u8])->f32` resolved once in a OnceLock, so adding AVX-512 or SVE
             later is a new table entry, not a new hot-path branch - keeps files under the
             300-line cap while widening target coverage. Also add an F16C-detected avx2 f16 path
             (today dot_f32_f16_bytes falls back to scalar on x86_64, a perf gap flagged in the
             file's own comment). (2) Compress-store primitive: port Highway's table-driven
             Compress (AVX2 permute-LUT / NEON / scalar) into a new simd/compress.rs. This unlocks
             the highest-leverage item on nest's roadmap: a deterministic SIMD
             candidate-compaction filter in ann/search.rs (compact surviving HNSW candidates
             branchlessly before the mandatory exact-cosine rerank), and it is the exact primitive
             needed if nest adds a vectorized RLE/sparse repack codec for non-embedding sections
             in nest-format/encoding. Keep it offline/deterministic: pure compute, fixed LUTs =>
             byte-identical results, fits the additive-format and never-panic constraints.
compression: Indirect but real. Highway is not a compressor; its payoff for nest's compress-more-than-everyone
             north star is the Compress/CompressStore stream-compaction primitive, the SIMD engine
             behind branchless partition, RLE/sparse repack, and mask-driven candidate pruning.
             Direct relevance: int8 quantization (tiny preset, 0.283 ratio) dot products and ANN
             rerank throughput; a faster, wider, table-dispatched kernel lets nest afford finer
             quantization (per-block int4/int8 with SIMD dequant) at serve time without blowing
             the mandatory exact-cosine rerank budget.
perf:        vqsort beats std::sort by 10-20x on AVX2/AVX-512 and ips4o by geomean 2.89x single-core / 1.59x
             parallel; robust pivot sampling adds <2% CPU. vqsort uses ~1.2KiB stack + a 3-word
             TLS RNG cache, no heap. Guidance: wide-vector overhead only pays above ~100KiB
             arrays, and SoA + aligned loads are prerequisites - relevant because nest embedding
             rows are mmap'd contiguous f32/f16/i8 (already SoA) and dims are typically 384-1536,
             well above SIMD break-even. Highway f16 paths assume F16C/NEON-fp16; nest's avx2 f16
             currently degrades to scalar.
license:     Dual-licensed Apache-2.0 / BSD-3-Clause (originally Apache-2.0 only). C++ library, so nest
             reimplements ideas in rust rather than vendoring; permissive for clean-room
             reimplementation either way.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] intel/x86-simd-sort (now numpy/x86-simd-sort)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A C++ header library of SIMD (AVX-512/AVX2) sorting primitives - quicksort, quickselect,
             partialsort, argsort/argselect, and key-value sort for 16/32/64-bit ints and floats -
             built as a hybrid of vectorized quicksort partitioning plus branch-free bitonic
             sorting networks for small partitions.
innovation:  Branch-free vectorized partitioning via AVX-512 compressstore (the single most transferable idea).
techniques:
  - AVX-512 compressstore partitioning: cmp-vs-pivot -> mask -> _mm512_mask_compressstoreu writes
    selected lanes densely, branch-free
  - Branch-free bitonic sorting networks for small arrays (<= ~512 elems) built purely from
    min/max COEX (compare-exchange) over SIMD registers, sorting N vectors simultaneously
  - Hybrid quicksort: recurse with vectorized partition until partition size drops below a
    power-of-2 threshold (512/256/128/64), then switch to the in-register network
  - Key-value / argsort: a parallel index (or value) vector is permuted in lockstep with the keys
    using the same shuffle/permute masks, so the sort carries a payload
  - Runtime CPU-feature dispatch: pick AVX-512F+DQ / AVX2 / scalar kernel at load time
  - NaN canonicalization: NaNs hoisted to array end and rewritten as a single quiet-NaN bit
    pattern so the comparator stays total/branch-free
  - Per-march tuning (skylake-avx512 for 32/64-bit, icelake for 16-bit) showing the network must
    be specialized per lane-width
nest steals: nest-runtime/src/simd (the dispatcher that already does AVX2/NEON/scalar dot products) is the
             natural home. Two concrete steals: (1) Branch-free top-k / threshold filtering on the
             search hot path. After exact-cosine rerank, nest must select top-k and filter
             candidates above a score threshold; today that is a scalar scan with branches. Adapt
             the compressstore trick - compute scores in a SIMD register, mask = (score >=
             threshold), then compressstore the surviving candidate indices densely (vpcompressd
             on AVX2 via _mm256_maskz_compress on AVX-512, or a NEON shuffle-table fallback). This
             collapses the candidate-pruning loop to branch-free SIMD and pairs directly with the
             existing dot-product kernel. (2) The key-value/argsort pattern maps onto
             materialize/search returning (score, chunk_id) pairs: permute the id payload in
             lockstep with the score vector so the final sorted top-k comes out without a separate
             gather. Keep all of this in the existing simd dispatcher behind the same
             AVX2/NEON/scalar feature-gates, never panic, scalar fallback mandatory. This does NOT
             touch the FROZEN nest-format container (no new section/encoding id), so it respects
             additive-only constraints - it is pure runtime acceleration.
compression: Low-to-indirect. This library is about sort/select throughput, not bytes-on-disk, so it does not
             advance the 'compress more than everyone' north star directly. Indirect leverage: (a)
             a fast branch-free argsort enables cheap reordering of chunks/embeddings by a
             similarity or cluster key at build time, and clustered/sorted embeddings compress
             better under delta+int8 quantization (locality improves residual entropy) - a real
             but second-order win for the tiny/compressed presets; (b) faster top-k keeps the
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank cheap even as corpora grow. It buys serving speed and a
             modest compression-locality assist, not a new codec.
perf:        Reported 4-10x over std::sort for object/key-value sort on AVX-512; the upstream research (Bramas)
             reports ~8x on small arrays (<16 SIMD vectors), ~4x vs libstdc++ and ~1.4x vs Intel
             IPP on large arrays. Performance comes entirely from eliminating branch
             mispredictions (compressstore) and from branch-free min/max networks that saturate
             SIMD ports; OpenMP variant adds up to ~3x with <=16 threads. Memory: object/argsort
             needs arrsize*sizeof(key) + arrsize*4 bytes aux (O(N) scratch). Critical caveat for
             nest: compressstore (vpcompressd) is an AVX-512 instruction; on AVX2 it must be
             emulated with a 256-entry precomputed permute LUT, and on aarch64/NEON there is no
             compress so it needs a shuffle-table fallback - the scalar fallback path is mandatory
             under nest constraints.
license:     BSD-3-Clause

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] simdutf/simdutf
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A dual-licensed (MIT/Apache-2.0) C++ library for SIMD-accelerated Unicode validation, transcoding
             (UTF-8/16/32/Latin1), and WHATWG base64 codec that hits billions of chars/sec via
             per-arch kernels (SSE2/AVX2/AVX-512-VBMI2/NEON/SVE/RVV/POWER) selected by runtime CPU
             dispatch.
innovation:  The "lookup" validation pattern: replace memory-bound finite-state machines with tiny
             SIMD-register-resident nibble-indexed lookup tables consulted via the byte-shuffle
             instruction (pshufb/vpshufb/tbl), aggregating all error conditions into a single
             OR-accumulated "error register" so the hot loop is branchless and runs at <1
             instruction per byte. The same nibble-LUT-via-shuffle idea generalizes to any
             byte-classification or range-mapping task (UTF-8 validation, base64 alphabet decode,
             charset filtering).
techniques:
  - UTF-8 lookup algorithm: 3 nibble-indexed pshufb LUTs (byte_1_high, byte_1_low, byte_2_high);
    bitwise-AND non-zero iff invalid; OR-accumulated error register, <1 instr/byte
  - Sliding-window vectorization with prev1/prev2/prev3 lane-shifts to validate multi-byte
    sequences across register boundaries
  - Muła/Lemire base64 nibble-LUT range+shift via pshufb then multiply-add pack
    (vpmaddubsw/vpmulhuw+vpmullw) to repack 6-bit groups; 14 vec instrs / 32 bytes
  - AVX-512 VBMI single-pass base64 decode via vpermb 256-entry permute
  - Runtime per-arch implementation table chosen once via function pointer; AVX-512 gated on VBMI2
    to avoid pre-Ice-Lake throttling
  - ASCII fast-path detecting zero high bits to skip full machinery (~66 GiB/s, 20x ICU)
  - Non-allocating, exception-free pointer+length API compiling to a few hundred KB
nest steals: Two concrete steals. (1) nest-format must validate/normalize text for content_hash stability
             (chunks_canonical, section 0x...text) and for the word-segmented bm25 tokenizer;
             vendor simdutf's lookup UTF-8 validation pattern as a small pure-Rust module under
             nest-format/src/sections/ (or reuse the rusticstuff/simdutf8 crate which already
             ports the exact algorithm and is MIT/Apache) to guarantee canonical sections are
             valid UTF-8 at <1 instr/byte before hashing, killing a class of non-deterministic
             decode bugs. (2) nest-runtime/src/simd/ already mirrors simdutf's dispatch design
             (OnceLock + function-pointer, avx2.rs/neon.rs/scalar.rs); copy simdutf's per-arch
             table discipline (add AVX-512-VBMI2 gating on VBMI2, an ASCII/zero-high-bit fast
             path, and the same fallback ordering) so the int8/f16 dot kernels and any future
             base64 codec follow one proven dispatch contract. (3) If nest ever base64-wraps
             binary blobs (pdf/audio/video sections, citation payloads), adopt the Muła/Lemire
             nibble-LUT+multiply-add-pack base64 kernel rather than a scalar encoder, keeping the
             compress-and-serve hot path fast and offline.
compression: Indirect but real. simdutf does not compress; it is a throughput-and-correctness library. Its
             direct value to nest's "compress more than everyone" north star is the SIMD dispatch
             discipline (cleanly reusable for the scoring hot path and any future entropy/codec
             kernels) and the nibble-LUT-via-shuffle pattern, which is the generic primitive
             behind fast byte-level codecs (base64, alphabet remap, and by extension
             dictionary/RLE byte classification). The base64 kernel matters only when nest
             base64-wraps binary modalities; embeddings stay raw for mmap scoring so no
             compression kernel here touches the hot path.
perf:        ~66 GiB/s on ASCII UTF-8 validation; transcoding >1 billion chars/sec on mixed scripts; 20x ICU on
             ASCII, 3-10x on non-ASCII. Base64 decode ~7x and encode ~10x vs scalar (AVX2), 14
             vector instrs per 32-byte decode. Scales naturally across w=16 (NEON/SSE2), w=32
             (AVX2), w=64 (AVX-512) register widths with the same algorithm. Whole library
             compiles to a few hundred KB, non-allocating, exception-free; AVX-512 path gated on
             VBMI2 to avoid clock-throttling on pre-Ice-Lake silicon.
license:     Dual MIT / Apache-2.0 (vendorable into nest; or use the MIT/Apache rusticstuff/simdutf8 Rust port
             of the same lookup algorithm)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[LOW] Maratyszcza/FXdiv
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A ~1-file MIT C99/C++ header that replaces integer division by a runtime-invariant divisor with
             one widening multiply-high plus two shifts, using the Granlund-Montgomery (1994)
             "division by invariant integers via multiplication" magic-number scheme, with
             portable mulhi backends per platform.
innovation:  Precompute a per-divisor "magic" reciprocal struct {value, m, s1, s2} once, then turn every later
             n/d and n%d into branch-free q = (mulhi(n,m) + ((n - mulhi(n,m)) >> s1)) >> s2 and r
             = n - q*value. This converts a 20-40 cycle hardware UDIV (which most SIMD ISAs lack
             for integers) into a 3-5 cycle mul+shift sequence that IS vectorizable, amortized
             across millions of dividends sharing the same divisor.
techniques:
  - Granlund-Montgomery magic-number reciprocal: m = floor(2^(N+l)/d)+1 with l = ceil(log2(d)),
    giving exact quotient for all N-bit n
  - Round-up correction split into two shifts s1=1 and s2=l-1 to avoid an (N+1)-bit intermediate
    without a wider integer type
  - mulhi (high half of NxN product) abstracted over backends: __umulh/_umul128 (MSVC), unsigned
    __int128 shift (gcc/clang), __umulhi/__umul64hi (CUDA), mul_hi (OpenCL), and a 32x32->64
    split-and-recombine portable fallback
  - Highest-set-bit via __builtin_clz / _BitScanReverse with a bit-twiddle fallback to compute l
    in O(1)
  - Trivial divisor==1 special-case (m=1, s1=s2=0)
  - Branch-free fxdiv_round_down(n,g) = (n/g.value)*g.value for size/offset alignment
  - POD divisor struct hoisted as loop-invariant and broadcast across SIMD lanes (used in
    NNPACK/QNNPACK to vectorize tiling index math)
nest steals: Adopt as a tiny internal Rust module in nest-runtime, NOT a change to the current dot-product
             kernels. Today the hot loop in crates/nest-runtime/src/search.rs and ann/build.rs
             addresses embedding rows with i*row_size / i*dim (pure multiply, no divide), so FXdiv
             buys nothing there. Its payoff is forward-looking: (a) a future tensor/Parquet/video
             section that maps a flat mmap offset back to (row,col,channel) and would need /dim
             and %dim per element; (b) product-quantization codecs (relevant to the tiny preset
             and extreme compression) where decoding sub-vector indices needs divide/modulo by
             codebook count; (c) deterministic seeded RNG / hash bucketing needing fast unbiased
             range reduction. In each, precompute a usize/u32 magic divisor once per build and
             reuse branch-free mul+shift, keeping kernels SIMD-friendly (AVX2/NEON have no integer
             UDIV) and integer-exact/identical across x86_64/aarch64/scalar, satisfying
             byte-reproducibility and never-panic. Port is ~60 lines of safe Rust using u128
             widening mul ((n as u128 * m as u128) >> 64), well under the 300-line cap; MIT lets
             nest reimplement rather than vendor.
compression: Indirect. It does not compress bytes itself; it is an enabling primitive for compression schemes
             that add integer division/modulo on the decode hot path -- specifically product
             quantization / residual codebooks (sub-vector = idx % K, codebook = idx / K) and
             packed multi-dim tensor layouts. If nest ships PQ for sub-int8 embedding compression
             (a real lever for beating the 0.283 tiny ratio), FXdiv-style magic division keeps the
             dequant loop branch-free and vectorizable. Until PQ/tensor sections exist, relevance
             to compress-more-than-everyone is latent, not active.
perf:        Replaces a ~20-40 cycle non-pipelined integer UDIV with mulhi (3-5 cycle latency, fully pipelined)
             plus two 1-cycle shifts; the divisor struct is loop-invariant and stays in registers.
             x86 AVX2 and ARM NEON have NO integer divide instruction, so this is the only way to
             vectorize per-lane integer divide/modulo. Header-only, zero allocations, divisor
             struct is 8-24 bytes POD. Used in production inside NNPACK / QNNPACK / XNNPACK for
             convolution tiling index arithmetic.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[LOW] nemequ/simde (now github.com/simd-everywhere/simde)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        Header-only C99 library that re-implements every native SIMD intrinsic
             (SSE/AVX2/AVX-512/NEON/SVE/WASM128, thousands of functions) as a per-function
             compile-time cascade -- native passthrough, then cross-ISA emulation, then compiler
             vector builtins, then portable scalar fallback -- exposing the FULL native API
             instead of a lowest-common-denominator abstraction.
innovation:  The full-API per-function fallback cascade: each operation picks the fastest available
             implementation at compile time (native intrinsic, then another ISA's intrinsics, then
             __builtin_shufflevector/__builtin_convertvector, then plain C99), so one source
             compiles everywhere with zero overhead where native support exists, and the portable
             C99 fallback is written shuffle/convert-aware so the compiler auto-vectorizes it
             (often beating naive scalar even on the wrong arch). The scalar path is a
             first-class, correctness-checked sibling of the SIMD paths, not an afterthought.
techniques:
  - Per-function implementation cascade (native -> cross-ISA -> compiler vector builtin -> scalar
    C99) selected at compile time via ifdefs
  - Cross-ISA emulation: implement SSE/AVX semantics with NEON ops and vice versa so one kernel
    runs on x86 and aarch64
  - Compiler vector extensions (__builtin_shufflevector, __builtin_convertvector, fixed-width
    vector typedefs) as a portable layer below intrinsics, above scalar
  - Auto-vectorization-friendly scalar fallbacks plus -fopenmp-simd / SIMDE_VECTORIZE pragmas to
    hint loop vectorization without rewriting kernels
  - Fixed-width types (int8_t/uint16_t) instead of ambiguous char/int for bit-identical results
    across architectures (determinism)
  - -DSIMDE_NO_NATIVE switch that forces the portable path purely for differential testing of
    fallback vs native equivalence
  - Reusable conversion primitives: f16<->f32 (F16C/fcvtl) and i8->i32 lane widening helpers
    shared across kernels
nest steals: Target crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/ (mod.rs + avx2.rs + neon.rs + scalar.rs). nest hand-writes 3
             dtype kernels (f32, f16, i8) x up to 3 ISAs as near-duplicated functions plus a
             OnceLock function-pointer dispatcher. Steal SIMDe's METHOD (not its C): (1) Make
             scalar a verified equivalence-sibling -- add to simd/tests.rs a differential test
             (the SIMDE_NO_NATIVE idea) asserting avx2/neon kernels equal the scalar kernel within
             epsilon on random vectors, gated on the existing NEST_FORCE_SCALAR env; this hardens
             the exact-cosine-is-ground-truth contract and the deterministic-build guarantee. (2)
             Factor the f16->f32 and i8->i32 widening steps (currently inlined in avx2.rs and
             neon.rs) into small shared inlined helpers so adding a new low-bit embedding dtype
             (int4/bf16 for a smaller tiny-class preset) becomes one widen-helper + one scalar
             reference, not a full new ISA matrix. Do NOT vendor SIMDe (C vs nest's repr(C) Rust +
             std::arch); borrow the cascade discipline only. This unlocks safe addition of
             lower-bit embedding quantization on the mmap hot path.
compression: Indirect but real. SIMDe is a portability layer, not a compressor, so it does not shrink the .nest
             file. Its value to the compress-more-than-everyone goal is enabling lower-bit
             embedding quantization: the hot-path dot product must stay fast AND bit-exact for any
             new on-disk dtype, and SIMDe's pattern (first-class scalar oracle + auto-vectorizable
             fallback + factored widening primitives) is the testing/structure discipline that
             lets nest safely add int4/bf16 embedding encodings (encoding ids 4-255 are
             reserved/additive in v1) and push the tiny preset below its current 0.283 size ratio
             without breaking deterministic builds or exact rerank.
perf:        Native passthrough = zero overhead vs hand-written intrinsics (wrappers inline away). Key claim to
             internalize: well-structured portable fallbacks auto-vectorize and beat naive scalar
             even off-target because compilers won't turn a naive loop into PSHUFB/fcvtl on their
             own -- so nest's scalar fallback should stay shape-friendly (fixed chunked loops,
             fixed-width types) to keep vectorizing on non-AVX2/non-NEON targets (older x86,
             RISC-V), where nest's dispatcher currently drops straight to scalar. -O3 and
             -fopenmp-simd-equivalent hints maximize fallback throughput with no runtime
             dependency.
license:     MIT (SIMDe COPYING permissive license); safe to study and reimplement the pattern in Rust with no
             copyleft obligation.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[REFERENCE] DLTcollab/sse2neon
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A single header-only C/C++ file (~11.7k LOC) that reimplements the entire x86
             SSE/SSE2/SSE3/SSSE3/SSE4.1/SSE4.2/AES/CRC32 intrinsic surface (xmmintrin.h through
             wmmintrin.h) on top of ARM NEON, preserving x86 semantics so SSE codebases compile
             and run on aarch64/armv7 unchanged.
innovation:  Semantics-preserving cross-ISA intrinsic emulation: every x86 SIMD op gets a hand-tuned NEON
             instruction sequence that reproduces exact x86 corner-case behavior (sign-bit packing
             order, masked-lane multiply-by-zero, IEEE-754 rounding, saturation), with
             compile-time precision flags trading speed for bit-exact fidelity. The transferable
             kernel patterns are the NEON replacements for x86 ops that have no NEON equivalent:
             movemask via shift-and-horizontal-add, and 128-bit-compare-to-bitmask via
             shift-right-narrow-by-4 (vshrn).
techniques:
  - movemask_epi8 on aarch64: vshrq_n_u8(x,7) isolates MSBs, vshlq_u8 by a per-lane shift table
    {0..7,0..7}, then vaddv_u8 over each 64-bit half packs 16 sign bits into a 16-bit integer
  - movemask on armv7 (no vaddv): cascaded vsraq_n_u64 shift-right-accumulate at offsets 7,14,28
    folds MSBs into one byte per half
  - compare-mask-to-bitmask via vshrn_n_u16(mask,4): shift-right-narrow turns a 128-bit
    all-ones/all-zeros compare result into a 64-bit nibble mask, then ctz/clz gives first-match
    length (cmpestri string ops)
  - _mm_shuffle_epi8 (pshufb) via vqtbl1q_s8 with index pre-masked by 0x8F so the high bit zeroes
    the lane, matching x86; armv7 falls back to vtbl.8 over even/odd D registers
  - _mm_maddubs_epi16 / madd via vmull widening multiply + vrshrn rounding-narrow-shift-right;
    mulhrs uses vrshrn_n_s32(mul,15) for round-to-nearest fixed-point
  - compile-time fidelity knobs: PRECISE_DIV/SQRT add Newton-Raphson iterations, PRECISE_MINMAX
    fixes NaN ordering, PRECISE_DP forces masked-lane multiply-by-zero in dot product
  - AES emulation from vqtbl1q_u8 table lookups (ShiftRows/ror32by8 permutation constants) when
    hardware crypto extensions are absent
  - QEMU-based cross-ISA differential test harness: same inputs through real SSE and the NEON
    emulation, compared bit-for-bit across armv7/armv8 aarch32/aarch64
nest steals: nest-runtime simd dispatcher (src/simd*.rs): nest already ships AVX2 + NEON + scalar dot-product
             kernels. Steal two NEON micro-patterns to harden/extend the aarch64 path. (1) The
             vshrn_n_u16(cmp,4) compare-to-nibble-mask trick: use in a NEON int8 cosine/dot kernel
             to find/skip zero lanes and to build candidate masks if nest adds SIMD-accelerated
             bm25 tokenization or pattern scanning over chunks_canonical (the known-gap
             word-segmented tokenizer). (2) The movemask shift-and-vaddv pattern: needed any time
             the NEON path reduces a per-lane boolean (quantization clamp detection, NaN guard
             before exact rerank). Also adopt sse2neon's compile-time precision philosophy: nest's
             int8 preset scores quantized embeddings directly off mmap, so a NEON vmull+vrshrn
             rounding-narrow widening-multiply (instead of plain truncation) gives bit-closer
             agreement with the AVX2/scalar reference on aarch64, protecting deterministic-build
             and exact-rerank guarantees across architectures. Reference its QEMU
             differential-test method to prove nest's three SIMD backends return identical dot
             products on the same .nest file.
compression: Low-direct: a portability/correctness library, not a compressor, so it does not shrink the .nest
             file. Indirect relevance is real: extreme compression in nest leans on aggressive
             quantization (int8 today, possibly sub-byte later), and quantized vectors are only
             useful if the on-disk codes decode and score identically on every CPU. sse2neon's
             rounding-narrow multiply kernels and cross-ISA differential-testing discipline are
             exactly what keeps a denser quantization scheme bit-faithful across AVX2/NEON/scalar,
             so the compression frontier can advance without breaking deterministic builds or the
             exact-cosine ground truth.
perf:        Header-only, zero runtime cost (all FORCE_INLINE). Most ops are 1-3 NEON instructions; expensive
             emulations are documented (_mm_maddubs_epi16 ~13 instructions; dp_ps and cmpestr* are
             multi-instruction). aarch64 movemask uses cheap vaddv horizontal add while armv7
             needs the 3-stage vsra cascade. Per-op cost tracked in perf-tier.md. Known semantic
             gaps: _mm_rsqrt_ps(0.0) returns NaN on NEON vs INF on SSE; min/max NaN ordering
             differs unless PRECISE flags set. NEON is 128-bit only, so AVX2 256-bit ops are out
             of scope (sse2neon stops at SSE4.2/128-bit lanes).
license:     MIT
