[HIGH] unum-cloud/USearch
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A single-C++11-header HNSW vector-search engine whose proximity graph and quantized vectors are
             packed into a contiguous, memory-mappable byte tape so an index is served zero-copy
             directly from disk, using compact uint40_t node IDs and transparent f16/i8/b1
             downcasting.
innovation:  Treat the entire HNSW index as a flat contiguous on-disk byte tape (per-node: level header +
             per-level neighbor lists + inline vector, laid out so absolute node offsets are
             computable from precomputed constants) plus uint40_t neighbor references, so the
             graph is served zero-copy via mmap with no in-RAM deserialization and minimal
             per-edge overhead.
techniques:
  - uint40_t: 5-byte little-endian node IDs in neighbor lists (37.5% smaller than u64, addresses
    up to 1T nodes) - cuts the dominant cost of an HNSW graph, the edge arrays
  - Contiguous node tape: each node = {level, per-level neighbor count + neighbor IDs, inline
    vector} packed back-to-back; precomputed_constants_t (neighbors_bytes, neighbors_base_bytes)
    make node offsets computable for zero-copy view()
  - Minimalist serialized header: 5x u64 only (size, connectivity, connectivity_base, max_level,
    entry_slot); metric/scalar/key types kept in a separate dense head, so graph blob format is
    stable across metrics
  - i8 quantization recipe: L2-normalize to unit length, scale to [-127,127], store int8, score in
    int8 with upcast accumulate; valid only for cosine/IP - 4x smaller than f32 at ~95% recall
  - b1x8 binary quantization: 1-bit-per-dim sign packing into bytes, scored via
    Hamming/Jaccard/Tanimoto/Sorensen popcount - 32x smaller than f32 for candidate generation
  - Transparent up/down-casting: same add/search path auto-converts f64/f32/bf16/f16/i8 so storage
    precision and compute precision are decoupled
  - AVX-512 / Arm SVE masked loads (SimSIMD/NumKong) to eliminate scalar tail loops in distance
    kernels; Horner's method for vectorized polynomial approximations
nest steals: nest-format: redesign the hnsw_index (0x07) section to mirror USearch's flat tape - a 5-field LE
             u64 header (node_count, connectivity_M, connectivity_base, max_level, entry_slot)
             followed by a contiguous node region where each node is {u8 level, then per-level:
             u16 neighbor_count + neighbor_count x uint40 IDs}. Adopt uint40_t (a repr(C) [u8;5]
             LE) for neighbor IDs - for nest's tiny preset this shrinks the graph payload ~37.5%
             vs u64 and standardizes a compact edge encoding; vectors stay in the separate
             embeddings section (mmap-scored, never zstd'd) so 0x07 and embeddings stay decoupled
             as nest requires. nest-runtime ann::HnswIndex computes node offsets from precomputed
             constants and reads neighbor IDs straight off the mmap with zero deserialization
             (matches the score-off-disk hot path). The i8 unit-norm-then-[-127,127] recipe is the
             exact spec for nest's int8 embedding encoding; add b1x8 sign-bit packing as a future
             candidate-generation encoding (32x smaller) feeding the mandatory exact-cosine rerank
             nest already runs, so binary codes never affect the returned score. USearch's header
             carries no RNG state, so nest's seeded build + alphabetical term order stays the
             source of truth. All additive (0x07 internals + new encoding id within reserved
             4-255), no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.
compression: High. (1) uint40 neighbor IDs make the HNSW graph itself ~37.5% smaller than naive u64 edges, and
             the graph is often the second-largest section after embeddings. (2) b1x8 binary codes
             (32x) and the i8 unit-norm recipe (4x) give a tiered storage ladder where 1-bit/8-bit
             codes drive ANN candidate generation while the mandatory exact rerank preserves real
             cosine - smaller indexes without lying about scores.
perf:        Claims ~10x faster HNSW than FAISS and ~20x over IndexFlatL2 brute force via SimSIMD/NumKong
             kernels using AVX-512 and Arm SVE masked loads (no scalar tail loop) - relevant to
             nest's AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatcher. i8 cuts 1536-d OpenAI vectors 75% at ~95% recall;
             in a 100M x 128-d case f32(50GB)+graph(25GB) drops to 19GB with i8. view()/mmap
             serves billion-scale indexes from disk with near-zero RAM, the same zero-copy posture
             nest targets.
license:     Apache-2.0

[HIGH] hora-search/hora
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A pure-Rust, dependency-light (no BLAS) ANN library implementing HNSW, SSG,
             Random-Projection-Tree, and Product-Quantization/IVFPQ indexes over a generic
             ANNIndex<E,T> trait, with packed_simd distance kernels and serde+bincode dump/load
             persistence.
innovation:  Product Quantization (PQ) with an Inverted-File coarse partition (IVFPQ) used as a lossy vector
             codec: split each D-dim vector into n_sub subvectors, k-means-train a codebook of
             2^sub_bits centroids per subvector, and store each vector as n_sub small codes (e.g.
             8 bits each) instead of D floats. This compresses embeddings ~8-32x while supporting
             Asymmetric Distance Computation (ADC): the query stays full-precision and distances
             to all centroids are precomputed into a lookup table, so scoring a stored code is
             just n_sub table lookups + adds. IVF adds a residual-quantization layer (each cluster
             PQ-encodes the residual to its coarse centroid) to cut quantization error.
techniques:
  - Product Quantization: vector split into n_sub subvectors, codebook = 1<<sub_bits centroids per
    subquantizer trained by k-means over _train_epoch iterations; code width
    _sub_bytes=(sub_bits+7)/8
  - IVFPQ residual quantization: _n_kmeans_center coarse centroids, _ivflist: Vec<Vec<usize>>
    inverted lists, per-cluster PQIndex encoding the residual (_residual=coarse center),
    _search_n_center probes
  - Asymmetric Distance Computation via precomputed query-to-centroid distance tables
    (get_distance_from_vec_range): full-precision query vs quantized codes
  - Codes stored as packed Vec<u8> (_code_bytes=sub_bytes*n_sub); centroids as Vec<Vec<Vec<E>>>
    shaped [n_sub][n_center][sub_dim]
  - SerializableIndex trait with dump(path)/load(path) over serde derive + bincode 1.3 binary
    format
  - Generic ANNIndex<E,T> trait separating element/distance type E from external id type T; Metric
    enum {Dot, Euclidean, Manhattan, Cosine}
  - packed_simd_2 0.3.6 vectorized distance kernels with a BruteForce SIMD baseline and no BLAS
    dependency
  - build->dump offline-codec pipeline (k-means via rand 0.8) usable deterministically if seeded
nest steals: Adopt PQ/IVFPQ as a new embedding ENCODING in nest-format (reserve a new encoding id in the
             additive 4-255 range, e.g. pq8), parallel to the existing int8 codec in
             crates/nest-format/src/encoding. The embeddings section stores a small trained
             codebook (n_sub x 2^bits x sub_dim f32 centroids) plus one n_sub-byte code per chunk,
             replacing the D f32/f16/int8 vectors. This is the biggest single lever for the
             'compress more than everyone' north star: PQ8 on a 384-d MiniLM embedding is ~48
             codes/vector vs 384 f32 (32x) or 384 int8 (8x), pushing the tiny preset below its
             current 0.283 size ratio. To honor nest's HARD search contract, do NOT score PQ codes
             as the final answer: use PQ as a fast ANN candidate generator in nest-runtime
             (ann::PqIndex alongside HnswIndex, with ADC lookup tables built per query), then run
             the MANDATORY exact-cosine rerank on decoded/materialized vectors so the returned
             score is real cosine, never an ANN proxy. Two shapes: (a) PQ-compressed storage with
             decode-then-rerank, or (b) PQ as an HNSW companion/replacement candidate stage.
             Determinism is met by seeding k-means and fixing centroid tie-breaks in index order
             (mirrors nest's deterministic-hnsw and alphabetical-bm25 rules). Reimplement ADC on
             nest's own AVX2/NEON/scalar SIMD dispatcher (do NOT vendor packed_simd: it is
             nightly-only/unmaintained and breaks edition-2024 stable). Keep the codec in a
             <=300-line single-responsibility file; bincode/serde persistence is discarded in
             favor of nest's frozen repr(C) little-endian layout. The ANNIndex/SerializableIndex
             trait shape and Metric enum are reference-grade for the nest-runtime ann module API.
compression: Directly central. PQ/IVFPQ is THE classic lossy vector-compression scheme (8-32x over f32) and the
             most promising path beyond nest's current int8 (8x) embedding quantization.
             Highest-leverage technique for compressing the embeddings section specifically, with
             a well-understood recall/size tradeoff and IVF residual coding to recover accuracy.
perf:        Pure Rust, no BLAS. packed_simd_2 0.3.6 kernels, but packed_simd is unmaintained/nightly-only and
             incompatible with nest's edition-2024 stable build, so reimplement ADC on nest's own
             dispatcher. ADC makes per-vector scoring O(n_sub) table lookups instead of O(D) float
             multiplies, so PQ search is smaller AND faster than brute-force f32 at scale; the
             lookup table is built once per query and IVF _search_n_center bounds candidates
             scanned. Codes are contiguous u8: mmap-friendly, cache-dense, scannable straight off
             disk in nest's score-from-mmap design (only the rerank stage needs decoded f32). The
             bincode/serde persistence layer is not reusable, but the codebook+codes data model
             maps cleanly onto a nest section.
license:     Apache-2.0 (permissive). Safe to study and reimplement the PQ/IVFPQ algorithm and the
             ANNIndex/SerializableIndex API shape; the bincode 1.3 / serde / packed_simd_2
             dependencies are NOT vendored.

[HIGH] rust-cv/hnsw
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A from-scratch, no_std-capable Rust HNSW (Malkov-Yashunin) index that is fully generic over the
             distance metric via the `space::Metric` trait, with const-generic fixed-width
             neighbor arrays and a first-class binary/Hamming path using XOR+popcnt over
             `Bits128..Bits4096` SIMD types and an amortized-O(1) `hamming-heap`.
innovation:  Decouple the ANN traversal metric from the stored representation: the graph navigates on a cheap,
             quantization-friendly integer distance (Hamming over 1-bit-packed vectors, computed
             as `(a ^ b).count_ones()` with hardware popcnt/SIMD), while the metric stays a
             swappable `Metric` trait whose `Unit` is "the smallest unsigned integer that
             represents your space" so ordering is branch-free integer compares. This is exactly
             the shape nest needs to push ANN below int8: navigate on binary codes, rerank exact
             cosine on top (which nest already mandates).
techniques:
  - space::Metric trait with associated `Unit: Ord + Zero + Copy` = smallest unsigned int for the
    space (integer distances => branch-free, deterministic ordering)
  - Hamming distance via XOR + count_ones (popcnt), used to TRAVERSE the graph instead of float
    math
  - Bits128..Bits4096 newtype tuple structs wrapping fixed byte arrays to unlock
    autovectorized/SIMD popcnt over binary codes
  - hamming-heap: amortized constant-time min/max heap bucketed into 256 Hamming-distance bins,
    replacing O(log n) binary-heap pops on candidate/nearest sets
  - const-generic fixed-width neighbor storage: Node { neighbors: NeighborNodes<M> { [usize; M] }
    } + zero layer Vec<NeighborNodes<M0>>, empty slot = !0 sentinel, no per-node Vec allocs
  - deterministic seeded level assignment: level = (-ln(unif) * (1/ln M)) as usize via R: RngCore
    + SeedableRng (libm::log for no_std)
  - Searcher object pooling: candidates/nearest reused across queries to avoid per-query
    allocation
  - separate M (non-zero layers) and M0=2*M (dense zero layer) out-degrees, fixed at the type
    level
nest steals: Steals into crates/nest-runtime/src/ann/ and a new codec in crates/nest-format/src/encoding/. (1)
             BINARY-QUANTIZED ANN METRIC: add a 1-bit codec (sign of each centered/L2-normalized
             dim, packed 64 dims per u64) as a new RESERVED embedding encoding id (4-255 free in
             v1, additive-only). Build and traverse the existing HNSW (ann/build.rs,
             ann/search.rs) on Hamming = XOR+popcnt over the packed codes instead of cosine_dist()
             in ann/mod.rs; nest's MANDATORY exact-cosine rerank already guarantees the returned
             score is real cosine, so binary codes only steer traversal and recall is recovered by
             rerank. ~32x smaller than f32 / ~4x smaller than int8 for navigation vectors, and
             popcnt drops into the SIMD dispatcher (AVX2 VPOPCNTQ / NEON CNT / scalar count_ones
             fallback) beside the existing dot kernel. (2) FIXED-WIDTH NEIGHBOR ENCODING: nest's
             Node stores `neighbors: Vec<Vec<u32>>` and codec.rs writes a per-layer u32 count +
             variable ids; adopt const-generic `[u32; M]`/`[u32; M0]` with a !0 sentinel so the
             0x07 hnsw_index section becomes fixed-stride (m known from header), smaller and
             zero-copy mmap-readable, still deterministic. (3) Optionally port hamming-heap's
             bucketed heap into ann/search.rs Candidate handling when distances are integer
             Hamming, dropping the f32 partial_cmp sorts.
compression: Directly on the north star. Binary (1-bit) quantization of the ANN navigation vectors is the
             single biggest lever below nest's current int8 floor: 768-dim f32 = 3072 B/vec, int8
             = 768 B, binary = 96 B/vec (32x vs f32, 8x vs int8). Because nest ALWAYS reranks with
             exact cosine, lossy binary codes never touch the returned score; they only cut
             index/navigation footprint, exactly what the `tiny` preset (0.283 ratio, recall@10
             0.992) optimizes. A `nano` preset (zstd + binary-quantized ANN nav + f16 rerank
             embeddings) could plausibly beat 0.283. Fixed-width neighbor arrays further shrink
             the 0x07 section vs the current length-prefixed encoding.
perf:        Hamming via popcnt is ~single-cycle throughput per 64 bits on modern x86 (POPCNT/AVX-512 VPOPCNTQ)
             and ARM (NEON CNT + addv); a 768-dim binary code is 12 u64 popcounts vs 768 f32 FMAs
             for cosine, ~order of magnitude cheaper for traversal, leaving rerank as the only
             float work. The Bits* newtypes exist so the array is statically sized and
             autovectorizes. const-generic `[u32; M]` neighbor arrays remove pointer-chasing and
             per-node heap allocs (better cache locality during walks) and make the on-disk
             section a flat mmap-able stride. hamming-heap turns candidate maintenance from O(log
             n) into amortized O(1) via 256 distance buckets.
license:     MIT (hnsw, space, hamming-heap all MIT or dual MIT/Apache-2.0) - clean for vendoring/porting into
             nest's small Rust core.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] djc/instant-distance
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A pure-Rust HNSW (Hierarchical Navigable Small Worlds, Malkov-Yashunin) approximate nearest
             neighbor index with fixed-arity neighbor arrays, generational visited tracking, the
             Malkov-Yashunin diversity pruning heuristic, and an optional serde serialization of
             the whole graph.
innovation:  Represent every HNSW node as a flat, fixed-size array of u32 PointIds (UpperNode([PointId; M]) on
             upper layers, ZeroNode([PointId; M*2]) on layer zero) so the entire graph is a
             contiguous Vec of plain integer records with no per-node pointers or heap allocations
             -- a layout that serializes trivially and could be mmap'd zero-copy, while the
             M*2-vs-M layer split bounds the on-disk graph size to a known constant per point.
techniques:
  - Fixed-arity neighbor records: UpperNode([PointId; M]) with M=32, ZeroNode([PointId; M*2]) with
    M*2=64, PointId = u32 newtype, INVALID = u32::MAX sentinel padding unused slots
  - Two-tier storage: Hnsw { ef_search, points: Vec<P>, zero: Vec<ZeroNode>, layers:
    Vec<Vec<UpperNode>> } -- separates the dense base graph from sparse upper layers
  - Generational Visited set: struct Visited { store: Vec<u8>, generation: u8 } gives O(1) reset
    by bumping the generation counter instead of clearing the buffer between searches
  - Malkov-Yashunin diversity/select_heuristic pruning: drops a candidate when it is closer to an
    already-selected neighbor than to the query, with optional keep_pruned, to bridge clusters and
    raise recall at fixed degree
  - Deterministic level assignment: SmallRng::seed_from_u64(seed) with ml = 1/ln(M) controls the
    exponential layer distribution -> byte-reproducible graph from same seed+points
  - Layer-aware descent: ef=1 / num=M on upper layers, ef=ef_search / num=M*2 on layer zero;
    greedy descent then ef-bounded beam search on the base layer
  - Candidate { distance: OrderedFloat<f32>, pid: PointId } heap entries; ordered-float for total
    ordering of f32 distances without NaN UB
  - serde + serde-big-array derive serializes the full graph (points + zero + layers) as one blob;
    MIT OR Apache-2.0 licensed
nest steals: nest-format already reserves section 0x07 for hnsw_index; instant-distance's flat fixed-arity
             layout is the exact on-disk encoding nest should adopt. Store ZeroNode as a packed
             [u32; M*2] and UpperNode as [u32; M] arrays, all little-endian repr(C), so the graph
             section is a pure POD byte block that nest-runtime mmaps and walks with zero
             deserialization (no serde, no allocation), matching the offline/mmap hot-path
             constraint. The M-vs-M*2 split and u32::MAX padding give a constant, predictable
             section size that compresses well under zstd (the index section is NOT on the SIMD
             scoring hot path, so unlike embeddings it CAN be zstd-encoded, helping the
             tiny/hybrid presets shrink further). Port the deterministic SmallRng::seed_from_u64 +
             ml=1/ln(M) level assignment into nest-runtime::ann::HnswIndex to satisfy nest's
             seeded-determinism rule, and adopt the generational Visited { Vec<u8>, generation }
             pattern to avoid per-query allocation. The select_heuristic diversity pruning is what
             lets nest's tiny preset hit recall@10 0.992 at low degree; keep it. Crucially, nest's
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank means the graph only needs to be a good candidate
             generator, so nest can push M lower than 32 (smaller index, more compression) and
             lean on rerank to recover precision -- a direct lever for 'compress more than
             everyone' without losing the real-cosine guarantee. Split across files to respect the
             <=300 line cap: types (node structs), build (seeded insert), search (descent +
             visited), and an encode/decode codec in nest-format.
compression: High and direct: the fixed-arity [u32; M] / [u32; M*2] node layout is a POD byte block with
             constant per-point size that zstd-compresses well and mmaps zero-copy; lowering M
             (safe because nest always exact-reranks) directly shrinks the 0x07 index section,
             improving the tiny preset's 0.283 size ratio without harming recall.
perf:        No SIMD distance kernel exists (open issue #20 requests generalizing SIMD to n-length vectors);
             distance is user-supplied via the Point trait, so nest's existing AVX2/NEON
             dot-product dispatcher is strictly ahead and should NOT be replaced by anything here.
             Performance wins come from the cache-friendly flat Vec<ZeroNode> layout (sequential
             u32 reads, no pointer chasing), bounds-check elision over fixed arrays, generational
             visited (no clear cost), and rayon-parallel construction. Build is parallel (rayon +
             parking_lot RwLock on zero layer during insert); query is single-threaded beam
             search. ordered-float avoids NaN ordering hazards.
license:     MIT OR Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] meilisearch/arroy
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust ANN library that ports Spotify's Annoy (forest of random-projection / random-hyperplane
             trees) onto LMDB memory-mapped key-value storage, adding incremental insert/delete,
             RoaringBitmap-filtered search, and optional 1-bit binary quantization of stored
             vectors.
innovation:  Binary quantization of stored vectors to exactly 1 bit per dimension (sign bit: x>0 -> 1, x<=0 ->
             0) for 32x raw vector shrink, while keeping the split hyperplanes in float32 so ANN
             partition quality is preserved -- the "lossy storage, faithful structure" split is
             the transferable idea for extreme compression of embedding sections.
techniques:
  - Forest of random-projection trees: each internal node stores a random hyperplane (from 2
    sampled points' equidistant plane) splitting space into two subspaces; n_trees forest,
    search_k controls recall/latency tradeoff
  - Two node types on disk: split/internal nodes (float hyperplane normal + offset) and
    descendant/leaf nodes (item ids); deletion collapses a last-descendant node into a direct item
    link to cut node count and lookups
  - 1-bit binary quantization: pack sign bits, reconstruct -1/+1 on the fly via bitmask + select
    (NEON vbslq_f32) so distance math runs on faked floats; default cosine over [0,1] vectors
  - Incremental indexing: insert/delete rewrites only the affected tree path (a few nodes) instead
    of rebuilding the whole forest, made cheap by LMDB copy-on-write Btree
  - LMDB-backed storage via heed: multi-reader concurrency while writing, u16 multi-index
    namespacing, mmap straight off disk
  - RoaringBitmap filtered search (Filtered-DiskANN style): restrict traversal to a candidate id
    subset to skip whole subtrees
  - Bachrach et al. 2014 transform: maps inner-product space to cosine space so dot-product search
    reuses cosine tree machinery
  - SIMD distance kernels borrowed from Qdrant for the float path
nest steals: Two distinct steals. (1) Compression: add a binary/sign-bit encoding to nest-format encoding ids
             (reserved 4-255) as a new embedding codec -- 1 bit/dim is 32x smaller than f32 and 4x
             smaller than nest's current int8 tiny preset, pushing the size ratio well below
             0.283. nest's mandatory exact-cosine rerank already covers the precision loss: the
             1-bit vectors serve as a coarse ANN/prefilter (Hamming via XOR+popcount, a cheap new
             SIMD kernel in nest-runtime's dispatcher alongside AVX2/NEON dot), then the real
             f32/f16 vectors produce the true cosine score, so the search_contract ground-truth
             guarantee is untouched. Highest-value adaptation. (2) Index codec: arroy's
             random-projection-tree node format (float split node + id descendant node,
             deterministic under a seeded RNG) is a smaller, simpler alternative to nest's
             hnsw_index(0x07) and fits nest's <=300 LOC/file and deterministic constraints better
             than HNSW -- a candidate reserved section (0x09+) 'rptree_index'. Its
             collapse-on-delete trick informs future real-time edit support. Do NOT copy LMDB: it
             violates nest's single-file/offline/frozen-format constraints; nest must keep nodes
             as repr(C) little-endian structs inside its own section.
compression: Directly central. 1-bit sign quantization is the single biggest lever arroy offers for "compress
             more than everyone": 32x over f32, 4x over nest's current int8 tiny preset. The key
             insight that makes it safe for nest is that nest already mandates exact-cosine
             rerank, so an aggressive 1-bit lossy code can be a pure prefilter without corrupting
             returned scores -- arroy itself had to keep hyperplanes in float precisely because it
             has no rerank stage. A new 'binary'/'sign1' embedding codec plus a tiny-binary preset
             is a concrete path to a sub-0.1 size ratio.
perf:        Binary distance is XOR + popcount (Hamming), extremely CPU-friendly and SIMD-trivial vs float dot
             products; arroy reports ~7x faster indexing and ~6.5x disk reduction at 3072 dims (26
             GiB -> 4 GiB), but note real-world space savings were diluted (~15% in one case)
             because tree/internal nodes are NOT quantized, only user vectors. Lesson for nest:
             quantize only the embeddings section (already the hot mmap path), leave
             index/structure full precision. arroy's search is acknowledged slower than Annoy/HNSW
             due to log(n) Btree lookups and non-aligned LMDB vectors -- nest avoids both via
             aligned repr(C) structs in one mmap'd file rather than LMDB.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] TusKANNy/kannolo (core codec engine in companion crate TusKANNy/vectorium)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A modular Rust (edition 2024, nightly portable-SIMD) HNSW + Product-Quantization ANN library whose
             real value for nest lives in its `vectorium` codec crate: a SIMD Stream-VByte sparse
             encoder with recursive-graph-bisection component reordering, an 8-bit PQ with SoA
             distance-table ADC, and cache-blocked batch-6 distance kernels.
innovation:  Compression is a codec trait, not an index feature: every vector encoder (PlainDense f32/f16,
             ScalarDense int8, ProductQuantizer 8-bit, DotVByteFixedU8 sparse) implements one
             VectorEncoder/QueryEvaluator interface so the same HNSW graph walks raw, quantized,
             or VByte-packed bytes interchangeably, and the graph is always BUILT on
             full-precision vectors while only the stored payload is compressed (why they beat
             Faiss up to 11x: quantization never pollutes graph topology, only storage size). The
             single most transferable trick is DotVByteFixedU8: sparse vectors are
             component-delta-gap + Stream-VByte byte-packed (1 control bit/element = 1 vs 2 bytes)
             over u64 words, values are int8 fixed-point, AND component indices are first remapped
             by a recursive graph bisection permutation learned offline so co-occurring terms get
             adjacent ids, shrinking the d-gaps VByte then encodes near-optimally.
techniques:
  - DotVByteFixedU8 sparse codec: sort components, delta-gap, Stream-VByte (1 control bit/elem = 1
    vs 2 bytes) packed into u64 words, int8 fixed-point values aligned at the tail for SIMD
    gather; decode via 256-entry compile-time swizzle mask LUT + simd_prefix_sum
  - Recursive graph bisection (rgbisect crate) learns a component->id permutation offline so
    frequently co-occurring dimensions become adjacent, minimizing VByte d-gaps; inverse
    permutation restores original ids on decode
  - 8-bit Product Quantization: M subspaces (M%4==0), KSUB=256 centroids/subspace, 1 byte/code;
    per-subspace seedable k-means (25 iters, 10% sampling above 1M vecs); centroids stored SoA
    [M*dsub*KSUB] so the distance-table build auto-vectorizes
  - ADC asymmetric distance: precompute per-query distance table [KSUB*M] once, then score each
    code with 4 independent f32 accumulators over chunks_exact(4) via get_unchecked LUT gathers
    (4-way ILP)
  - Two-level PQ (coarse IVF u32 id + residual PQ codes + optional f16 residual norm) for ColBERT
    MaxSim, blocked [coarse_ids][pq_codes][norms] layout, const-generic Q unrolled SAXPY kernel
  - Cache-aware batch-6 distance kernels (dense dot/L2 and dense-sparse) with 6 independent
    accumulators plus explicit prefetch_with_range during the graph beam search to hide memory
    latency
  - CSR graph storage: flat neighbors:Box<[u32]> + offsets:Box<[usize]> compact form vs
    fixed-degree Optioned<u32> padded array for the growable builder, with optional
    local->external id mapping
  - fast-math algebraic_add/algebraic_mul intrinsics + std::simd gather/swizzle, and scalar code
    that auto-vectorizes by construction (SoA layouts, register-resident accumulators,
    single-write tiling)
nest steals: Three additive grafts, all within format v1. (1) nest-format/src/encoding.rs: add a new encoding
             id for a `vbyte` codec (delta-gap + Stream-VByte + int8) ported from DotVByteFixedU8,
             applied to bm25_index(0x08) term postings and the chunk_ids section that nest already
             zstd-compresses; it never touches embeddings, honoring the 'never compress
             embeddings' rule. (2) nest-python build step + nest-runtime/src/bm25: add
             deterministic recursive-graph-bisection term reordering (alphabetical tie-break keeps
             builds byte-identical), store the term-id permutation in the bm25_index header, apply
             the inverse map at query time so postings d-gaps shrink before vbyte/zstd. (3)
             nest-runtime/src/simd + ann::HnswIndex: adopt the PQ ADC distance-table + batch-6 +
             prefetch pattern and add 8-bit PQ as a fourth embedding encoding (raw/f16/int8/pq).
             Because nest mandates an exact-cosine rerank, PQ is used ONLY for the ANN candidate
             shortlist while exact f32/f16 cosine still produces the returned score - exactly
             kannolo's build-on-full-precision philosophy - so PQ recall loss is fully masked and
             the size ratio drops far below the current tiny preset.
compression: Directly on-mission. The DotVByte+RGB sparse codec is a textbook 'compress more than everyone'
             technique for BM25 postings and any inverted/sparse section; 8-bit PQ behind nest's
             mandatory exact rerank lets nest store embeddings at ~M bytes/vector (e.g. 4-16 bytes
             vs 1536 f32) for the ANN shortlist while still returning real cosine, slashing the
             size ratio below the current tiny preset (0.283) without breaking the recall
             guarantee. Both compress only non-embedding sections or ANN-only payloads, leaving
             the directly-mmap-scored f32/f16 embeddings untouched on the hot path.
perf:        Beats Faiss up to 11.1x on HNSW build by building the graph on full-precision vectors, not
             quantized ones. Kernels lean on portable std::simd (nightly), fast-math
             algebraic_add/mul, SoA centroid layout for auto-vectorized table builds, batch-6 ILP
             with explicit prefetch, and 256-entry compile-time swizzle mask LUTs; PQ table build
             keeps 8 partial accumulators in registers across all dsub passes (single write per
             slot); ADC scoring uses 4-way unrolled get_unchecked LUT gathers. CAVEAT: requires
             Rust NIGHTLY (portable_simd, gen blocks) - nest targets stable edition 2024, so nest
             must reimplement the codec on stable using its existing AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatcher
             rather than std::simd. Graph uses u32 neighbor ids (max u32::MAX-1 nodes); CSR
             offsets are usize.
license:     MIT (both kannolo and vectorium) - clean for vendoring/porting. The recursive bisection pulls in
             the rgbisect / enhanced-graph-bisection crate; verify that crate's license before
             vendoring that specific piece.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] spotify/annoy
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A C++/Python random-projection-forest ANN index whose entire forest lives in one contiguous,
             headerless, raw-float32 blob that is mmapped read-only and shared across processes
             with zero deserialization.
innovation:  The index IS the file: a flat array of fixed-size `Node` structs (`_s = offsetof(Node, v) +
             _f*sizeof(T)` bytes each) packed contiguously, so loading is just mmap + pointer
             arithmetic `(Node*)((uint8_t*)_nodes + _s*i)` with NO header, NO footer metadata, and
             NO parse step. Node count is recovered as `file_size / _s`; tree roots are recovered
             by scanning backward from EOF and collecting nodes whose `n_descendants == m` (the
             max). One union'd struct overloads three roles via a single discriminant
             (`n_descendants`): leaf (==1, vector in v[]), compact internal node (2..=_K, child
             item-ids packed where v[] would be), or split node (>_K, hyperplane normal in v[]). A
             zero-copy, query-immediately, structurally-self-describing on-disk format.
techniques:
  - Random projection forest: n_trees independent trees, each split is the hyperplane equidistant
    from two sampled points (two_means, 200 refinement iterations) -- build-once, static, no graph
    mutation
  - Headerless contiguous blob: n_nodes = file_size/_s, roots found by backward EOF scan for max
    n_descendants, roots copied into a trailing segment at build end
  - Single union'd Node struct discriminated by n_descendants: leaf(1)/compact-internal(<=_K ids
    inline)/split(hyperplane in v[]); _K=(_s-offsetof(Node,children))/sizeof(S)
  - search_k runtime knob (default n*n_trees) over a std::priority_queue keyed by margin() (signed
    distance to split plane) -- one parameter trades recall for latency at query time, index
    unchanged
  - Hand-written SIMD distance kernels: AVX _mm256_mul_ps/_mm256_add_ps + hsum256_ps_avx; AVX512
    _mm512_fmadd_ps + _mm512_reduce_add_ps; Manhattan/Euclidean use _mm256_andnot_ps for abs
  - Angular metric stores L2-normalized vectors so cosine becomes sqrt(2-2cos); DotProduct metric
    reduced to cosine space via an extra dot_factor dimension per node
  - Hamming metric packs bits into 64-bit words and uses popcount primitives -- a compact
    binary-quantization candidate path
  - prefault flag: eager full read vs lazy on-demand page-faulting of the mmap, tuning cold-start
    latency vs RAM for partial scans
nest steals: nest-format: adopt Annoy's discriminant-only headerless self-description for ANN sub-blocks. The
             lesson is that an ANN section body can be a single flat array of fixed-stride records
             where structure is recovered by arithmetic (count = byte_len/stride) plus a trailing
             roots segment, eliminating per-node offset tables -- directly shrinks the optional
             hnsw_index(0x07) footprint and keeps it mmap-zero-copy. nest-runtime: Annoy's static
             build-once forest is a compelling SECOND ann backend beside ann::HnswIndex, exposed
             as a RESERVED section id (>=0x09, additive, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump) and a
             search-ann variant; a seeded KISS-style RNG (mirroring nest's existing deterministic
             hnsw seeding) makes the forest byte-identical across builds, satisfying
             reproducibility, and trees compress far better under zstd than HNSW graphs since they
             have no back-edges. The single search_k knob maps onto nest's mandatory exact-cosine
             rerank: the forest over-fetches cheap candidates, nest reranks with SIMD dot products
             off mmap, so ann stays a pure candidate generator and the returned score is real
             cosine. Annoy's Hamming/popcount path is the primitive for a future int1/binary
             embedding encoding feeding the ann candidate stage (never the exact ground truth).
compression: Indirect but real. Annoy does ZERO embedding compression (raw float32, no entropy coder, no
             quantization), so it is not a codec to copy. Its value to compress-more-than-everyone
             is structural: (1) headerless self-describing flat-array layout removes offset/index
             metadata so an ANN section is near-pure payload that zstd compresses cleanly; (2)
             static projection trees have no bidirectional edges (unlike HNSW), so they
             entropy-code much smaller; (3) the Hamming/popcount path is a concrete template for
             binary/1-bit embedding quantization, the most aggressive lever for shrinking
             embeddings -- but since nest scores embeddings directly from mmap, such a codec would
             feed only the ann candidate stage, never the exact-cosine ground truth.
perf:        Hand-rolled AVX/AVX512 kernels (_mm256_add_ps + hsum256_ps_avx; _mm512_fmadd_ps +
             _mm512_reduce_add_ps) match nest's existing SIMD dispatcher design; Annoy has
             AVX/AVX512 but NO NEON, so nest's aarch64 NEON path is already ahead. mmap with
             optional prefault (eager for hot indexes, lazy page-fault for low-RAM/partial scans)
             is a tunable nest could expose. Memory-minimal by design (Spotify: 5M x 40-dim shared
             as one blob across processes); build is multi-threaded (ThreadedBuildPolicy, seed =
             base_seed + thread_idx) but query is a single cache-friendly pointer walk. No
             deserialization cost: load() is mmap + a backward EOF root scan, so query-immediately
             after load.
license:     Apache-2.0

# category: vector-quantization  (6 repos)
# focus: vector quantization and bit-compression of embeddings (the compression key for vectors)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] gaoj0017/RaBitQ
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A randomized 1-bit-per-dimension quantizer (SIGMOD 2024) that maps a normalized D-dim vector,
             after a random orthogonal rotation, to its sign-bit string on the {+-1/sqrt(D)}^D
             hypercube codebook, yielding an unbiased inner-product estimator with a sharp
             O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound, computed via bitwise-AND + popcount.
innovation:  Replace learned codebooks (PQ/OPQ) with a fixed deterministic codebook (the sign-corners of the
             unit sphere scaled by 1/sqrt(D)) plus a random rotation P. Quantization is just
             "rotate, take signs" -- no training, no k-means -- yet the estimator
             <o-bar,q>/<o-bar,o> is provably unbiased with a per-vector O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound
             holding with high probability. This makes ANN distance estimation a quantity with a
             confidence interval, letting the search loop decide per-candidate whether to trust
             the cheap estimate or trigger exact rerank.
techniques:
  - Fixed sphere-corner codebook C = {+1/sqrt(D), -1/sqrt(D)}^D; quantize = sign(P^-1 * o), stored
    as a D-bit sign string (1 bit/dim)
  - Random orthogonal rotation P (Johnson-Lindenstrauss style) randomizes geometry so the
    sign-quantization error concentrates; same P reused for all vectors and queries
  - Unbiased estimator: <o,q> ~= <o-bar,q>/<o-bar,o>, where <o-bar,o> (~0.8, concentrated) is the
    single per-vector scalar stored alongside the bit code
  - Asymmetric bit allocation: data = 1 bit/dim, query quantized to 4 bits/dim; error dominated by
    data quantization so query precision is cheap insurance
  - Weighted popcount kernel: dot = 1*popcount(code & q1) + 2*popcount(code & q2) +
    4*popcount(code & q3) + 8*popcount(code & q4) over the 4 query bit-planes
  - Distance reconstruction via law of cosines around a centroid c: ||o_r-q_r||^2 = ||o_r-c||^2 +
    ||q_r-c||^2 - 2*||o_r-c||*||q_r-c||*estIP
  - FastScan-style batched layout: 32 codes reorganized at index time for SIMD lane-parallel scan
    (AVX2/AVX512 VPOPCNTDQ); scalar bitwise fallback in space.h
  - Per-vector footprint = D bits + a couple of f32 scalars (the <o-bar,o> factor and ||o_r-c||),
    independent of any trained dictionary
nest steals: Add an int1/rabitq embedding codec to nest-format encoding (new RESERVED encoding id 4-255,
             additive within v1, repr(C) little-endian bit code + two f32 per-vector scalars). The
             rotation matrix P is deterministic from a seed -> store the seed in
             provenance/search_contract, preserving nest's reproducible-build + deterministic
             guarantee (same seed => byte-identical codes). In nest-runtime add a rabitq distance
             estimator beside the SIMD dot dispatcher: NEON vcnt/vaddv + AVX2 popcount kernels off
             the mmapped bit codes, scalar fallback to satisfy "no panic / three-arch" rule. This
             slots perfectly into nest's existing mandatory-exact-rerank contract: RaBitQ becomes
             the cheap ANN proxy (replacing or feeding the hnsw shortlist) and the stored real
             f32/f16 embeddings still drive the ground-truth cosine rerank, so the returned score
             stays honest. The error bound lets nest cite a recall guarantee in the
             search_contract, not just an empirical number.
compression: Directly the compression key for the embeddings section, nest's largest and currently
             least-compressed payload (zstd is banned on embeddings because they are scored from
             mmap; today the smallest is int8 at 0.283 ratio). RaBitQ takes a 768-dim f32 vector
             (3072 bytes) to ~768 bits + 8 bytes = ~104 bytes, a ~30x reduction (~0.034 ratio on
             the embedding bytes), far below int8's 4x, while remaining directly scorable from
             mmap via popcount with NO decompression on the hot path -- the exact property zstd
             lacked. The provable O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound plus nest's existing exact rerank means
             this extreme compression costs almost no recall (RaBitQ matches PQ/OPQ accuracy at
             ~3x their speed; multi-bit 4-5 bit variants reach 90-95% recall with no rerank at
             all). This is the single highest-leverage move toward the "compress data more than
             everyone" north star.
perf:        Distance estimate reduces to bitwise-AND + popcount, a 1-cycle-class op on modern CPUs; dedicated
             AVX512VPOPCNTDQ on IceLake+/Zen4+, and aarch64 NEON has vcnt + horizontal add
             (relevant since nest targets NEON). FastScan batches 32 codes for lane-parallel SIMD
             scan. Reported ~3x faster than PQ/OPQ at equal accuracy. Memory: 1 bit/dim + ~2 f32
             scalars/vector, no trained dictionary to load, fully streamable/mmap-friendly.
             Query-side cost is the 4 bit-plane weighted popcount; estimator is branch-light.
license:     Apache-2.0 (original archived repo; active development moved to VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library).
             Method published SIGMOD 2024, arXiv 2405.12497, DOI 10.1145/3654970. C++ (Eigen
             3.4.0) + Python; nest would reimplement clean-room in Rust, so only the algorithm
             (patent-free, openly published) is used.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] VectorDB-NTU/RaBitQ-Library
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        RaBitQ is a randomized 1-bit-per-dimension vector quantizer (D-dim -> D bits + 2 float correction
             factors) using a random hypercube rotation to yield an unbiased distance estimator
             with provable O(1/sqrt(D)) error, evaluated via AND+popcount or FastScan.
innovation:  Random orthogonal rotation converts 1-bit sign quantization into a provably-unbiased distance
             estimator with a sharp O(1/sqrt(D)) error bound, making bit-only distance ordering
             reliable; the estimator reduces to AND+popcount against an int4 query, beating PQ on
             both size and speed with theoretical guarantees PQ lacks.
techniques:
  - 1-bit/dim binary code = nearest vertex of a randomly-rotated hypercube on the unit sphere (D/8
    bytes/vec)
  - Two stored f32 factors/vec: distance-to-centroid ||v-c|| and projection factor <x_bar, o>
  - Asymmetric quantization: data is 1-bit, query is int4/B-bit scalar quant, combined via bitwise
    ops
  - Unbiased distance/inner-product estimator with O(1/sqrt(D)) bound from JLT theory
  - Distance kernel = bitwise-AND + popcount (single query) or SIMD shuffle FastScan (batched)
  - Random orthogonal matrix stored as a deterministic SEED; P^-1 q computed once/query, amortized
    over the probe list
  - B-bit extension splits code into binary MSBs + ex-code for incremental refinement (4/5/7-bit ~
    90/95/99% recall no-rerank)
  - Centroid-relative encoding before normalize+quantize (IVF/KMeans, or single global centroid
    standalone)
nest steals: New embedding codec in nest-format/src/encoding (id from reserved 4-255, additive, no version
             bump): per vector = D/8 packed sign bits as LE u64 words + 2 LE f32 factors; store
             global centroid + rotation seed in the embeddings section header (repr(C), LE,
             deterministic). ~32x vs f32 (1024-dim: 4096->136 bytes), a tier below int8. Add an
             AND+popcount SIMD kernel in nest-runtime/src/simd (NEON/AVX2/scalar) that scores
             codes straight off mmap as the ANN/coarse score; nest's mandatory exact cosine rerank
             stays ground truth, so recall holds. New nest-cli "nano" preset (zstd/rabitq/hnsw)
             and pairs with the existing HnswIndex (HNSW+RaBitQ). Determinism preserved via seeded
             rotation and fixed-seed centroid.
compression: Highest-leverage compression for nest: ~32x on the embeddings section (the largest part of a .nest
             file) vs 4x int8 / 2x f16, with recall preserved because nest already does mandatory
             exact cosine rerank and the unbiased O(1/sqrt(D)) estimator selects good candidates.
             B-bit ex-code gives a 1/4/5/7-bit dial. Directly advances 'compress more than
             everyone' for vector bytes.
perf:        Single-query distance is AND+popcount, single-cycle ops, reported ~3x faster than PQ RAM table
             lookups at equal accuracy; batched queries use PQ-style SIMD gather/shuffle FastScan.
             Reads D/8+8 bytes/vec instead of 4D, so the mmap hot path is memory-bandwidth-light.
             Inverse rotation P^-1 q is one O(D^2) op per query amortized over the whole probe
             list; per-vector rotation is paid once at build time. GPU implementation also exists
             upstream.
license:     Not confirmed from README in this session; upstream is C++ (academic, VectorDB-NTU / NTU
             Singapore). Verify exact license (likely MIT/Apache-style academic release) before
             vendoring; nest only needs the algorithm/format, reimplemented clean in Rust, so
             license is a reference concern not a code-copy dependency.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] Stardust-SJF/cuvs_rabitq
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A GPU-native (NVIDIA cuVS) fork implementing IVF-RaBitQ: it fuses inverted-file clustering with
             RaBitQ quantization, which compresses each D-dim float32 vector to ~1 bit/dim plus
             two scalar factors and estimates distances with a provably unbiased,
             asymptotically-optimal-error estimator via random rotation, so no raw-vector
             reranking is needed.
innovation:  RaBitQ replaces a trained codebook (PQ) with a SINGLE random orthogonal rotation P shared by every
             vector: normalize the residual-to-centroid o, then store only the sign bit of P^-1 o
             per dimension (the binary code x_bar is the nearest vertex of a randomly-rotated
             +/-1/sqrt(D) hypercube). Because the codebook is random, the cross-term <o_bar, q>
             yields an UNBIASED estimator of the true dot product, <o,q> ~= <o_bar,q>/<o_bar,o>,
             with sharp O(1/sqrt(D)) error -- asymptotically optimal (matches Alon-Klartag
             FOCS17). This means 1 bit/dim gives reliable enough distances and confidence
             intervals to skip touching the original float vectors entirely, which is the
             compression-without-recall-collapse that ordinary binary quantization cannot achieve.
techniques:
  - 1-bit-per-dimension sign quantization of randomly-rotated residual: store sign(P^-1(o-c)) as a
    D-bit string, total per-vector = D bits + 2 f32 factors
  - Shared random orthogonal rotation P (Johnson-Lindenstrauss transform) -- no per-corpus
    codebook training, fast variant uses Fast Hadamard Transform instead of dense matmul
  - Two stored scalar factors per vector: <o_bar,o> (code-to-data inner product, the unbiasedness
    normalizer) and ||o-c|| (residual norm to centroid)
  - Unbiased distance estimator <o,q> ~= <o_bar,q>/<o_bar,o> with provable O(1/sqrt(D)) error
    bound enabling rerank-free retrieval
  - Asymmetric bit budget: data side stays 1 bit/dim, query is rotated then scalar-quantized to
    B_q bits (e.g. 4) and decomposed into B_q bit-planes
  - Bitwise distance kernel: per query bit-plane, AND with the code then popcount, summed with
    plane weights -- 3x faster than PQ LUT scan on CPU, maps to GPU as AND+__popc
  - FastScan-style layout packing 32 binary codes per batch with 4-bit quantized 8-bit LUT entries
    for SIMD/warp-parallel filtering
  - Two-stage filter-then-refine: cheap 1-bit code filters candidates, 'ex-code' (extra low bits)
    refines survivors; extended B-bit RaBitQ (Sep-2024) for multi-bit budgets
nest steals: Add a new embeddings codec in nest-format/src/encoding/ as `rabitq.rs` (encoding id 4 -- currently
             only 0..3 are used; 4..255 are RESERVED within format v1, so this is purely additive,
             no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump). Layout mirrors int8.rs: a u32 payload_version, a single
             shared D*D rotation seed (deterministic, store the seed not the matrix to stay
             reproducible and tiny), then per vector a packed D-bit sign code plus two f32 factors
             (<o_bar,o>, ||o-c||) and the global centroid c once. This is a drop-in replacement
             for the int8 path in the `tiny` preset, taking embedding storage from ~1 byte/dim
             (int8) to ~1 bit/dim -- roughly an 8x shrink of the embeddings section, which is the
             dominant section in compressed presets, directly advancing the 'compress more than
             everyone' north star (tiny's 0.283 ratio could drop materially). In
             nest-runtime/src/simd/, add a popcount-based RaBitQ kernel beside scalar/avx2/neon
             (AND + popcount over query bit-planes; NEON vcnt, AVX2 popcount, scalar count_ones
             fallback) for the cheap ANN filter stage. Critically, nest's search contract is
             PRESERVED for free: the mandatory exact-cosine rerank already re-scores survivors
             from the mmapped vectors, so RaBitQ becomes the ANN-proxy filter and the contract's
             exact rerank guarantees real cosine -- RaBitQ's own error bound just makes the
             candidate set smaller/cleaner. Determinism holds because the rotation is seeded and
             sign() is exact.
compression: Directly central. RaBitQ is the single best-known route to push nest's embeddings section from
             int8 (8 bits/dim) to ~1 bit/dim WITHOUT recall collapse, because its rotation-based
             estimator is unbiased with an optimal error bound. Embeddings dominate
             compressed-preset file size, so an ~8x cut there is the largest available lever on
             nest's headline compression ratio while the mandatory exact-cosine rerank keeps
             returned scores ground-truth.
perf:        Data side is D/8 bytes per vector (e.g. 1536-dim -> 192 bytes) plus 2 f32 factors and one shared
             seed; no per-vector codebook. Distance filter is bitwise AND + popcount over B_q
             query bit-planes (B_q typically 4), which is branch-free and SIMD/SIMT friendly: maps
             to AVX2/NEON popcount on nest's x86/aarch64 dispatch and to __popc on GPU. The cuVS
             paper reports >2.7x throughput over IVF-PQ (no raw rerank) and ~2.2x QPS over CAGRA
             at recall ~0.95 with 7.7x faster index build, quantizing >1M 960-dim vectors/sec to
             8-bit codes on GPU. For nest's CPU-only mmap runtime, the win is memory traffic:
             1-bit codes mean ~8x fewer bytes touched per scanned vector than int8, and popcount
             throughput easily saturates the hot path.
license:     cuVS is Apache-2.0 (RAPIDS/NVIDIA); this fork inherits Apache-2.0. The reference RaBitQ
             algorithm/library from VectorDB-NTU is MIT. Algorithm itself is patent-free per
             authors. Clean for vendoring/reimplementation in nest.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] bitsandbytes-foundation/bitsandbytes
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A CUDA/C++/PyTorch library for k-bit weight quantization whose transferable core is NF4 (a fixed
             16-entry quantile codebook information-theoretically optimal for normally distributed
             data), blockwise absmax scaling, double/nested quantization of the scales, and a
             dynamic-tree 8-bit data type with per-value exponent/fraction allocation.
innovation:  Codebook quantization driven by the data's own distribution: instead of uniform int8 (which wastes
             code points on empty bins), NF4 places its 2^k reconstruction levels at the quantiles
             of a standard normal so each level is used equally often (minimum-entropy / Lloyd-Max
             optimal). Stored values are just k-bit indices into a tiny fixed lookup table;
             reconstruction is index -> codebook[index] * blockwise_absmax. This is strictly
             better than nest's current uniform int8 for L2-normalized embeddings, whose
             per-dimension components are approximately zero-mean Gaussian.
techniques:
  - NF4 NormalFloat: hardcoded 16-level codebook at quantiles of N(0,1) (-1.0, -0.6962, -0.5251,
    ... 0.0 ... 0.7230, 1.0), store 4-bit index per value
  - Blockwise absmax scaling: split vector into blocks (default 64), normalize each block by its
    max-abs into [-1,1], store one scale per block to bound error and absorb outliers
  - Double/nested quantization: quantize the fp32 block scales themselves (groups of 256) to
    8-bit, reclaiming ~0.4 bits/parameter of scale overhead
  - Dynamic tree 8-bit: per-value variable exponent/fraction split via sign bit + run-of-zeros
    exponent + indicator '1' bit + linear fraction, low relative error across magnitudes
  - Quantile quantization (estimate_quantiles): build a data-adaptive codebook from the empirical
    CDF so each code point holds equal mass (equal-frequency binning)
  - Separation of storage dtype (4-bit index) from compute dtype (fp16/fp32): dequantize on the
    hot path only, keep the dot product in float
  - k-bit generalization: same codebook+absmax machinery parameterized to 8/4/lower bits, one
    codec family spanning tiny->exact presets
nest steals: Add codebook-based vector codecs to nest-format's encoding layer (crates/nest-format/src/encoding)
             as new encoding ids in the additive v1 RESERVED space (ids 4-255 free): e.g. id 4 =
             nf4-blockwise, id 5 = quantile-codebook-int8. The embeddings section keeps its
             mmap-friendly fixed-stride layout but each vector becomes [block_scales | packed
             k-bit indices], with a single shared 16- or 256-entry f32 codebook stored once in the
             section header. Determinism holds: the codebook is either the fixed NF4 constants or
             computed by a seeded/sorted quantile estimate over the corpus, so same chunks =>
             byte-identical file. nest-runtime's simd dispatcher (crates/nest-runtime simd module)
             gains a dequantize-and-dot kernel: AVX2 vpermps / NEON vqtbl gather from the LUT into
             f32 lanes, multiply by block absmax, then the existing SIMD dot, preserving the
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank (ANN proxy never returned). Double-quantizing the block
             scales shrinks per-vector scale overhead further. Unlocks a new preset below 'tiny':
             nf4 embeddings at ~0.5 bytes/dim vs int8's 1 byte/dim, roughly halving the dominant
             embeddings section while staying offline, single-file, deterministic,
             exact-on-rerank.
compression: Directly central. Embeddings are the largest section and the one nest deliberately leaves
             uncompressed (zstd banned on the hot path). NF4/quantile codebooks attack exactly
             that: ~4 bits/dim instead of int8's 8 or f32's 32, near-lossless recall because the
             codebook matches the Gaussian shape of normalized embedding components. Blockwise
             absmax + double quantization push bytes/dim lower while bounding error, and the LUT
             dequant is cheap enough to run inline before the SIMD dot, serving straight from
             mmap. Most credible path to beating int8/'tiny' on the 'compress more than everyone'
             north star for the vector payload.
perf:        Reference kernels are CUDA; nest must reimplement in pure Rust SIMD. Dequant is a 16-entry LUT
             gather (fits in registers/L1) plus one fp32 multiply by block scale before the dot.
             AVX2: shuffle/permute-based 16-entry LUT (_mm256_permutevar8x32_ps / vpermps for 8
             f32 lanes); NEON: vqtbl byte-table lookup of indices then convert. Packing 4-bit
             indices is 2 values/byte, halving hot-path memory bandwidth vs int8, which often
             makes dequant effectively free relative to bandwidth saved. Block size 64 keeps
             scales in cache; double-quant of scales is a second tiny LUT.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] intel/neural-compressor
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A multi-backend (PyTorch/ONNX/TF) post-training quantization toolkit whose LLM core implements
             weight-only quantization (RTN, GPTQ, AWQ, AutoRound/SignRound) and microscaling
             block-float formats (MXFP4/NVFP4, NF4/FP4) at 1-8 bits with group-wise scales and
             zero-points.
innovation:  Block/group-wise affine quantization with a shared per-group scale (and optional zero-point) where
             the scale maps the group amax to the format max, combined with learned rounding
             direction (AutoRound SignSGD) and microscaling block-float element formats (MXFP4
             E2M1+E8M0, NVFP4 E2M1+E4M3+FP32) to hit near-lossless 4-bit.
techniques:
  - RTN group-wise affine quant: per-group scale=amax/qmax, sym (signed, no zero-point) vs asym
    (unsigned + zero-point) schemes, group_size from 32/128 down to per-channel(-1)
  - AutoRound/SignRound: SignSGD optimization of per-weight rounding offset v in [-0.5,0.5] plus
    learned clip scales alpha/beta over ~200 steps, block-wise output reconstruction, no inference
    overhead
  - GPTQ: Hessian-based (inverse activation covariance) error-compensated quantization,
    column-by-column with percdamp dampening and actorder reordering
  - AWQ: protect ~1% salient channels (chosen by activation magnitude) via per-channel scaling
    before quant
  - MXFP4 microscaling: E2M1 4-bit element (values +/-{0,.5,1,1.5,2,3,4,6}) + shared E8M0 8-bit
    power-of-two scale per 32-element block (decode = pure exponent add, no multiply)
  - NVFP4 two-level scaling: E4M3 FP8 scale per 16 elements + per-tensor FP32, finer granularity,
    amax encoded near-FP8 precision
  - NF4 non-uniform codebook: information-theoretically optimal 4-bit levels for
    normally-distributed weights (QLoRA), plus FP4
  - Double quantization: quantize the scales themselves (fp16->fp8->int4) to amortize per-group
    scale overhead; layer-wise quant to stay within memory
nest steals: Adapt block/group-wise quantization to nest's embeddings section codec in nest-format
             (crates/nest-format/src/encoding.rs + sections.rs). Today nest has only flat int8 and
             float16 for embeddings. Add a new additive encoding id (4-255 reserved) for
             grouped/microscaled vectors: split each embedding vector into fixed groups of 16 or
             32 dims sharing one scale (mirroring MXFP4/NVFP4), giving int4-equivalent footprint
             at far better recall than per-vector int8 because the scale adapts to local dynamic
             range. Two concrete codecs worth adding: (1) group-int8 with per-group fp16 scale
             (asym), and (2) an MXFP4-style block-float (E2M1 nibble-packed, one E8M0 byte scale
             per 32 dims) for a ~4x size cut over f32. Critically, the E8M0 power-of-two scale
             means decode is an exponent-add not a multiply, so the nest-runtime SIMD dot-product
             hot path (crates/nest-runtime simd dispatcher, AVX2/NEON) can dequant-on-the-fly
             cheaply during the mandatory exact-cosine rerank without breaking the
             score-straight-off-mmap design. AutoRound's learned rounding is a build-time (python,
             nest builder) one-shot pass that picks better int4/fp4 rounding per dimension to
             recover recall lost at 4 bits; it adds zero runtime cost and keeps determinism
             (seeded, fixed 200 steps). Store per-group scales contiguously after the packed codes
             so layout stays repr(C), little-endian, and reproducible.
compression: Directly central to nest's north star of compressing embeddings harder than anyone.
             Group/block-wise scaling is the proven path from int8 (0.5x) to int4-equivalent
             (~0.25x) without the recall collapse of naive flat int4, because each group of 16-32
             dims gets its own scale matching local amax. MXFP4-style E8M0 power-of-two scales
             keep dequant cheap enough to survive nest's mmap-and-score hot path, and AutoRound
             recovers near-lossless accuracy at 4 bits as a free build-time step, advancing nest
             past its current tiny preset (0.283 ratio, recall 0.992) toward a sub-0.2 ratio
             preset while preserving the exact-cosine rerank guarantee.
perf:        Group sizes 32/128 are the sweet spot in INC; -1 is per-channel. MXFP4 block=32 single
             power-of-two scale (decode via exponent add, shift-friendly, no FMA); NVFP4 block=16
             two-level (needs real multiplies but tighter error). Int4 codes are nibble-packed
             two-per-byte. AutoRound quantizes a 72B model in ~37min on one A100 (build-time only,
             zero inference overhead). INC validated 20+ LLMs at 4-bit WOQ reaching ~99% of FP32
             accuracy. For nest, the hot-path cost is one extra scale fetch + exponent-add per
             group of dims inside the existing SIMD dot kernel; nibble-unpack is a shift+mask,
             vectorizable on AVX2/NEON.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] vllm-project/llm-compressor (+ companion format repo github.com/vllm-project/compressed-tensors)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A post-training quantization toolkit that compresses LLM weights to int4/int8/fp8 and microscale
             fp4 using calibration-driven, Hessian-aware algorithms (GPTQ, AWQ, SmoothQuant),
             serializing the result in `compressed-tensors`, a safetensors extension that
             bit-packs sub-byte values with per-group scales/zero-points.
innovation:  GPTQ-style error-compensated quantization: instead of rounding each vector dimension independently
             (nest's current naive int8), quantize columns left-to-right and redistribute each
             value's rounding error onto the not-yet-quantized dimensions, weighted by the inverse
             Hessian (H^-1 = (X^T X + lambda*I)^-1) of the per-dimension covariance computed once
             over a calibration set of embeddings. Because all rows share the same Hessian, the
             inverse is precomputed once via a Cholesky factorization and reused, with lazy block
             updates for cache locality. This recovers far more accuracy at a given bit-width than
             round-to-nearest, letting you push embeddings to ~4 bits while keeping cosine recall
             high.
techniques:
  - GPTQ: OBS/OBQ-derived inverse-Hessian error compensation, fixed column order, one-time
    Cholesky factorization of (X^T X + 0.01*diag damping), lazy block updates (blocksize ~128)
  - ActOrder: reorder dimensions by sensitivity (largest Hessian diagonal first) so high-impact
    dims are quantized while more dims remain to absorb error
  - Group-wise scales: one scale/zero-point per contiguous group of 128 values, ~0.125 bits/value
    overhead but captures local distribution -> big win vs per-tensor
  - pack-quantized codec: logical int4 stored as int8 then packed 8-per-int32, with separate
    weight_scale (bf16) and weight_shape side tensors
  - NVFP4 microscale: 4-bit E2M1 elements, shared E4M3 fp8 scale per 16-value block, plus one fp32
    per-tensor scale (two-level scaling)
  - MXFP4 microscale: 4-bit E2M1 elements with shared UE8M0 power-of-two scale per 32-value block
    (cheaper, lower quality than NVFP4)
  - SmoothQuant: migrate per-channel activation outliers into weights via a diagonal scaling
    transform so both quantize cleanly
  - safetensors-extension layout: quantized payload plus sibling scale/zeropoint/shape tensors
    named by convention, self-describing per-layer config
nest steals: Three concrete steals. (1) nest-format/encoding: add two additive encoding ids in the reserved
             4-255 range -- a `pack_int4` codec (8 logical int4 per int32, deterministic LE
             packing) and a `microfp4` codec (E2M1 elements + per-16 fp8 block scale + per-shard
             fp32 scale, NVFP4-style two-level), each storing scales in a side blob so the
             embeddings section stays a flat mmap-friendly array; nest-runtime SIMD dispatcher
             gets an unpack+dequant-to-f32 step before the AVX2/NEON dot product (or
             dequant-on-the-fly into the accumulator). This unlocks ~4x and ~8x smaller embedding
             sections vs f32, beating the current `tiny` int8 preset. (2) nest-python builder: add
             an offline GPTQ/group-wise calibration pass over corpus embeddings (compute X^T X
             once, Cholesky inverse, error-compensated round) producing quantized bytes; pure
             build-time, keeps the rust runtime offline and deterministic, and is where the real
             compression-ratio gains live. (3) Group-wise scales (group=128) become a tiny
             side-section feeding the rerank: since nest ALWAYS does an exact-cosine rerank, an
             approximate fp4/int4 ANN-stage score is acceptable because the mandatory rerank
             restores true cosine -- so aggressive quantization is risk-free for final scores.
             Determinism holds: seeded calibration, fixed column order, alphabetical/index group
             order; format stays additive (new encoding ids only, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump).
compression: Directly central to nest's 'compress more than everyone' north star: GPTQ error compensation +
             group scales let embeddings drop from int8 (nest's current floor) to ~4 bits at
             comparable recall, roughly halving the largest section again, and NVFP4/MXFP4 give a
             principled sub-byte float path. The mandatory exact-cosine rerank in nest's search
             contract is the perfect safety net that makes aggressive lossy embedding quantization
             safe for final-score fidelity.
perf:        Quantization cost is all build-time (Cholesky is O(d^3) in embedding dim d once, e.g.
             d=384/768/1024 is trivial; per-vector pass is O(d^2) within a block). Runtime cost is
             the unpack/dequant before SIMD: int4-into-int32 unpack is a few shifts+mask
             vectorizable under AVX2/NEON; NVFP4 needs a small E2M1 LUT + fp8 scale multiply.
             Memory: int4 group=128 adds ~0.125 bits/value; NVFP4 adds ~0.5 bits/value (fp8 scale
             per 16) plus negligible per-tensor fp32. Smaller embeddings also mean less mmap page
             traffic on the hot dot-product path, partly offsetting dequant overhead.
license:     Apache-2.0 (both llm-compressor and compressed-tensors) -- permissive, safe to vendor/learn
             algorithms from

# focus: vector databases and sqlite vector/columnar extensions
