--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] neo4j/neo4j
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A GPLv3 Java graph database whose storage engine stores nodes/relationships/properties as
             fixed-size records (legacy Record format) or hardware-page-aligned 128B/8KB
             co-located blocks (Block format), with a multi-root generational B+ tree (GBPTree)
             for dense overflow and a CSR in-memory graph (GDS) that compresses adjacency lists
             via sort + delta + variable-byte, or optionally block-based bit-packing ("integer
             packing").
innovation:  The adjacency-list compression pipeline for graph edges: sort target node IDs ascending,
             delta-encode consecutive IDs into small gaps, then bit-pack those gaps in fixed-size
             BLOCKS where each block uses only as many bits as its largest value needs ("integer
             packing") -- this decompresses faster than per-value varint (branchless,
             SIMD-friendly, cache-local) while compressing at least as well. The deeper principle:
             choosing bit width per-block by the local maximum beats both a global encoding and
             per-value continuation-bit varints on the speed/ratio Pareto front.
techniques:
  - sort + delta-encode adjacency targets so neighbor IDs become small monotone gaps before any
    entropy step
  - variable-byte (varint) encoding: 7 data bits + 1 continuation bit per byte (the slow baseline)
  - integer packing: block-of-N values bit-packed to ceil(log2(max_in_block)) bits, branchless
    unpack for cache locality
  - GBPTree: multi-root generational copy-on-write B+ tree giving lock-free readers via generation
    pointers (one root per dense node)
  - Block format co-location: node + labels + ~6-7 properties + first ~5 relationships packed into
    one 128B/8KB block aligned to NVMe/OS page size to cut IOPS and cache misses
  - inline short-string/short-array storage to avoid out-of-line block-store pointer chasing
  - CSR layout: offsets (HugeLongArray) + degrees (HugeIntArray) + compressed-target byte arrays
    as a flat columnar adjacency representation
nest steals: Add an optional section to nest-format (a reserved 0x09+ section id, additive within v1) named
             e.g. graph_adjacency, encoding a CSR: a u64 offsets array + per-node degree + a
             packed-targets blob where chunk-to-chunk edges (citation links, semantic-neighbor
             graph, hnsw neighbor lists) are stored sort+delta+block-bit-packed. nest-runtime
             gains a graph traversal path beside ann::HnswIndex (e.g. graph::CsrIndex) that mmaps
             offsets and unpacks blocks branchlessly, reusing the SIMD dispatcher pattern.
             Critically, the SAME block-bit-packing codec belongs in nest-format/encoding as a new
             integer-stream codec (encoding id in the reserved 4-255 space) to losslessly shrink
             the existing chunk_ids and chunks_original_spans sections (monotone u64 offsets/spans
             are ideal delta+pack candidates) and to compress neighbor-id lists inside
             hnsw_index(0x07) -- advancing the compress-more-than-everyone goal without touching
             embeddings on the hot path. Determinism holds: sort gives canonical order, block size
             is fixed, no entropy-coder dictionary state. Keep the codec file under 300 loc,
             repr(C), LE-unsigned, thiserror-typed.
compression: Directly on-mission. Delta+block-bit-packing is the canonical lossless codec for monotone integer
             streams, which nest already has in abundance: chunk_ids, chunks_original_spans (byte
             offset/length pairs), and HNSW neighbor lists. These are currently raw or zstd'd; a
             dedicated sort/delta/packed integer codec beats zstd on structured monotone integers
             at far lower decode cost, and unlike zstd it can decode a single value by block off
             mmap without inflating the whole stream. It also enables compressed on-disk graph
             search (a stated north-star feature) via CSR rather than a heavyweight adjacency
             structure, advancing both compression and graph-search goals with one codec.
perf:        Block bit-packing (integer packing) decodes strictly faster than continuation-bit varint because
             it is branchless and cache-local (one block = one tight loop, no per-byte
             continuation test) at equal-or-better ratio; Neo4j reports it always wins on decode
             and usually on ratio. Block format reports ~40% faster traversal fully in-memory and
             ~70% when only 1/3 fits, via co-location plus 8KB NVMe/OS-page alignment cutting
             IOPS. Caution: Block format can use MORE disk than the tightly packed Record format
             due to padding/headers, so for size-first nest adopt the co-location/alignment idea
             selectively and take primarily the integer codec.
license:     GPLv3 (Community Edition); Enterprise features commercial. GPL is incompatible with nest's
             likely-permissive licensing, so learn-from-only: do not vendor neo4j source. The
             sort+delta+block-bit-packing technique is a public algorithm (also in Lucene
             PForDelta, Roaring, StreamVByte, FastPFOR) and can be cleanly reimplemented from
             spec.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] neo4j/graph-data-science
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A JVM graph-analytics library whose core asset is an immutable, in-memory Compressed-Sparse-Row
             (CSR) graph store that compresses each node's adjacency list with sort -> delta ->
             variable-length (VarLong) byte encoding, plus an optional block-based integer
             bit-packing strategy ("PackedAdjacencyList") tuned for branch-light, cache-local
             decompression during traversal.
innovation:  Lossless small-integer column compression via sort -> delta -> VarLong, plus block bit-packing at
             per-block minimum bit-width, decoded branch-free with cache locality on the hot path.
techniques:
  - sorted delta encoding
  - VarLong/LEB128 variable-length integers
  - CSR: offsets + degrees + paged compressed targets
  - block bit-packing at per-block minimum bit-width
  - tail strategies for partial blocks
  - HugeArray paging
nest steals: crates/nest-format/src/encoding: add an integer-stream codec module (delta+VarLong, optional block
             bit-pack) with a RESERVED v1 encoding id (4-255), applied first to the chunk_ids and
             chunks_original_spans sections; later, crates/nest-runtime/ann gets an on-mmap CSR
             adjacency index using the same codec with a branch-free unpack kernel in
             crates/nest-runtime/src/simd. Reimplement clean-room from the algorithm (GDS is
             GPL-3.0); determinism holds since all steps are pure functions of the sorted input.
compression: Directly on-mission. nest currently leans on zstd for non-embedding sections, but zstd is a
             general entropy coder that underperforms specialized integer codecs on dense
             monotonic ID/offset columns (chunk_ids, chunks_original_spans).
             Delta+VarLong+bitpacking is the standard way to push those columns toward 1-2
             bytes/value losslessly and decode at memcpy speed, advancing the
             compress-more-than-everyone goal while staying deterministic and mmap-friendly (no
             allocation, no entropy-decoder state). It also future-proofs the graph-search roadmap
             with a proven compact adjacency format.
perf:        Reported ~8.2 bytes/edge uncompressed vs ~1.4 bytes/edge after sort+delta+VarLong (~6x) on real
             graphs. Packed (bit-packed) variant: similar-or-better ratio than VarLong but
             strictly faster decompression due to branch-free unpack and better memory locality;
             ideal for traversal-heavy / tight inner loops. Block bit-packing is the same family
             as SIMD integer codecs (e.g. lnx-search/upack, FastPFor) so an AVX2/NEON unpack
             kernel in nest-runtime/simd is realistic. Decode requires zero allocation and no
             dictionary/window state, unlike zstd, so it is mmap-pure.
license:     GPL-3.0 (sources assemble into "OpenGDS"). LICENSE-INCOMPATIBLE with nest if nest is permissively
             licensed: do NOT copy code. The techniques (delta, VarLong/LEB128, block bit-packing,
             CSR) are decades-old public-domain algorithms, so reimplement from the algorithm, not
             from GDS source.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] dgraph-io/dgraph (with companion repos dgraph-io/sroar and dgraph-io/badger)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A distributed GraphQL/DQL graph database storing each predicate as a sorted posting list of 64-bit
             node UIDs, encoded as serialized roaring bitmaps (sroar) whose in-memory byte layout
             equals the on-disk layout, persisted in a WiscKey-style LSM+value-log KV store
             (Badger).
innovation:  sroar: a serialized roaring bitmap whose in-memory representation equals its on-disk
             representation (single []uint16 buffer), so an integer-id set / graph adjacency list
             is mmapped and set-operated with zero marshal/unmarshal -- compressed storage that is
             also directly operable, matching nest's no-decode-on-hot-path rule.
techniques:
  - Serialized roaring bitmap (sroar): single []uint16 buffer, zero-copy mmap, no
    marshal/unmarshal; keys section (header of node-size + key-count, then 48-bit-base-key ->
    container-offset pairs) followed by sequential containers
  - Two container types selected by density: array container (sorted uint16 list) below 4096
    cardinality, bitmap container (1024 uint64 = 8KB dense bitset) above it; run containers
    deliberately omitted to keep impl simple yet faster
  - 64-bit keyspace split: mask 0xFFFFFFFF0000 isolates the high 48 bits as the container base
    key, low 16 bits are the index within a container (uint16)
  - Per-container header words: [size, type, cardinality] then payload, so And/Or/AndNot iterate
    matching base keys and run containerAnd/containerOr directly on slices via pointer/reslice
    arithmetic (FastAnd/FastOr pre-size the destination from cardinality estimates)
  - Group-varint delta encoding (the predecessor scheme): split sorted id list into fixed 128-int
    blocks, store first value whole then deltas as varints (frame-of-reference); cheap but
    requires decode -> superseded by sroar
  - Posting list as graph primitive: predicate:entity -> sorted list of value-ids, so a graph join
    becomes a sorted-set intersection (bitmap AND) instead of an application-layer hash join
  - WiscKey key-value separation (Badger): small keys stay in the LSM tree, large values go to an
    append-only value log, cutting write amplification; SSTable blocks compressed with Snappy or
    Zstandard, bloom filters + block cache for reads
  - Mutations as WAL + in-memory overlay layers over an immutable base posting list (separate
    layers for replace vs add/delete) to avoid re-sorting the whole list on every write
nest steals: Two concrete steals. (1) Add a serialized-roaring-bitmap codec to nest-format (new RESERVED
             encoding id, e.g. 0x04, or a helper in encoding/) for any section that is a SORTED
             SET OF u32/u64 ids: chunk_ids, hnsw neighbor adjacency lists, and bm25 postings (term
             -> doc-id list in 0x08). Mirror sroar's contract exactly: the encoded bytes ARE the
             in-memory layout, so nest-runtime mmaps and runs set intersections (AND) with no
             decode, matching nest's "no decode on hot path" rule. Store containers little-endian
             repr(C) per nest constraints; determinism is free because roaring container choice is
             a pure function of the id set (array<4096 cardinality else dense bitmap). This
             unlocks (a) a graph_index section (0x09+) where each node's adjacency = a roaring
             bitmap, giving the "graph search" north-star item with compressed, mmap-scored
             traversal; and (b) much smaller bm25 postings than raw varint id lists, improving the
             tiny/hybrid presets' size ratio. (2) Adopt the array-vs-bitmap density switch as a
             general nest codec primitive in nest-format/encoding for sorted-id sections, and
             reuse the group-varint frame-of-reference (first value + 128-block deltas) as the
             int8-tier analog for monotonic id columns like chunks_original_spans offsets,
             shrinking provenance/span sections in compressed/tiny presets. Skip Badger/LSM
             entirely: it is a mutable multi-file server store and violates nest's
             single-file/offline/append-only invariants -- only the sroar byte-layout discipline
             and the posting-list=set-intersection model transfer.
compression: Directly on-mission. Roaring bitmaps are the state-of-the-art compressed representation for sorted
             integer-id sets, and sroar proves you can get that compression while keeping a
             zero-copy mmap layout (no decode tax), which is exactly nest's hot-path constraint.
             For nest this shrinks bm25 postings, enables a compact compressed graph adjacency
             section, and gives a reusable sorted-id codec -- advancing "compress data more than
             everyone" for the non-embedding sections without ever touching the directly-scored
             embedding bytes.
perf:        sroar benchmarks: ~15x less memory, ~25x fewer allocations, ~7x faster than the reference Go
             roaring library, largely because there is no encode/decode step. Integrated into
             Dgraph v21.12 (Zion) with posting-list cache off, >40% of a heavy production
             workload's queries became >=20x faster and >50% became >=10x faster. Array containers
             cap at 4096 cardinality before switching to an 8KB dense bitmap (1024 uint64); set
             operations are sorted-key merges over containers, so AND/OR are linear in container
             count and SIMD-friendly on the dense-bitmap path. Badger uses Snappy/Zstandard block
             compression plus bloom filters and a block cache; WiscKey separation trades read
             locality for far lower write amplification (not relevant to nest's read-only file).
license:     Dgraph core historically Apache-2.0 with portions under the Dgraph Community License / later
             relicensed (BSL-style) under Hypermode; sroar and badger are Apache-2.0. Treat
             sroar/badger as Apache-2.0 (permissive, attribution) -- safe to reimplement the
             byte-layout idea in nest's own Rust. Verify the current sroar LICENSE.md before
             vendoring any code; prefer a clean-room Rust reimplementation given nest's small-core
             and edition-2024 constraints.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] arangodb/arangodb (core innovation lives in arangodb/velocypack)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        ArangoDB is a multi-model (document + graph + search) database whose on-disk currency is
             VelocyPack (VPack): a single-type-byte, self-describing binary format with trailing
             offset-index tables that allow zero-copy O(1)/O(log n) random access into
             sub-documents without parsing the whole value, layered on a RocksDB LSM store.
innovation:  The trailing-offset-table layout enabling zero-copy random access into compactly-encoded
             structured values, with per-value smallest-width offset selection and an optional
             integer-key dictionary.
techniques:
  - Single-type-byte self-description: one leading byte encodes both type and size class
    (0x40-0xbe inline short strings up to 126 bytes, 0x30-0x39 single-byte small ints 0-9,
    0x20-0x27 width-tagged signed ints) so type+length read off the first byte(s)
  - Trailing index/offset table for O(1) array / O(log n) sorted-object random access without full
    parse
  - Smallest-width offset selection: pick 1/2/4/8-byte offsets based on max offset (<256, <64K,
    <4G, else) -- pay only for the addressing range actually needed
  - Compact types 0x13 (array) / 0x14 (object): drop the index table entirely and use LEB128-style
    varints (high bit = continuation) for bytelength + count, with the item count written
    backwards from the end for sequential-only/dump use cases
  - Object-key dictionary compression: two-pass collect+count of attribute names, replace repeated
    long keys with small integers via an O(1) hash table (e.g. 153KB->49KB, 20.8KB->9.2KB in their
    benches)
  - Sorted-by-bytewise-key object storage enabling binary search and deterministic byte layout
  - Contiguous single-block memory layout (zero-copy) -- sub-value pointers are just base+offset
nest steals: nest currently stores chunks_canonical(text) and a future json/yaml/parquet payload as opaque
             encoded blobs. Adapt VPack's trailing-offset-table layout as a new additive section
             codec in nest-format (sections.rs + encoding.rs) for structured payloads: a json/yaml
             document section laid out as [tag][smallest-width offsets][values] so nest-runtime
             can mmap it and resolve a sub-path (e.g. doc.field[3].name) with pure base+offset
             arithmetic and binary search, zero decode, matching the same hot-path discipline
             already used for SIMD dot products straight off mmap. Two pieces are immediately
             stealable under nest's constraints: (1) the smallest-width offset selection (1/2/4/8
             LE bytes) is a clean, deterministic, repr(C)-friendly encoding that fits the additive
             encoding-id space (ids 4-255 reserved in v1) and gives compact structured storage;
             (2) the object-key dictionary -- collect+count keys, emit a per-file key dictionary,
             replace keys with varints -- maps directly onto nest's content_hash determinism (sort
             keys bytewise => byte-identical output for identical input) and would massively
             shrink repeated-schema corpora like the PT-BR datasets and any parquet/jsonl ingest.
             The compact 0x13/0x14 varint mode is the right default for sequential/citation
             materialize paths where random access is not needed, squeezing the size ratio further
             toward the 'compress more than everyone' north star. All of this is offline,
             single-file, deterministic, and additive -- no v2 bump required.
compression: Directly central. VPack's key-dictionary + smallest-width offsets is a proven recipe for
             compressing structured/repeated-schema data (json, yaml, parquet rows, datasets)
             while STILL serving random sub-document access from raw mmap -- exactly nest's twin
             goals of extreme compression AND fast serve. It gives nest a principled structured
             codec that beats naive zstd-of-json on access cost (no decompress to read one field)
             and complements zstd: store the VPack-encoded structured payload, optionally zstd the
             non-hot parts. The standalone size wins are modest vs JSON (5-20%) but the
             key-dictionary path shows 2-3x reductions on repeated-schema data, and the zero-parse
             access is the real unlock for serving compressed datasets/whole-databases.
perf:        VPack is designed for in-kernel sub-document access used by indexes: type+length read from the
             first byte(s), values in one contiguous block, O(1) hash-table key->int translation.
             No SIMD in the format itself (it is a layout, not a kernel) -- it pairs well with
             nest's existing SIMD by keeping decoded fields byte-aligned and addressable.
             Index-table reads are random memory jumps; the compact 0x13/0x14 modes trade that
             random access for sequential scan to save the table bytes. Size benches: JSON->VPack
             typically 5-20% smaller; with key compression 153KB->49KB (~3.1x) and 20.8KB->9.2KB
             (~2.3x). Caveat: object-key dictionary needs a second pass over input (acceptable in
             nest's python build stage, not the rust serve path).
license:     Apache-2.0 (both arangodb/velocypack and arangodb/arangodb core; note ArangoDB server moved to BSL
             1.1 / community license in newer releases, but the velocypack format library and spec
             are Apache-2.0 and the on-disk format is documented/open)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] memgraph/memgraph
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        In-memory C++ property-graph engine using lock-free concurrent skip lists for vertices/edges,
             delta-chain MVCC (Hekaton-style snapshot isolation), full-snapshot + WAL durability
             with a section-offset binary file format, and a USearch/HNSW vector index using a
             single-store reference pattern.
innovation:  The PropertyStore "single-store reference + variable-length packed-metadata-byte" encoding: each
             property is prefixed by one metadata byte that bit-packs the value type tag, the
             byte-width of the property-key ID, and the byte-width-indicator of the payload, so
             integers/strings consume the smallest of 1/2/4/8 bytes and large values (like
             embedding vectors) are never duplicated in the row store but replaced by a small
             index-ID reference. A self-describing, additive, per-record variable-width codec that
             gives size wins without a separate schema and without a global dictionary pass.
techniques:
  - Packed metadata byte: bit-fields for {type tag, property-ID width,
    payload-size-width-indicator} in 1 byte before each value
  - Variable-length integer storage: ints in the minimum of 1/2/4/8 bytes that hold the value
    (LEB128-like family)
  - Name-to-ID mapper: intern property keys/labels to small integer IDs once, store IDs not
    strings per record
  - Single-store reference pattern: store a fat value (vector) only in its index backend; property
    store keeps just a u32/u64 index-ID reference
  - Snapshot/WAL layout: magic+version header, a section-offset table, then encoded-edges /
    encoded-vertices / indices / name-mapper / metadata / batch-info sections for seek-and-parse
    recovery
  - Batched + parallel snapshot writer/reader recorded in a batch-info section so write and
    recovery parallelize
  - Delta-chain MVCC: per-object delta linked list, visibility resolved at read time by
    transaction timestamp (snapshot isolation)
  - USearch HNSW with per-node fine-grained locking instead of one global mutex
nest steals: Steal the packed-metadata-byte + name-to-ID interning into nest-format. (1) In
             crates/nest-format/src/encoding add a 'varpack' codec for chunks/provenance/json-like
             sections: each scalar gets a 1-byte tag {type, len-width, int-width} so small ints
             and short strings shrink to 1-2 bytes vs fixed f32/u64 fields; additive (new encoding
             id in reserved 4-255), deterministic. (2) Add a name-to-ID mapper section (new
             reserved section id 0x09+) interning repeated provenance keys, model fingerprints,
             metadata field names to u16 IDs, dictionary stored once, IDs per record;
             deterministic via alphabetical key order. (3) In nest-runtime apply the single-store
             reference pattern: bm25/hnsw sidecars reference chunk_ids by small interned IDs not
             re-embedded strings. (4) Adopt the section-offset table + batch-info idea for the
             nest-format manifest/reader so large sections seek (and later mmap-parse in parallel)
             without scanning. All four respect single-file/offline/deterministic/additive and fit
             the <=300-line codec-per-file rule.
compression: On-mission for "compress more than everyone" on NON-embedding sections
             (text/json/yaml/provenance/metadata) which nest currently treats as zstd-only.
             Packed-metadata-byte + varint + name interning is how to shrink small-integer-heavy
             and repeated-key-heavy structured payloads (provenance, spans, json/parquet datasets
             nest wants to ingest) before zstd, often beating generic zstd on structured data by
             removing per-record type overhead and key-string repetition. mmap-friendly,
             decode-cheap, does not touch the hot scoring path (embeddings stay raw/f16/int8).
             Single-store references also stop duplicating large blobs across index + payload,
             shrinking hybrid/tiny presets.
perf:        In-memory engine targets sub-millisecond traversals; skip lists give O(log n) lock-free reads,
             coordinated writes. Per-object RAM cost is high (Edge >=88B incl 56B Delta, Vertex
             >=136B) which is the opposite of nest's goal, so steal the on-disk encoding ideas NOT
             the in-RAM skip-list/delta layout. Snapshot write/recovery are batched and
             parallelized via a batch-info section. PropertyStore zlib compresses whole per-vertex
             property blobs (nest should keep zstd, applied AFTER varpack). USearch HNSW uses
             per-node fine-grained locks; nest's HNSW is single-threaded deterministic build, so
             reference-only.
license:     Business Source License 1.1 (BSL 1.1; converts to Apache 2.0 after a time window). Core is
             source-available, not OSI-open: treat as LEARN-FROM-ONLY, do not vendor/copy source
             into nest. The ideas (varint metadata byte, name interning, section-offset layout)
             are standard techniques and safe to reimplement cleanly.

[HIGH] vesoft-inc/nebula
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        NebulaGraph is a distributed C++ property-graph database that translates graph operations into
             RocksDB key-value scans using a custom prefix-locality key encoding where a vertex
             and its out-edges share a sorted key prefix, plus bidirectional (out/in) edge
             materialization and fixed-offset schema-versioned property values.
innovation:  see core_innovation field
techniques:
  - prefix-locality key encoding
nest steals: Add an optional graph adjacency section to nest-format (reserve a v1 section id 0x09,
             additive-only) holding HNSW/k-NN edges as a CSR-style structure: a sorted offsets
             array indexed by chunk ordinal plus a flat neighbor-id array, mirroring Nebula's
             prefix-locality so one node's neighbors are a single contiguous mmap slice (no
             per-node random reads). nest-runtime/ann already builds the HNSW graph (build.rs,
             select_neighbors.rs) holding neighbor lists in memory; serialize those lists in this
             contiguous layout via nest-format/sections so the runtime mmaps adjacency straight
             off disk and walks it with zero allocation, matching the existing 'score directly
             from mmap' discipline. Borrow the Rank-as-sort-key trick to store the float16/int8
             edge distance INLINE next to each neighbor id (delta-sortable), letting hnsw
             expansion skip a separate distance lookup. Borrow the sign-encoded-direction byte to
             fold bidirectional/typed edges (e.g. bm25 term-postings vs hnsw vector edges) into
             one section without a second container, keeping single-file. Compression angle:
             neighbor-id arrays are monotone-ish within a node, so delta + varint/zigzag encode
             them in nest-format/encoding (new codec id 4-255, reserved-range, never on the
             embedding hot path) -- adjacency is a NON-embedding section so zstd over delta-coded
             ids is fair game and shrinks the graph index materially, advancing the 'compress more
             than everyone' north star while keeping graph traversal as cheap sequential mmap
             reads. Schema-version-byte idea maps to nest's manifest: a per-section format byte
             lets the adjacency codec evolve additively without a true v2 bump.
compression: Direct: graph adjacency (HNSW edges, future graph-search index) is a non-embedding section, so
             delta+zigzag+varint over monotone neighbor ids then zstd compresses it hard while CSR
             prefix-locality keeps traversal as O(1) contiguous mmap reads. Co-locating
             fixed-offset distances inline avoids a parallel array. This is the cheapest path to
             add 'graph search' to nest's mission without inflating the .nest file or breaking the
             offline/single-file/mmap guarantees.
perf:        Nebula's design makes 'get neighbors' a single RocksDB prefix range scan rather than N point
             lookups -- the same asymptotic win nest gets from a CSR adjacency slice over mmap
             (sequential, cache-friendly, prefetchable, zero alloc). Bidirectional dual-write
             trades ~2x edge storage for O(scan) reverse traversal; nest can choose
             single-direction for vector graphs (HNSW is undirected-symmetric anyway, so store
             once) and reserve dual-write only for typed/directed graph-search edges. Fixed-offset
             property decode = O(1) field access, the analog of nest reading f32/f16/int8 lanes at
             known strides off mmap.
license:     Apache-2.0 (with Common Clause on some Nebula components historically; core is Apache-2.0) --
             ideas/format-design are not copyrightable, only reference for design, no code
             vendored
