--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] QwenLM/Qwen3-Embedding
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A family of decoder-only LLM-based text embedding/reranker models (0.6B/4B/8B, dims
             1024/2560/4096) using causal last-token (EOS) pooling, instruction-prefix
             conditioning, and Matryoshka Representation Learning so a single vector can be
             truncated to as few as 32 dims and re-normalized with minimal retrieval loss.
innovation:  Matryoshka Representation Learning makes embedding dimensionality a near-lossless, deterministic
             compression knob via nested-prefix-trained vectors that stay valid under
             truncate-then-renormalize.
techniques:
  - Matryoshka Representation Learning: nested-prefix loss front-loads importance so vec[:d] for d
    in {32,64,128,256,512,768,1024} is independently usable
  - Truncate-then-renormalize: slice the first d dims then L2-normalize so dot product == cosine
    remains exact on the prefix
  - Last-token (EOS) causal pooling: final hidden state aggregates the whole sequence under the
    causal mask (no [CLS]), with left/right padding handling
  - Instruction-prefix conditioning: 'Instruct: {task}\nQuery: {q}' template steers the same
    weights per task, +1-5% with no retraining
  - L2-normalized output vectors as the contract so similarity is a plain dot product
  - Per-model fixed full dim (1024/2560/4096) advertised as the MRL ceiling
nest steals: nest-format: add an MRL-aware dimension-truncation codec/manifest field on the embeddings section.
             The Python builder (nest-python) records the model's full dim plus a chosen mrl_dim
             (e.g. 256 of 1024); it truncates each vector to the prefix and re-L2-normalizes
             before writing, storing mrl_dim and full_dim in the embeddings section header /
             search_contract. nest-runtime's SIMD dot-product kernels already score raw
             f32/f16/int8 straight from mmap, so they need only honor the smaller per-vector
             stride from the manifest; the exact-cosine rerank still returns true cosine because
             vectors are renormalized on the truncated prefix. This stacks multiplicatively with
             the existing tiny preset (int8): a 256-of-1024 MRL prefix at int8 is 4x (dims) * 4x
             (int8 vs f32) = ~16x smaller embeddings than f32-1024, while exact rerank guarantees
             the returned score is real cosine on the stored prefix. Add a CLI flag (nest-cli
             build/stats) to surface mrl_dim and the size/recall tradeoff. Determinism is
             preserved: truncation is a pure deterministic slice, so same chunks + same model
             fingerprint + same mrl_dim => byte-identical file. Stays additive (new manifest field
             + reserved encoding id), single-file, offline.
compression: Directly central to the 'compress more than everyone' north star. MRL adds a compression axis nest
             does not yet exploit: the embedding-dimension axis, independent of and multiplicative
             with the value-precision axis (int8/f16) nest already has. Qwen reports truncating
             4096->1024 keeps ~95% retrieval while cutting storage 75%; for nest's tiny preset, a
             256-of-1024 int8 layout could roughly quarter the dominant on-disk cost (embeddings
             are usually the largest section) at small recall cost, and because the format
             mandates exact-cosine rerank, the headline score stays honest. It also shrinks the
             HNSW index footprint since graph vectors are shorter.
perf:        Truncation is a contiguous prefix slice, so per-vector memory stride shrinks linearly with mrl_dim
             and the existing AVX2/NEON dot-product kernels run unchanged on the shorter stride
             (fewer SIMD lanes per vector => proportionally faster scoring and smaller mmap
             working set). No new entropy coder or transform needed; cost is one L2 renorm per
             vector at build time only. Shorter vectors also reduce HNSW distance-eval cost and
             index size. The models themselves are heavy (0.6B params, BF16, 32K ctx, RoPE), but
             nest only consumes their float outputs, so the model weight is irrelevant to the
             runtime; only the build-time embedder cares.
license:     Apache-2.0 (Qwen3-Embedding model weights and repo)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] facebookresearch/fastText
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A C++ library for linear-bag-of-words text classification and subword word embeddings whose real
             gem for nest is its model-compression pipeline (fastText.zip): product quantization
             of the embedding matrix plus greedy coverage-based feature pruning, shrinking 400MB+
             models to single-digit MB with near-zero accuracy loss.
innovation:  Product Quantization of the embedding matrix: subvector k-means with 256 centroids (1-byte codes)
             plus decoupled scalar-quantized norms and asymmetric lookup-table scoring, enabling
             sub-byte-per-dimension storage scored without full decode.
techniques:
  - Product quantization: per-subvector-position k-means (k=256 -> 8-bit codes), dsub=2 subvector
    dim, decouples norm (qnorm: scalar-quantize the L2 norm separately from the direction codes)
  - Greedy coverage-based feature pruning: keep the n-gram with highest L2 norm that covers each
    training doc, then top up by norm until a target feature budget is hit - retains only
    high-information rows before quantizing
  - Asymmetric distance computation: query stays full-precision; build a per-subvector lookup
    table of query-vs-256-centroids dot products, then scoring a code is m table lookups + adds
    (no decode)
  - Character n-gram subwords (3-6 chars, with < > word-boundary markers) hashed into a fixed
    bucket array for OOV-robust representations
  - FNV-1a hashing trick: n-grams mapped via h=2166136261, XOR+multiply by 16777619, mod
    bucket(=2M) into a fixed-size row table; n-gram rows placed after word rows to avoid
    word/ngram index collision
  - Hierarchical softmax over a Huffman tree of labels (frequent labels get shorter codes) for
    O(log k) classification
  - repr-stable little-endian binary model format (dictionary + hyperparams + quantized matrices)
    loadable by mmap
nest steals: Add a new embeddings codec encoding=4 ('pq') in crates/nest-format/src/encoding/ (new pq.rs
             sibling to int8.rs, registered in encoding/mod.rs and writer/encoding_choice.rs)
             using a reserved id in the 4-255 additive range - no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump.
             On-disk layout mirrors int8.rs prefix conventions: u32 payload_version, u32 dsub, the
             k-means codebook (m_subspaces * 256 * dsub f32 centroids, deterministic via seeded
             k-means to honor the reproducible-build constraint and alphabetical/seeded
             tie-breaks), per-vector scalar norm (reuse int8 norm machinery), then n*(dim/dsub) u8
             centroid-index codes. In crates/nest-runtime the SIMD dispatcher gains a PQ scoring
             path: build the asymmetric lookup table (query dotted against the mmapped codebook
             with the existing AVX2/NEON/scalar dot kernels) once per query, then score every
             candidate as dim/dsub byte-indexed table lookups + adds straight off mmap - then feed
             into the MANDATORY exact-cosine rerank path so the returned score stays true cosine
             (PQ is only the ANN coarse filter, never the final score, exactly matching nest's
             search_contract). This makes a new 'nano' preset below tiny: at dsub=2 the embeddings
             section is ~half of int8 (approaching 0.14 size ratio territory) directly advancing
             'compress more than everyone' while staying single-file/offline/deterministic.
             Optionally port the greedy coverage pruning into the python builder
             (nest-python/build path) to drop low-norm chunks/rows before encoding.
compression: Directly central. PQ is THE classic technique for pushing vector storage below 1 byte/dimension -
             it is what nest's current int8 codec (1 byte/dim, 4x) leaves on the table. At
             dsub=2,k=256 PQ stores 1 byte per 2 dims (8x base, ~16x vs f32 on the embedding
             bytes) with recall recoverable via the mandatory exact rerank. fastText.zip
             demonstrated ~250x model shrink (427MB -> 1.7MB) with negligible accuracy loss,
             validating that PQ + norm-decoupling + pruning is the highest-leverage path to beat
             everyone on embedding compression.
perf:        PQ scoring is cache- and SIMD-friendly: build one query lookup table (m_subspaces x 256 f32) using
             nest's existing AVX2/NEON dot kernels against the mmapped codebook, then each
             candidate costs dim/dsub u8-indexed loads + f32 adds - no float decode on the hot
             path, fits nest's score-off-mmap model. Codebook (m*256*dsub*4 bytes, e.g.
             dim=384,dsub=2 -> ~600KB) is tiny, stays hot in L2. Caveat: k-means training is the
             only added build-time cost and must be seeded for byte-identical deterministic
             builds; keep it in python builder, not rust library.
license:     MIT License (facebookresearch/fastText)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] openai/CLIP
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        CLIP is a contrastively-trained dual-encoder (ViT/ResNet image tower + 12-layer Transformer text
             tower with byte-level BPE) that projects both modalities into one shared
             L2-normalized embedding space (embed_dim 512 for ViT-B/32) where cosine similarity,
             scaled by a learned temperature, is the native cross-modal metric.
innovation:  The unit-hypersphere embedding convention plus a single shared text/image space, which turns SIMD
             dot product into exact cosine and makes int8 quantization nearly lossless.
techniques:
  - L2 normalization to unit hypersphere so cosine(a,b) == dot(a,b) for unit vectors
  - Shared cross-modal embedding space: text and image projected to identical embed_dim (512), one
    index serves both
  - Byte-level BPE tokenizer with a 256-entry byte<->unicode bijection that skips
    whitespace/control bytes, vocab 49408, gzipped rank-ordered merges file
  - Regex pre-tokenization (contractions, unicode letter runs \p{L}+, single digits \p{N}, symbol
    runs) before applying merges
  - Per-word BPE merge cache keyed on input token to avoid recomputation
  - Learned temperature logit_scale = exp(log(1/0.07)) (~100x) as a stored scalar that rescales
    raw cosine into calibrated logits
  - End-of-text token position used as the pooled text feature (deterministic single-vector
    reduction)
nest steals: nest-format: document/enforce L2-normalized embeddings in search_contract + store optional
             temperature scalar in provenance (additive, content_hash-safe). nest-runtime: adopt
             byte-level BPE (byte<->unicode bijection + frozen ranked merges section) in
             bm25::Bm25Index to fix the CJK/Thai tokenizer gap deterministically. Unlocks: exact
             cosine via raw SIMD dot product, near-lossless int8 compression, and a path toward
             shared text+image vectors for the 'fast image similarity' north-star.
compression: Directly central to extreme compression: L2-normalized unit vectors are the precondition that
             makes int8 (and even lower-bit) embedding quantization nearly lossless because every
             component lies in [-1,1] with a globally bounded scale, so a single symmetric
             quantization scale per vector or per file suffices -- maximizing nest's compression
             ratio while keeping the int8 tiny preset's 0.992 recall. It also lets cosine == dot
             product, so no extra magnitude bytes ever need storing.
perf:        Inference is GPU/PyTorch-heavy and out of scope for nest's runtime (nest never runs models). The
             transferable cheap part is purely the post-encoder convention: a single L2 normalize
             (one pass) at build time, and a static byte-BPE lookup tokenizer with O(word_len^2)
             merges + per-word cache. Both are deterministic, allocation-light, and fit nest's
             offline mmap serving model. embed_dim 512 f32 = 2KB/vector raw, 512B at int8.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] google-research/bert
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        BERT is a bidirectional transformer encoder for contextual text embeddings, but its
             independently-reusable artifact is the WordPiece tokenizer: a greedy
             longest-match-first subword segmenter over an ordered string-to-id vocabulary,
             fronted by a CJK-aware basic tokenizer.
innovation:  Vocabulary-driven subword segmentation that is language-agnostic and OOV-robust: a fixed vocab of
             word pieces (with a "##" continuation marker) plus a deterministic
             greedy-longest-match loop turns any string, including unsegmented CJK/Thai, into a
             stable bounded-size token set without a whitespace assumption and without ever
             emitting an out-of-vocab token (worst case falls back to single chars / [UNK]). The
             CJK pre-pass that inserts spaces around every Han codepoint makes ideographic scripts
             tokenize per-character instead of collapsing into one giant token.
techniques:
  - WordPiece greedy longest-match-first: for each word, scan end index downward from full length,
    take longest substring (## prefixed when start>0) present in vocab, advance start, repeat;
    is_bad -> [UNK] if no prefix matches
  - Vocab as OrderedDict / sequential id map (token string -> u32 id) giving O(1) lookups plus a
    stable inverse id->token table
  - ## continuation prefix preserves word-boundary info while sharing subword pieces across words
  - BasicTokenizer pre-pass: NFD accent stripping, control-char removal, whitespace normalization,
    lowercasing, punctuation isolation (split both sides of every punct char)
  - _tokenize_chinese_chars: insert spaces around CJK codepoints (U+4E00-9FFF, U+3400-4DBF,
    extension blocks) so ideographs become single-char tokens despite absent whitespace
  - max_input_chars_per_word guard (default 200) to bound worst-case segmentation cost
  - Deterministic data-only design: entire tokenizer is a frozen vocab.txt plus a pure function,
    no model weights needed to run it
nest steals: Replace nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs (currently a word-split-on-non-alphanumeric splitter
             that, per nest's own known-gaps note, degrades on CJK/Thai because space-less scripts
             collapse into one dropped/huge token) with a two-stage pipeline mirroring BERT: (1) a
             basic-tokenizer pass that lowercases, NFD-strips accents (already partial for PT-BR),
             isolates punctuation, and inserts boundaries around CJK codepoint ranges so Han/Thai
             segment per-character; (2) an optional WordPiece-style greedy-longest-match over a
             small frozen vocab. The on-disk BM25 codec (bm25/codec.rs) needs ZERO format change:
             terms stay UTF-8 strings written length-prefixed and sorted alphabetically, so
             determinism and the additive-format constraint hold and section 0x08 layout is
             byte-identical. If a learned subword vocab is later shipped, store it as a new
             additive optional section in the 0x09+ reserved range (or fold into search_contract)
             so the runtime stays offline and builds stay reproducible. This unlocks correct
             multilingual lexical recall (CJK/Thai/Korean) for the hybrid preset's BM25-via-RRF
             leg, closing a named gap without touching the frozen header or the embedding hot
             path.
compression: Indirect. WordPiece is a vocabulary-substitution scheme yielding fewer, shared subword units than
             character or naive word tokenization, which shrinks the BM25 term dictionary and
             postings (fewer unique terms, shared pieces) -- classic dictionary coding. But it
             does not touch the embedding bytes that dominate .nest size, so it improves
             multilingual search quality far more than the compress-more-than-everyone north star.
             Treat the CJK-aware basic tokenizer as the must-have piece and WordPiece as a
             quality/size win for the lexical index only.
perf:        Pure CPU, allocation-light, single-pass per word; greedy match is O(word_len^2) worst case but
             bounded by max_input_chars_per_word and dominated by hashmap lookups. No SIMD needed;
             runs in the rust runtime with no socket/model dependency, preserving
             offline-by-construction. Memory cost is the frozen vocab hashmap (tens of KB to a few
             MB by vocab size), mmappable if stored as a section.
license:     Apache-2.0 (code). Subword vocabs are training-produced, but the greedy-match algorithm and CJK
             char-range logic are trivially reimplementable in clean-room Rust; only the algorithm
             is adapted, not the vocab.txt artifact.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] UKPLab/sentence-transformers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A PyTorch framework for dense/sparse/cross-encoder text+image embeddings whose most transferable
             assets are three post-hoc compression techniques: scalar(int8)/binary embedding
             quantization with float32 rescoring, Matryoshka (front-loaded truncatable
             dimensions), and Model2Vec static distillation (attention-free token lookup tables).
innovation:  Compress-then-rescore as a search contract: store embeddings in a lossily quantized form (binary
             1-bit or int8) for cheap candidate retrieval over Hamming/int8 distance, then rerank
             the small candidate set against a higher-fidelity query representation so the FINAL
             score is near-full-precision. This decouples on-disk storage cost from result
             quality, exactly nest's "ann/bm25 always run a mandatory exact cosine rerank"
             philosophy generalized to the codec layer.
techniques:
  - Binary quantization: sign-threshold normalized float32 at 0 -> 1 bit/dim, np.packbits into
    bytes (1024d -> 128 bytes, 32x), retrieve by popcount Hamming distance (~2 CPU cycles)
  - Scalar int8 quantization: per-dimension min/max calibration buckets map float32 -> int8
    (-128..127), 4x shrink, affine dequant for rescoring
  - Asymmetric rescore: fetch rescore_multiplier*top_k binary candidates, rescore with float32
    query vs stored quantized doc vectors; recovers ~96% (binary) / ~99% (int8) recall
  - Matryoshka (MRL): train so information is front-loaded; truncate 1024->512 (1.5% drop) or
    ->256 (0.6% drop) by slicing the prefix, no re-encode
  - Model2Vec distillation: forward each vocab token through teacher once, PCA the token-embedding
    matrix (denoises even w/o dim reduction), then SIF weight w=1e-3/(1e-3+proba)
  - Zipf-rank proxy: vocab is frequency-sorted so rank substitutes for corpus frequency ->
    IDF-like downweighting of stopword tokens with zero corpus access
  - Static inference: EmbeddingBag token lookup + mean pooling, no attention, ~100k sents/sec CPU,
    model ~30MB (potion-base-32M) down to 1.8MB (potion-base-2M)
  - Tiered store: binary for first-stage memory-resident search, int8 on disk for rescore (cited
    200GB->5GB RAM / 52GB disk)
nest steals: Three concrete steals. (1) nest-format encoding layer: add MRL-style truncation metadata to the
             embeddings section and search_contract so a 'matryoshka' preset stores only the front
             D dims (e.g. 256 of 768) deterministically, shrinking the embeddings section ~3x with
             ~0.6% recall loss; stacks on existing int8/f16 codecs for compounding compression.
             (2) nest-runtime search path: generalize the EXISTING mandatory exact-cosine rerank
             into a two-fidelity store -- keep an optional binary (1-bit packed, popcount-Hamming)
             shadow of embeddings as a new RESERVED-id codec for first-stage candidate gen, then
             rerank with the int8/f32 vectors already mmapped; binary Hamming is a tiny NEON/AVX2
             popcount kernel that fits the SIMD dispatcher and never touches Python. (3)
             nest-python build side: implement a Model2Vec-style static distiller (PCA-normalize +
             Zipf/SIF reweight via vocab rank) so nest ships a sub-30MB deterministic CPU encoder
             bundled offline, killing the ~300-500ms python boot for search-text and giving
             sovereign single-file embedding with no transformer download. int8 per-dim min/max
             calibration should be stored in provenance for reproducible, byte-identical dequant.
compression: Directly central. Binary quantization is 32x on the embeddings section (the part nest deliberately
             leaves uncompressed for hot-path scoring) -- the asymmetric rescore trick is the ONLY
             way to compress embeddings without breaking nest's "score is real cosine" guarantee,
             since the lossy form is used only for candidate generation. Matryoshka truncation
             gives a free orthogonal ~3x. With Model2Vec's PCA denoising they push the 'tiny'
             preset (currently 0.283 ratio, int8) materially lower while preserving recall@10
             ~0.99.
perf:        Binary Hamming = packed-bit XOR + popcount, ~2 CPU cycles/comparison, ideal for a small std::arch
             popcnt/vcnt SIMD kernel (scalar fallback trivial via u64::count_ones). int8 dot
             product is the existing nest path. Static encoder ~107k sents/sec single-thread CPU
             (397x all-mpnet), pure table lookup + mean, deterministic and reproducible -- aligns
             with nest's seeded/deterministic-build constraint. Model2Vec PCA-then-int8 reaches
             25% of original size with no quality loss, evidence quant+dim-reduction compose
             without recall collapse.
license:     Apache-2.0 (sentence-transformers); Model2Vec is MIT (MinishLab) -- both vendor-friendly for
             nest's build-side python and rust runtime.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] FlagOpen/FlagEmbedding (BGE / BGE-M3 / BGE-reranker)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A retrieval toolkit (MIT) whose flagship BGE-M3 model emits, from one shared XLM-RoBERTa encoder,
             three aligned representations per text -- a 1024-d dense vector, a learned sparse
             lexical-weight dict (ReLU over a linear head per token id, max-pooled on repeats),
             and ColBERT-style per-token multi-vectors -- combined at query time by a weighted
             hybrid score.
innovation:  Learned sparse lexical weights as a {token_id: float} dictionary that is a drop-in upgrade over
             BM25 term frequencies: w_t = ReLU(W_lex . H[i]) per contextual token, scored as
             s_lex(q,d) = sum over shared tokens of w_q,t * w_d,t. It is a sparse vector keyed by
             tokenizer vocab id (~250k dims, almost all zero) that is cheaper, more compressible,
             and more semantic than a raw text index, and it fuses with dense cosine via a fixed
             weighted sum -- exactly the hybrid contract nest already runs.
techniques:
  - Learned sparse lexical weights: w_t = ReLU(W_lex . H[i]) per token, max-pool weight on
    repeated token ids, drop special tokens (CLS/SEP)
  - Sparse score s_lex = sum_{t in q intersect d} w_q,t * w_d,t over two token-id->weight dicts
    (set-intersection sparse dot product / merge-join)
  - Storage as parallel ascending-sorted (token_id:u32, weight:f32) arrays per doc -- trivially
    int8/f16 quantizable and zstd-friendly via delta+varint over ascending ids
  - ColBERT late interaction: s_mul = (1/N) sum_i max_j (E_q[i] . E_d[j]^T), per-query-token
    MaxSim over row-normalized doc-token vectors
  - Weighted hybrid fusion: score = w1*dense + w2*sparse + w3*colbert (e.g. [0.4,0.2,0.4])
    alongside/instead of reciprocal-rank fusion
  - Self-knowledge distillation: combined hybrid score used as teacher to train each single mode
    (training-side; explains why fixed fusion weights are stable)
  - Matryoshka dimensionality + quantization knobs on the dense head for size-vs-recall tradeoffs
  - MCLS (multiple CLS tokens) to embed up to 8192-token long inputs without long-text fine-tuning
nest steals: nest-format: add an OPTIONAL learned-sparse section in the reserved id space (0x09+, additive
             within v1) storing per-chunk ascending (token_id:u32, weight) pairs; quantize weights
             to int8/f16 and zstd the section (it is scored by integer merge-join, not a SIMD dot
             off mmap, so zstd is safe here unlike embeddings). nest-runtime/src/bm25: generalize
             the existing Bm25Index into a SparseIndex whose postings hold learned weights instead
             of tf-idf; the merge-join s_lex = sum w_q*w_d replaces BM25 term scoring while
             reusing the deterministic ordering and the existing reciprocal-rank fusion in
             bm25/fusion.rs. nest-format/src/sections/contract.rs (search_contract) plus the
             manifest gain a fusion descriptor (mode = rrf | weighted_sum and deterministic
             w1/w2/w3) so the hybrid path is self-describing and cross-checked at open. The
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank stays ground truth -- sparse only changes candidate
             generation, preserving nest's 'returned score is real cosine' guarantee. nest-python
             build_fn computes BGE-M3 lexical_weights at build time; rust does only the integer
             merge-join at serve time, so the runtime stays offline and model-free.
compression: High and direct. Learned sparse weights compress the lexical index far below storing original text
             or a classic inverted index: each doc is a short list of (u32 token_id, quantized
             weight) pairs, weights int8/f16-quantize cleanly and ascending token-id streams zstd
             well (delta + varint). This replaces nest's word-segmented BM25 tokenizer -- a known
             gap that degrades on CJK/Thai -- with language-agnostic subword ids, so it both
             shrinks the index AND fixes a correctness gap. It lets nest carry semantic keyword
             search in a fraction of the bytes of raw text, advancing the 'compress more than
             everyone while still serving' north star.
perf:        Sparse scoring is a sorted merge-join over two integer-keyed posting lists -- branchy, not SIMD,
             but cheap (a few hundred shared tokens) and runs off mmap with no float kernel; int8
             weights keep the intersection in cache. ColBERT MaxSim is O(N_q*N_d*dim) per pair and
             is the costly mode -- nest should use colbert as an optional rerank-only signal,
             never a candidate generator, to stay light; the cheap (dense + learned-sparse) pair
             is the practical hybrid. Sparse vocab is ~250k but stored sparsely, so per-doc cost
             scales with unique tokens/doc, not vocab.
license:     MIT (toolkit + BGE-M3 weights). Only the on-disk format and scoring ideas are adapted, not the
             weights; ColBERT late-interaction concept originates from Stanford ColBERT (separate,
             but the idea is unencumbered).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] MinishLab/model2vec
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A dataset-free distillation method that collapses a sentence-transformer into a static
             token-embedding lookup table, so encoding a sentence becomes tokenize + table-lookup
             + mean-pool (no forward pass), shrinking models up to 50x and running up to 500x
             faster on CPU.
innovation:  Encoding is reduced from a neural forward pass to a pure memory operation: a frozen [vocab x dim]
             static embedding matrix indexed by token ids, averaged per sentence. The matrix is
             post-processed offline with PCA (which normalizes/whitens the embedding space, giving
             ~2.8% gain even with no dim reduction) plus SIF/Zipf per-token weights (w =
             1e-3/(1e-3+p), p approximated from token rank via Zipf's law since vocabularies are
             frequency-sorted), then optionally quantized to int8/f16 with no measurable quality
             loss. The whole model is just three files: model.safetensors (the matrix),
             tokenizer.json, config.json.
techniques:
  - Static embedding matrix as a [vocab~32K x dim] lookup table; encode = tokenize -> gather rows
    -> mean-pool -> optional L2 normalize
  - PCA whitening of the distilled matrix as a quality step even at full dimensionality
    (decorrelates/normalizes the space), enabling cheap dimensionality reduction on top
  - SIF/Zipf per-token weighting: w = 1e-3/(1e-3+p) with p derived from token vocabulary rank
    (frequency-sorted), folded into the matrix offline so query time stays a plain gather+mean
  - int8 and float16 scalar quantization of the embedding matrix (25% and 50% of f32 size) with
    negligible recall loss because vectors are L2-normalized and PCA-conditioned
  - safetensors on-disk format: single 'embeddings' tensor (f32/f16/i8) + zero-copy mmap load,
    dtype and dim read from tensor shape, normalize flag in config.json
  - token_mapping array for vocabulary quantization (remaps original token ids to a reduced row
    set) plus optional per-token weights array
  - median-token-length pre-trim and unk-token filtering to bound tokenization cost
  - official Rust impl (model2vec-rs) proving the entire encode path runs with no Python, only
    safetensors + tokenizers crate
nest steals: Two concrete steals. (1) Builder-side codec for nest-format: add a deterministic 'static-embed'
             build mode in nest-python that distills/loads a model2vec matrix and produces
             embeddings via tokenize+lookup+mean-pool with PCA+SIF baked in offline. This makes
             nest's embed step dataset-free, ~500x cheaper on CPU, and fully deterministic (no
             GPU/transformer nondeterminism) -- directly serving the reproducible-build guarantee
             (same chunks + same model fingerprint => byte-identical file). (2) Validate and
             strengthen nest's int8 codec: model2vec confirms PCA-whitened + L2-normalized vectors
             quantize to int8 at ~25% size with no measurable loss, which is exactly nest's 'tiny'
             preset (int8, 0.283 ratio, recall@10 0.992) -- so nest should add an optional offline
             PCA/whitening pass before int8 packing in nest-format's encoding module to lift the
             int8 codec quality at the same byte cost. The static matrix itself (~32K x 256 int8
             ~= 8MB) could ship as an optional dictionary/section to support on-device
             re-embedding of edited chunks without a server. model2vec-rs (MIT, safetensors +
             tokenizers) is a near drop-in reference for the nest-runtime side if nest wants to
             embed query text natively offline, closing the search-text 300-500ms python boot gap.
compression: High but indirect: model2vec compresses the MODEL not the stored vectors, yet its PCA-whitening +
             SIF reweighting conditions the embedding space so int8/f16 scalar quantization loses
             ~nothing -- the precise lever nest needs to make its 'tiny' int8 codec safe and
             possibly push to lower-bit packing. It also enables a sovereign offline re-embed path
             (no 500MB transformer), keeping the single-file footprint tiny. It does not add a new
             entropy coder, but it is strong evidence for nest's quantization presets and a path
             to compressing the embedding-generation dependency itself to ~8-30MB.
perf:        ~500x faster CPU encode than the parent sentence-transformer; distillation ~30s on CPU,
             dataset-free. Models 8MB (smallest on MTEB) to ~30MB f32; int8 = 25% size, f16 = 50%
             size, both ~no quality loss. Encode is memory-bound (gather + mean over ~dozens of
             rows), trivially SIMD/multiprocessing-friendly (multiprocessing kicks in >10K
             sentences). safetensors load is zero-copy mmap, matching nest's mmap philosophy. Only
             hard runtime dep on the Python side is numpy; the Rust port needs only safetensors +
             tokenizers.
license:     MIT (code and POTION models; authors Stephan Tulkens & Thomas van Dongen, Minish Lab)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] huggingface/setfit
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        SetFit is a prompt-free few-shot text-classification framework that fine-tunes a
             Sentence-Transformer body via contrastive Siamese pair training
             (CosineSimilarityLoss) then fits a tiny logistic-regression head on the resulting
             frozen embeddings, plus a contrastive knowledge-distillation path that compresses a
             teacher into a much smaller student.
innovation:  The transferable idea is contrastive knowledge distillation: a large teacher embedder's geometry
             is transferred into a tiny student embedder using cheap unlabeled in-domain text
             turned into positive/negative pairs (and the teacher's own similarity scores as soft
             targets via ContrastiveDistillationDataset). This decouples the model that PRODUCES
             the on-disk vectors from the model the user originally chose, so the same corpus can
             be re-embedded into a smaller, faster, dimension-reduced vector space whose neighbor
             ordering is preserved -- exactly the lever nest needs to shrink the embeddings
             section (its largest, non-zstd-compressible payload) without changing the search
             contract.
techniques:
  - Contrastive Siamese fine-tuning with CosineSimilarityLoss on same-class=positive /
    diff-class=negative pairs
  - Combinatorial pair amplification: from N labeled sentences generate O(N^2) pairs (8+8 -> 28
    pos + 64 neg = 92), controlled by num_iterations=R
  - Two-stage decoupling: frozen Sentence-Transformer body produces embeddings, cheap
    LogisticRegression / differentiable SetFitHead does classification
  - ContrastiveDistillationDataset: teacher similarity scores become soft regression targets for
    the student (DistillationTrainer), reported ~123x inference speedup
  - Mean-pooling of token embeddings into a single fixed-width sentence vector (the unit of
    storage)
  - Body-swap multilinguality: any compatible ST checkpoint replaces the encoder without changing
    the pipeline
nest steals: nest steals contrastive distillation as an offline, Python-side (nest-python build path, not the
             rust core) re-embedding step: ship a small distilled student embedder fingerprint so
             a corpus can be re-encoded into a lower-dimension vector space (e.g. 768 -> 256)
             before the embeddings section is written. This shrinks nest's single largest section
             -- which the format deliberately never zstd-compresses because nest-runtime/src/simd
             scores it straight from mmap -- in a way zstd/float16/int8 cannot, since it cuts the
             float COUNT not just the per-float width, and stacks multiplicatively with the
             existing int8 codec in nest-format/src/encoding. The provenance/search_contract
             sections record the student model fingerprint so determinism holds (same chunks +
             same student => byte-identical file); content_hash stays stable because it hashes
             decoded canonical text, not vectors. The teacher-vs-student recall delta is validated
             by the mandatory exact-cosine rerank already in nest-runtime/src/search.rs, so a
             lossy smaller embedder still returns true cosine over whatever vectors are stored.
             Optionally expose a build-time preset in nest-cli (a 'nano' preset =
             distilled-256-dim + int8 + hnsw) below the current 'tiny' preset. The
             contrastive-pairs-from-unlabeled-text trick also gives nest a cheap way to
             domain-adapt the student to its PT-BR fake-news demo corpus without labels.
compression: Direct and high: embeddings are nest's biggest and least-compressible section (raw f32 is
             incompressible by zstd, and nest forbids zstd on it anyway for hot-path mmap
             scoring). Distillation attacks the only remaining axis -- the dimensionality/count of
             floats -- via a smaller student embedder, which composes with int8/float16 width
             reduction multiplicatively. A 768->256 distill is a 3x cut on top of int8's ~4x,
             plausibly pushing below the current tiny preset 0.283 size ratio while the
             exact-cosine rerank guards recall. Cleanest path toward 'compress more than everyone'
             for vector payloads.
perf:        Reported 30s training on 8 examples/class (V100, ~$0.025); distillation yields ~123x inference
             speedup of student vs teacher. Bodies are standard ST checkpoints (MPNet ~110M,
             RoBERTa-Large ~355M). All cost is training/build-time; the rust runtime is unaffected
             and gains pure win (fewer/smaller floats per dot-product, fewer cache lines touched
             by the SIMD kernels in nest-runtime/src/simd).
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] tmikolov/word2vec
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        The original C implementation of word2vec: trains static word embeddings (CBOW / skip-gram) with
             negative sampling and hierarchical softmax, and ships utilities (distance,
             word-analogy) that linear-scan a flat float32 matrix with precomputed L2-normalized
             vectors so a dot product equals cosine.
innovation:  Normalize-at-load + flat-contiguous-matrix + brute-force dot product so cosine is a single FMA
             loop -- nest's exact-cosine ground truth, minus the per-query normalization cost.
techniques:
  - L2-normalize every vector once at load so dot(q,v) == cosine, removing per-query sqrt/division
    from the scan loop
  - Flat row-major contiguous matrix M[word*size + dim] with no per-vector object header -- ideal
    mmap target, stride = size*sizeof(real)
  - Top-N nearest neighbor via insertion into sorted bestd[40]/bestw[40] arrays (no heap
    allocation, branch-predictable shift) instead of a full sort
  - Negative-sampling unigram table: precompute a 1e8-entry array filled proportional to
    count^0.75, then sample neighbors/negatives by a single random index lookup (O(1)
    frequency-weighted sampling)
  - Sigmoid lookup table: EXP_TABLE_SIZE=1000 precomputed values over [-MAX_EXP=6, +6], index =
    (f+6)*(1000/12), replacing exp() with a table read
  - Huffman-tree hierarchical softmax giving each word a variable-length binary code
    (MAX_CODE_LENGTH=40), frequent words get short paths -- a frequency-optimal prefix code
  - Vocabulary hash: 30M-slot open-addressing table, polynomial rolling hash (*257 per char),
    linear probing, rebuild when load factor > 0.7
  - Tiny self-describing text header `vocab_size layer1_size\n` then either ASCII floats or raw
    fwrite of float32 rows (binary flag) -- minimal format, no index
nest steals: nest-runtime/src/search + simd dispatcher: adopt the normalize-at-build invariant. Store
             embeddings L2-pre-normalized in the embeddings section (a one-time deterministic
             transform recorded in provenance/search_contract) so the SIMD AVX2/NEON kernel runs a
             pure dot product with zero per-query normalization, and the mandatory exact rerank
             after hnsw/bm25 becomes a bare FMA scan -- faster hot path, byte-identical
             determinism preserved. The bestd[40]/bestw[40] sorted-insert pattern maps directly
             onto nest-runtime's top-k collection for small k (avoid a BinaryHeap; a fixed sorted
             array is branch-predictable and allocation-free). For the int8 'tiny' preset,
             pre-normalizing before int8 quantization tightens the value range and improves
             recall@10. The count^0.75 unigram-table trick is reusable in nest-runtime/bm25 or any
             future negative-sampling / frequency-weighted sampler as an O(1) deterministic
             (seeded) sampler. The flat M[words x dim] row-major layout validates nest-format's
             contiguous embeddings section as the correct mmap shape; the 'vocab_size dim\\n'
             header is the minimalist ancestor of nest's manifest.
compression: Indirect but real: the L2-normalize-then-quantize ordering directly helps nest's int8/float16
             codecs -- normalized vectors have a bounded, predictable per-dimension range, so int8
             scale/zero-point is tighter and recall loss at the 0.283 'tiny' ratio shrinks. The
             count^0.75 / Huffman-code ideas are frequency-optimal coding lessons (short codes for
             frequent symbols) that generalize to entropy-coding the non-embedding sections.
             word2vec itself does NO embedding compression (raw float32), so it is a baseline to
             beat, not a source of a codec.
perf:        Brute-force linear scan is O(words*dim) per query with a tight inner FMA loop over contiguous
             memory -- cache-friendly, trivially SIMD/AVX-vectorizable, exactly nest's
             exact-cosine path. Normalization moved to load (one-time) means the query loop has no
             sqrt/div. Lookup tables (sigmoid 1000-entry, unigram 1e8-entry) trade memory for
             branch-free O(1) compute. No threading in distance.c (single-thread scan); training
             uses lock-free Hogwild-style multithreaded SGD. float32 throughout, no quantization
             -- memory-bound at scale, which is why nest's int8/f16 + hnsw matter for anything
             large.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] huggingface/text-embeddings-inference
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust inference server for embedding/reranking transformers (BERT/XLM-R/GTE/ModernBERT/Qwen) with
             a candle + ONNX-Runtime backend, token-based dynamic batching, and four pooling heads
             (CLS, mean, last-token, SPLADE) exposing dense and sparse embeddings plus
             cross-encoder rerank scores.
innovation:  SPLADE: reuse the MaskedLM vocab-projection head, apply log(1+relu(logits)) then max-pool over
             tokens to get a vocabulary-sized, ~99%-zero learned sparse embedding whose nonzeros
             are (term_id, weight) pairs usable for inverted-index retrieval and trivially
             compressible.
techniques:
  - SPLADE head: Linear+GELU -> LayerNorm -> decoder Linear to vocab -> relu -> log1p -> amax over
    sequence (max-pool) = vocab-sized sparse vector
  - Sparse encoding: filter nonzeros into (index:u32, value:f32) pairs, CSR-style
    offsets/indices/values layout
  - Token-based dynamic batching capped by --max-batch-tokens (16384) not request count, for
    variable-length inputs
  - Pooling head abstraction (CLS/mean/last-token/SPLADE) auto-detected from 1_Pooling/config.json
  - Dual backend (candle native Rust + ONNX Runtime) behind one pooling interface; safetensors +
    ONNX weight loading
  - Cross-encoder rerank head emitting one real relevance score per pair as a final exact rerank
    stage
nest steals: Add a third index section alongside hnsw(0x07) and bm25(0x08): a SPLADE-style learned-sparse
             index, e.g. splade_index(0x09) in nest-format (sections.rs + new encoding path),
             built in nest-python at build time by running a ForMaskedLM model with the
             log(1+relu) max-pool head. Store each chunk's sparse vector as deterministic
             alphabetical/ascending term-id (index:u32 LE, value:f16/int8) pairs (CSR: offsets[] +
             indices[] + values[]), which respects nest's additive v1 reservation (0x09+
             reserved), little-endian, repr(C) rules. nest-runtime gains a splade::SparseIndex
             that scores queries via sparse dot-product over an inverted index, then feeds
             candidates into the EXISTING mandatory exact-cosine rerank (search contract
             preserved: SPLADE/bm25 are ANN proxies, cosine stays ground truth) and fuses with
             bm25 via the existing reciprocal-rank fusion. This is a strict upgrade over the
             current word-segmented bm25 tokenizer (the known CJK/Thai gap): SPLADE term expansion
             is model-learned and vocabulary-based, so it degrades far less on non-space-delimited
             languages. Crucially for the north star, a SPLADE vector is ~99% zeros, so CSR + zstd
             (allowed on non-embedding sections) compresses it dramatically better than a dense
             f32 embedding, letting a 'sparse' preset ship retrieval quality near dense embeddings
             at a fraction of the bytes.
compression: High and direct. A SPLADE vector is a learned sparse vector that is ~99% zeros, so stored as CSR
             (sorted term-id deltas + quantized weights) it is an order of magnitude smaller than
             a dense f32 embedding while remaining a strong retrieval signal. Delta-coding
             ascending term ids (varint or fixed u32) plus int8/f16 weights, then zstd on the
             whole section (permitted because sparse indices are not on the SIMD dot-product hot
             path), pushes nest's size ratio well below the current tiny preset for text-dominant
             corpora. It also unlocks 'compress-and-still-search' for the bm25 replacement path.
perf:        Sparse dot-product retrieval scales with nonzeros per query (tens to low hundreds of terms), not
             embedding dimension, so it is cache-friendly and avoids dense vector scans; well
             suited to nest's mmap-off-disk model. Dense pooling (mean/cls/max) is expressed as
             framework tensor reductions; on CPU these map cleanly to nest's existing AVX2/NEON
             dot-product dispatcher for the exact-cosine rerank stage. Dynamic batching is a
             server-side throughput trick (offline nest does not need the queue) but the per-token
             budget idea informs batched build-time embedding in nest-python. License: Apache-2.0
             (note: TEI's own server has a custom HFOIL license on newer releases; only the
             algorithmic SPLADE/pooling math is being reused, not their code).
license:     Apache-2.0 for older code; newer TEI releases carry a custom HFOIL source-available license. Only
             the SPLADE/pooling math (public research, SPLADE paper) is adapted, not TEI source,
             so nest stays clean.

[MEDIUM] jina-ai/clip-as-service
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A scalable client/server (gRPC, HTTP, WebSocket) for producing CLIP text+image embeddings and
             cross-modal rank scores, with swappable PyTorch / ONNX Runtime / TensorRT inference
             backends and DocArray protobuf transport.
innovation:  Decoupling the CLIP model into two independently-runnable towers behind a single L2-normalized
             shared embedding space, served through a portable ONNX Runtime backend so the same
             image-aligned text encoder (including XLM-RoBERTa-based multilingual M-CLIP) runs
             anywhere without the full PyTorch/CUDA stack. For nest the load-bearing idea is "the
             embedding model is a portable ONNX graph + a vocab, not a server" plus the
             cross-modal exact-score rerank pattern.
techniques:
  - ONNX Runtime backend: CLIP towers exported to a single .onnx graph run on CPU via onnxruntime
    with no PyTorch/CUDA dependency (TensorRT/JIT optional accelerators)
  - Two-tower shared embedding space: separate text and image encoders projecting into one
    L2-normalized 512/640/768/1024-dim space so cross-modal cosine is meaningful
  - M-CLIP multilingual: XLM-RoBERTa / LaBSE text tower aligned/distilled to the frozen CLIP image
    space, giving non-English (PT-BR, CJK) text image-aligned vectors
  - /rank cross-modal re-ranking: cheap candidate gather then exact joint CLIP-score (softmax of
    logit_scale * cosine) rerank, returning a real score not an ANN proxy
  - minibatch_size (default 32) encode batching decoupled from num_worker_preprocess (4) CPU
    preprocessing workers: separate preprocessing parallelism from the encode kernel
  - DocArray columnar protobuf serialization of ndarrays with streaming on-disk format and
    configurable ndarray_type/dtype for large batch transfer
nest steals: nest-python build path: adopt the ONNX Runtime CPU backend pattern so nest's embedding step is a
             portable .onnx graph + tokenizer instead of a torch dependency, keeping builds
             deterministic and shrinking the model payload shipped with the demo corpus. Adopt an
             M-CLIP-style XLM-RoBERTa/LaBSE multilingual text tower as a default text model
             fingerprint written into provenance: this fixes the documented bm25 CJK/Thai
             degradation at the embedding layer and matches the PT-BR fake-news datasets, all into
             the existing embeddings section. In nest-runtime the /rank pattern is conceptually
             identical to nest's mandatory exact-cosine rerank after hnsw/bm25 RRF, validating
             that design; a logit_scale*cosine softmax could be an optional scoring mode in
             search_contract. The image tower (ViT-B/32 exported to ONNX) is the concrete route to
             the mission's "fast image similarity": image vectors land in the same embeddings
             section and are scored by the existing SIMD dot-product dispatcher with zero new
             codec, since L2-normalized CLIP image embeddings cosine-search identically to text.
             Fully additive: no format bump, only embeddings + provenance(model fingerprint).
compression: Low-to-indirect for raw bytes-on-disk compression. clip-as-service has no entropy coder, no
             embedding quantization, and no on-disk index; it never compresses embeddings
             (mirroring nest's rule that embeddings stay raw for hot-path scoring). The
             compression-adjacent wins are second-order: a portable ONNX text+image model shrinks
             the build-side dependency/model payload vs a torch install, and a single shared
             512-dim image-aligned space lets images and text reuse one embeddings section and one
             SIMD kernel instead of a separate image codec, keeping the format minimal. CLIP's
             native fp16 weights also confirm nest's float16 embedding preset is safe for these
             vectors.
perf:        Documented ~800 QPS on an RTX 3090 default config; throughput is GPU-bound and irrelevant to
             nest's CPU/mmap runtime. Transferable perf lessons: the preprocessing/encode split
             (num_worker_preprocess=4 vs minibatch_size=32), and that ONNX Runtime CPU inference
             of a ViT-B/32 tower is viable for batch indexing on commodity hardware. CLIP
             embeddings are L2-normalized so nest's cosine==dot-product fast path applies
             directly, and image and text vectors share the exact same SIMD dot kernel
             (AVX2/NEON/scalar) with no per-modality branch.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] stanfordnlp/GloVe
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        GloVe is a log-bilinear word-embedding model trained on a global word-word cooccurrence matrix,
             shipped as a 4-stage C toolchain (vocab_count -> cooccur -> shuffle -> glove) whose
             real engineering value is its external-memory sparse-matrix pipeline that
             sorts/merges/shuffles billions of records on a tiny RAM budget.
innovation:  External-memory construction of a giant sparse keyed dataset: fixed-size POD records (CREC = {int
             word1; int word2; real val}) are streamed to disk in sorted runs, then combined with
             a min-heap k-way merge that ACCUMULATES values for duplicate (word1,word2) keys
             during the merge pass, so deduplication/aggregation costs nothing extra and peak RAM
             is bounded by a single overflow buffer regardless of total data size. The same
             fixed-record discipline enables a two-phase external Fisher-Yates shuffle. This is
             the canonical recipe for building large derived index sections on a
             resource-constrained machine.
techniques:
  - CREC fixed-size POD record {int word1; int word2; float val} written via raw
    fwrite(sizeof(CREC)) with no per-record framing -- pure densely-packed binary array
  - Hybrid dense/sparse storage: a dense bigram_table indexed by lookup[w1-1]+w2-2 for frequent
    pairs (product of frequency ranks < max_product), sparse overflow buffer cr[] for the long
    tail
  - Overflow-to-disk: when cr[] hits overflow_threshold, qsort by (word1,word2) and flush a sorted
    run to a temp file, bounding peak RAM
  - k-way merge of sorted temp files via a min-heap priority queue (merge_files), emitting
    globally ordered records and accumulating val for equal keys in one streaming pass
    (merge_write)
  - Frequency-rank remapping: words encoded as integer ranks (most frequent = small id) so the hot
    dense region is the small-id corner -- an implicit Zipf-aware layout
  - AdaGrad per-parameter adaptive LR with gradient clipping over a flat W array of vocab*(dim+1)
    floats (dim + 1 bias), context vectors offset by vocab*(dim+1)
  - Weighting function f(x) = (x/x_max)^alpha for x<x_max else 1, alpha=0.75, x_max=100 --
    sublinear down-weighting of rare cooccurrences
  - Two-phase external Fisher-Yates shuffle: shuffle within array_size (2e6) record chunks to temp
    files, then merge-shuffle ~array_size/num records from each chunk; budget set by -memory
nest steals: Steal the external-memory sparse pipeline into nest-format's writer/build path (NOT the embedding
             model -- nest already produces embeddings from a real model). Concretely: (1) when
             building the optional bm25_index (0x08) inverted postings and any future graph-search
             edge section, use GloVe's CREC pattern -- a repr(C), little-endian, fixed-width POD
             posting/edge record {u32 term_id; u32 doc_id; f32 weight} written as a packed array,
             sorted into runs, then combined with a min-heap k-way merge that accumulates
             duplicate keys in one pass. This builds indexes for corpora larger than RAM on a
             minimal-budget machine while preserving determinism (alphabetical/sorted term order
             maps directly onto GloVe's sorted-merge ordering, so determinism falls out for free).
             (2) Adopt frequency-rank id remapping so dense postings cluster at small ids,
             improving zstd ratio on the non-embedding index sections (encoding already covers
             0x07/0x08). (3) Reuse the two-phase external Fisher-Yates only if a build step ever
             needs shuffled sampling order, with a seeded RNG to honor deterministic builds. Each
             module stays under the 300-line cap as a single-responsibility unit (e.g.
             nest-format/src/build/external_merge.rs). The model/AdaGrad/weighting math is
             reference-only.
compression: Indirect but real. GloVe itself does NOT compress (raw f32 vectors, unframed binary; released
             vectors are large uncompressed files). Its relevance to nest's
             compress-more-than-everyone north star is the BUILD side: the sorted-run +
             k-way-merge-with-accumulation pattern is how you construct large index sections (bm25
             postings, graph edges) deterministically on a tiny RAM budget, and frequency-rank id
             remapping makes those non-embedding sections more compressible by zstd (small
             clustered ids -> better entropy coding). It does not help embedding compression (nest
             already uses float16/int8 there).
perf:        Pure sequential disk IO with fixed-size records: no parsing, fwrite/fread of sizeof(CREC) blocks,
             trivially mmappable and vectorizable. Min-heap merge is O(N log k) for k temp files.
             Peak RAM bounded by the overflow_threshold/array_size buffer, set explicitly by
             -memory. CREC is 12 bytes (2x int32 + float32) -- nest should mirror with a repr(C)
             packed 12-byte record. The dense bigram_table avoids hashing for the hot Zipfian
             corner. No SIMD in GloVe's IO path; the AdaGrad inner loop is scalar over dim.
             Determinism caveat: GloVe's shuffle uses rand_long() (not reproducibly seeded by
             default), so any nest adaptation must seed the RNG to honor the deterministic-build
             constraint.
license:     Apache License 2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] ddangelov/Top2Vec
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        Top2Vec is a topic-modeling pipeline that places documents AND words in one shared embedding
             space, then derives topics geometrically: UMAP-reduce doc vectors, HDBSCAN-cluster
             the dense regions, take each cluster's centroid as a "topic vector," and cosine-rank
             the nearest word vectors to label it; search is served by an optional hnswlib HNSW
             index over the same vectors.
innovation:  The single most transferable idea is the joint document+word embedding space combined with the
             "centroid-as-query" trick: because words and documents are embedded with the same
             model into the same metric space, a cluster centroid (mean of member doc vectors) is
             itself a valid query vector, and the n nearest WORD vectors to it become a free,
             human-readable label for that cluster of chunks. No separate keyword extraction, no
             supervised step, no extra index types are needed: one cosine kernel over one vector
             pool produces clustering, topic labels, and semantic search. This turns "what is this
             corpus / cluster of chunks about" into pure linear algebra over vectors nest already
             stores.
techniques:
  - Joint doc+word embedding: embed corpus words alongside chunk text with the same model so word
    and chunk vectors are cosine-comparable in one pool
  - Centroid topic vectors: topic = arithmetic mean of member doc vectors in ORIGINAL full-dim
    space, not the UMAP-reduced space
  - Cosine-rank labeling: rank n-nearest word vectors to a centroid to auto-generate topic
    keywords
  - UMAP for clustering-prep only (n_neighbors=15, n_components=5, metric=cosine, min_dist=0.0) to
    densify sparse high-dim vectors before clustering
  - HDBSCAN density clustering (min_cluster_size=15, metric=euclidean,
    cluster_selection_method=eom) that emits noise/outliers instead of forcing assignment; topic
    count emerges from density, no preset k
  - Hierarchical topic reduction: iteratively merge nearest topic centroids to collapse N topics
    to a requested smaller count
  - Optional hnswlib HNSW index (M / ef_construction / ef_search, space=cosine/ip/l2, incremental
    add_items) over the same vectors for fast doc/word ANN
nest steals: Two additive moves, both reusing the existing SIMD cosine kernel and embeddings section, no heavy
             deps. (1) nest-runtime: a new offline `topics` module (~250 loc,
             single-responsibility, under the 300-line cap) that streams centroids over the
             already-mmapped embeddings section and, for labeling, cosine-ranks nearest chunk/term
             vectors via the existing simd dispatcher + exact rerank path used by `search`.
             Clustering should be a deterministic, seeded, dependency-light scheme (seeded k-means
             or fixed-threshold greedy cosine agglomeration) to honor nest's
             deterministic/never-panic/offline-by-construction rules instead of vendoring
             UMAP/HDBSCAN (Numba/SciPy stacks that break single-file/offline). The
             centroid-as-query trick fits nest's contract: a topic vector is just another query,
             so exact-cosine ground truth still holds. (2) nest-format: an OPTIONAL additive
             section in the RESERVED 0x09+ range, e.g. topics_index(0x09), holding repr(C)
             little-endian records {topic_id, inline f16/int8 centroid or offset into embeddings,
             member chunk_id ranges, top-k label term ids}; it is covered by the existing
             per-section SHA-256 checksum and folds into content_hash via decoded canonical bytes,
             preserving determinism and the four integrity surfaces. Centroids are NOT on the hot
             scoring path, so they MAY be zstd-compressed (unlike embeddings). nest-cli gains a
             thin `topics` subcommand mirroring the existing 8-subcommand clap surface. Unlocks:
             corpus summarization, cluster-scoped/faceted "search within topic", graph-search
             seeds (centroids as nodes), and auto-labels for citations.
compression: Indirect but real. (a) Centroids are a coarse codebook: store K topic centroids plus per-chunk
             topic_id (1-2 bytes) and keep each embedding as centroid + small residual -- the
             IVF/PQ residual idea reached via Top2Vec's centroid geometry -- shrinking the
             embeddings section (the largest in tiny/compressed presets) below flat int8,
             composing with nest's existing int8/f16 codecs. (b) Reordering chunks by topic_id
             places semantically similar embeddings adjacent, improving byte-locality and the zstd
             ratio on the NON-embedding sections (chunks_canonical, provenance) that ARE
             zstd-eligible. (c) Auto-generated label terms double as a tiny corpus dictionary that
             can seed a zstd custom dictionary for chunks_canonical. Net: a path toward "compress
             more than everyone" by exploiting redundancy ACROSS chunks (codebook + residual +
             locality), not just within each chunk.
perf:        Centroid computation is a single streaming mean over the embeddings section (one mmap pass,
             O(N*d), SIMD-summable). Cosine-ranking labels reuses nest's AVX2/NEON/scalar
             dot-product dispatcher -- no new kernel. The expensive/fragile upstream parts (UMAP
             via Numba, HDBSCAN, hnswlib build) are training-time, heavyweight, and
             non-deterministic by default; do NOT vendor them into the rust runtime -- push
             optional clustering to the python BUILD side (nest-python / build pipeline) where
             SciPy-class deps are acceptable, and keep the rust serve path to deterministic
             centroid math plus existing HNSW (0x07) and cosine rerank. hnswlib's
             M/ef_construction/ef_search and incremental add_items already overlap nest's seeded
             ann::HnswIndex, so nothing new there. Memory: K centroids at d dims is negligible (K
             << N); residual quantization is the only footprint lever and it strictly shrinks the
             file.
license:     BSD-3-Clause

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[LOW] guillaume-be/rust-bert
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust port of Hugging Face transformers built on the tch-rs libtorch bindings (with optional ONNX
             Runtime via ort), whose sentence_embeddings pipeline reproduces the
             Sentence-Transformers modules.json chain (Transformer -> Pooling -> optional Dense ->
             optional Normalize) to turn token sequences into fixed Vec<f32> embeddings.
innovation:  The exact, masking-correct reimplementation of the Sentence-Transformers post-processing chain in
             Rust: attention-mask-weighted mean pooling, optional dense projection, and L2
             normalization, driven declaratively by the model's own modules.config.json so the
             embedding is bit-faithful to the upstream Python model.
techniques:
  - Mask-weighted mean pooling: multiply token states by expanded attention mask, sum_dim over seq
    axis, divide by clamped mask-sum (min 1e-9) so padding never dilutes the mean
  - Alternative pooling modes in one struct: CLS (select(1,0)), max (masked_fill_ padding to -1e9
    then max_dim), and mean-sqrt (divide by sqrt(mask_sum))
  - Declarative module chain parsed from modules.json/sentence_bert_config.json with enforced
    order (pos0 Transformer, pos1 Pooling, pos2-3 optional Dense/Normalize) via
    SentenceEmbeddingsModuleType enum
  - Dense post-projection layer (nn::Linear + activation) that can reduce embedding dimensionality
    before storage
  - L2 normalization making cosine similarity reduce to a plain dot product
  - tch-rs convert_model.py path: PyTorch .bin weights converted to a libtorch-loadable tensor
    store, cached under RUSTBERT_CACHE
  - rust-tokenizers multi-threaded WordPiece/BPE tokenization decoupled from the model
nest steals: nest never runs inference (python builds embeddings), so do NOT vendor tch/libtorch. The steal is
             narrow and goes into nest-python's build path plus nest-format provenance: (1) record
             the resolved Sentence-Transformers module chain (pooling mode, dense in/out dims,
             normalize bool) into the provenance section and search_contract as the model
             fingerprint, so two builds with the same chain are provably byte-identical and a
             consumer can verify the embeddings were L2-normalized. (2) If normalize=true is
             recorded in search_contract, nest-runtime's simd dispatcher can drop the per-vector
             norm in cosine rerank and run a pure dot product against the int8/f16 mmap'd
             embeddings section (the hot path), since cosine == dot for unit vectors. (3) The
             mask-weighted mean-pool semantics document exactly what a 'chunk embedding' means,
             hardening the deterministic contract; it is reference-level guidance, not Rust code
             to import into the small core.
compression: Low-to-indirect. rust-bert has no entropy coder, quantization, or on-disk compression to steal.
             The only compression-adjacent lever is the optional Dense projection layer, which is
             the upstream mechanism for shipping lower-dimensional embeddings (e.g. 384->256):
             fewer dims directly shrinks nest's embeddings section, the largest tiny-preset
             payload. Recording normalize=true also lets the runtime store and score embeddings
             without keeping norms, marginally tightening the format.
perf:        Inference throughput is entirely libtorch/ONNX-bound (CPU or CUDA via .with_device()); no custom
             SIMD kernels, no quantized inference. Heavyweight C++ runtime dependency, opposite of
             nest's small-core/offline philosophy. The post-processing tensor ops (mean/max pool,
             normalize) are trivial and would be plain scalar/ndarray loops if ever ported.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[REFERENCE] cpcdoy/rust-sbert
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A thin Rust port of sentence-transformers that runs DistilUSE/DistilBERT multilingual
             sentence-embedding models through libtorch (tch-rs/rust-bert), producing fixed-size
             embeddings via masked mean pooling plus a dense+tanh projection.
innovation:  The exact, reproducible embedding-head pipeline that turns per-token transformer outputs into one
             sentence vector: attention-mask-weighted mean pooling (sum of masked token embeddings
             divided by the clamped mask count) followed by a Dense linear+tanh projection. This
             is the canonical post-encoder math every sentence-transformer uses, and matching it
             bit-for-bit is what makes embeddings reproducible and model-aligned.
techniques:
  - masked mean pooling: input_mask_expanded = mask.unsqueeze(-1).expand_as(emb);
    (emb*mask).sum_dim(1) / mask.sum_dim(1) so padding tokens contribute zero
  - Dense embedding head: x.apply(linear).tanh() projecting hidden dim (768) to the model output
    dim, the learned reduction that defines the final vector space
  - dual tokenizer backends behind one API: HF tokenizers (wordpiece/BPE) and rust_tokenizers
    (sentencepiece), selectable as SBertHF vs SBertRT
  - batched forward over Vec<String> with rayon CPU parallelism sized by num_cpus
  - tch-rs/libtorch tensor ops (sum_dim_intlist, unsqueeze, expand_as, clamp) as the f32 compute
    kernel; output_attentions=true config flag exposes mask-aware tensors
  - offline model conversion via prepare_distilbert.py into a libtorch-loadable weights directory
    (Apache-2.0)
nest steals: nest-python is the embedding producer and MUST replicate this exact pooling+dense head so
             nest-runtime cosine scores are meaningful. Concretely: in nest-python's embedding
             build path, after running the encoder, apply the identical masked-mean formula
             (sum(token*mask)/clamp(sum(mask),1e-9)) then the model dense linear+tanh (or
             L2-normalize) head, and record the precise pooling mode plus dense weights identity
             inside the provenance section's model fingerprint. This locks determinism: same
             chunks + same model fingerprint => byte-identical embeddings. For nest-runtime,
             knowing the head is mean-pool+tanh (NOT L2-normalized by default here) clarifies that
             the SIMD dot-product hot path needs an explicit normalization assumption in
             search_contract; nest should pin 'embeddings are L2-normalized at build time' so the
             AVX2/NEON dot product equals true cosine with no per-query norm. The dual-tokenizer
             split (wordpiece vs sentencepiece) is also a direct fix for nest's bm25 CJK/Thai gap:
             adopting a sentencepiece-style subword tokenizer in nest-runtime bm25::Bm25Index
             removes the word-segmentation degradation while keeping deterministic alphabetical
             term ordering.
compression: Low and indirect. The repo has no entropy coder, quantization, or on-disk compression (embeddings
             are dense f32 tensors from libtorch). Its relevance to compress-more-than-everyone is
             upstream: pinning the exact pooling+dense head plus an explicit L2-normalize step is
             the precondition that makes nest aggressive embedding quantization (int8/float16 in
             tiny/compressed presets) safe -- once vectors are unit-norm, int8 scalar quantization
             preserves cosine recall (nest already reports recall@10 0.992 at 0.283 ratio).
             Without the deterministic normalized head, lossy embedding compression cannot be
             validated against a stable ground truth.
perf:        Runs on libtorch f32 via tch-rs 0.15 (heavy hundreds-of-MB native dependency, not embeddable --
             nest must NOT vendor this; reference-only for the math). CPU batching via rayon
             scaled by num_cpus. DistilUSE multilingual covers 13 languages, STS2017 score 80.1,
             output dim ~512 (768 hidden then dense projection). No SIMD kernel of its own; vector
             math delegated to libtorch BLAS. No quantization, no mmap, no custom on-disk format
             -- model is a plain converted libtorch weights directory.
license:     Apache-2.0

# category: tokenizers  (4 repos)
# focus: tokenization (the front of every embed + a compression primitive)
