--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] huggingface/tokenizers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust-core tokenization library (Python/Node/Ruby bindings) implementing BPE, WordPiece, Unigram
             and WordLevel behind a five-stage normalizer -> pre-tokenizer -> model ->
             post-processor -> decoder pipeline, with full original-span alignment tracking and a
             single self-describing tokenizer.json serialization.
innovation:  Reversible byte-level alphabet (GPT-2 scheme): the 256 raw bytes are bijectively remapped to
             printable Unicode codepoints so any byte stream - CJK, Thai, emoji, binary, JSON, PDF
             text - is losslessly tokenizable by a learned subword vocabulary with zero UNK and
             zero out-of-vocab loss, turning a learned merge table into a reversible
             variable-length dictionary code over arbitrary bytes.
techniques:
  - Byte-level BPE: bijective 256-byte to printable-Unicode map (byte_level.rs) so the model
    alphabet is closed and tokenization is fully reversible/lossless on any input
  - BPE merge_word: Word as Vec of (symbol, prev, next) linked-array plus a BinaryHeap of Merge
    candidates keyed by (rank, pos); merges lowest-rank pair first with deterministic tie-break
    (leftmost), O(n log n) per word
  - merges stored as HashMap<(u32,u32), (rank:u32, new_id:u32)> - pair-rank lookup, not string
    compares; vocab as HashMap<String,u32> plus reverse Vec for id_to_token
  - Unigram model: Viterbi forward-DP over a lattice of all substrings present in vocab, scored by
    log-probabilities, returns minimum-cost (most-probable) segmentation; trained via EM with
    iterative pruning
  - LRU word-result cache (cache.rs) + per-thread cache (PR 2028) to memoize BPE output per
    pre-token, +47-62% on parallel workloads
  - byte_fallback (SentencePiece trick): unknown chars decomposed to <0xNN> byte tokens so vocab
    stays small but coverage is total
  - Full alignment/offset tracking: every token carries (start,end) byte offsets back into the
    ORIGINAL string even through destructive (NFKC/lowercase/strip) normalization
  - Single self-describing tokenizer.json via serde: one file carries
    normalizer+pre_tokenizer+model(vocab,merges)+post_processor+decoder, version-tagged,
    reconstructs the exact pipeline
nest steals: Three concrete lifts. (1) Replace the word-segmented bm25 tokenizer in nest-runtime/src/bm25 with
             a byte-level pre-tokenizer + tiny deterministic BPE/Unigram model: kills the known
             CJK/Thai degradation gap, stays deterministic (alphabetical-equivalent merge-rank
             order), needs no extra section. (2) Add a learned-BPE codec for the text-bearing
             sections: train a per-file BPE/Unigram vocab over chunks_canonical, store the merge
             table as a new RESERVED encoding id (4-255) in nest-format/src/encoding, and emit
             token-id streams instead of raw UTF-8 - this is dictionary compression that composes
             with zstd and is the single most mission-relevant idea for
             compress-more-than-everyone on repetitive corpora; vocab+merges become a
             deterministic part of content_hash. (3) Steal the offset-alignment model to harden
             chunks_original_spans: nest already maps canonical->original; the
             (start,end)-through-normalization technique is the proven design for keeping
             nest://content_hash/chunk_id byte-exact after lossy normalization. Mirror the single
             tokenizer.json idea: keep the tokenizer config inline in the manifest so the file
             stays self-describing and offline.
compression: Directly central: a learned BPE/Unigram vocabulary IS a variable-length dictionary code, and the
             byte-level alphabet makes it lossless and reversible on arbitrary bytes
             (text/json/yaml/pdf-text). Token-id streams shrink repetitive corpora before zstd,
             and Unigram-Viterbi gives provably minimum-cost segmentation. The merge table is the
             entropy-model that beats generic zstd on structured/repetitive data - the core lever
             for the compress-more-than-everyone north star. Caveat: must never be applied to the
             embeddings section (hot-path SIMD scoring needs raw f32/f16/int8 off mmap), only to
             text-bearing sections.
perf:        Rust core tokenizes ~1GB/CPU-min (<20s/GB cited). Hot path is merge_word with the BinaryHeap; LRU
             + per-thread cache memoize per pre-token (single-thread flat, +47-62% at 88+
             threads). Merge lookups are integer (u32,u32) HashMap probes, no string compares.
             Build-time win: BPE::Builder no longer formats strings in hot loop (~45% faster
             from_file on Llama-3). No SIMD in the tokenizer itself; parallelism is rayon over the
             batch. Memory: vocab+merges are plain HashMaps, fits easily; tokenizer.json for large
             LLM vocabs (128k-256k) is a few MB.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] google/sentencepiece
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        Language-agnostic subword tokenizer that trains a unigram-LM (or BPE) vocabulary by EM + Viterbi
             over a lattice and ships the whole model (pieces, float scores, byte-fallback, NFKC
             normalizer FST) self-contained in one protobuf, decoded at runtime via a Darts
             double-array trie.
innovation:  A tokenizer is a self-contained, deterministic compression codec: a vocabulary of variable-length
             pieces with floating-point scores plus a single best-path (Viterbi-over-lattice)
             decoder segments arbitrary text losslessly into ID streams. The whole model (vocab +
             scores + normalization rules + byte-fallback) is one frozen byte-stable blob, and the
             hot-path lookup structure is a double-array trie that holds 80k+ pieces in ~2 MB
             instead of ~83 MB. This is exactly the deterministic, single-file, compress-text
             primitive nest needs, sitting in front of (or instead of) zstd for the canonical-text
             section.
techniques:
  - Unigram LM training: suffix-array-seeded large vocabulary, then EM (E:
    forward-backward/Viterbi expected piece counts, M: update piece probabilities) with
    loss-ranked pruning by shrinking_factor=0.75 until target size -- inverse of BPE's
    grow-by-merge
  - Viterbi best-path segmentation over a lattice where each piece is an arc scored by log unigram
    prob; O(n) memory by storing only best_path score+backpointer per char position (lattice nodes
    recognized then discarded, no node pool)
  - Darts double-array trie: 4 parallel arrays base[]/check[]/value[]/score[], transition
    next=base[node]+byte validated by check[next]==node; collapses 256-pointer-per-node tries to
    ~2 MB for 80k pieces
  - first_byte_max_len[256] table for early trie-traversal termination (2x on CJK where most byte
    continuations are invalid)
  - byte_fallback: OOV chars decompose into 256 raw BYTE-type pieces (Type=6) so the model is
    total/lossless over any input, never emitting <unk>
  - Self-contained proto2 ModelProto: repeated SentencePiece{piece, float score, Type} plus
    NormalizerSpec, all behavior-changing params embedded for reproducible portable artifact
  - precompiled_charsmap: NFKC normalization compiled into a second double-array trie +
    '\0'-delimited rewrite string, layout [u32 trie_len][trie][normalized], so normalization is a
    data table not code
  - U+2581 meta-symbol marks whitespace so detokenization is a pure string replace -> fully
    reversible round-trip
nest steals: Two distinct steals. (1) New section + codec for extreme text compression: add a frozen unigram-LM
             tokenizer model as an optional v1 section (reserved id 0x09+) and a new encoding id
             (range 4-255) that stores chunks_canonical as Viterbi-segmented piece-ID streams
             instead of raw/zstd bytes. Python (nest-python) trains the EM model offline;
             nest-format/src/encoding/ gets a unigram_codec.rs holding the read-only double-array
             trie + scores and running O(n)-memory Viterbi decode; the ID stream is then
             varint/range-coded and optionally zstd'd. Because IDs index a shared vocab, this
             beats per-chunk zstd on short repetitive corpora and is deterministic (seeded EM +
             stable tie-break) so byte-identical builds hold. Segmentation is reversible so
             content_hash over decoded canonical text stays stable. (2) Fix the known CJK/Thai
             bm25 gap: replace the word-split tokenizer at
             crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/tokenize.rs with subword Viterbi segmentation from the
             same shipped model, giving script-agnostic BM25 terms (alphabetical term order
             preserved for determinism). The Darts double-array trie fits nest's repr(C),
             little-endian, mmap, never-panic constraints: it is just integer arrays read straight
             off disk.
compression: Directly central to compress-data-more-than-everyone. A shared subword vocabulary turns canonical
             text into dense small-integer ID streams; on repetitive/structured corpora (news,
             JSON keys, code) this routinely cuts entropy-coder input vs raw bytes, and stacking
             range/zstd on the ID stream compounds it. Unlike a general byte compressor the model
             is trained ON the corpus so it captures domain-specific morphemes, and byte_fallback
             guarantees losslessness on any input. The double-array trie keeps the decoder tiny
             and mmap-resident, fitting the light/portable mandate.
perf:        ~50k sentences/sec segmentation, ~6 MB runtime footprint (vocab ~1.5 MB + trie ~2 MB + score/hash
             arrays). Inference is O(n*L) time, O(n) memory (L = max piece len 16-32 bytes). Hot
             path is pointer-light integer indexing into base/check, ideal for mmap. SIMD upside
             is modest: community ARM64 port used CRC32 only for model-load hashing, not Viterbi;
             trie traversal is lookup/branch-bound not a dot-product kernel, so it does not
             contend with nest's AVX2/NEON cosine path. Stack-allocated Viterbi buffers (cap ~8192
             chars) with heap fallback keep allocation off the hot path.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] openai/tiktoken
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A fast Rust-core byte-level BPE (byte pair encoding) tokenizer whose vocabulary is a flat
             HashMap<Vec<u8>, Rank> mapping byte sequences to compact integer merge-priority
             ranks, driving a regex pre-split plus greedy lowest-rank merge loop that is
             reversible, lossless, language-agnostic, and deterministic.
innovation:  BPE as a deterministic, reversible, lossless byte-to-integer compression code: pre-split text with
             a fixed unicode-property regex, then greedily merge adjacent byte-pairs in strict
             ascending "rank" order (rank = global merge priority loaded from a tiny on-disk
             vocab), so the same input always yields the same shorter integer-ID stream and decode
             is a pure HashMap<Rank, Vec<u8>> concat. Tokenization is itself compression (fewer,
             denser symbols than raw UTF-8 bytes), it is byte-level so it never fails on
             arbitrary/CJK/Thai/binary input, and it is bit-exact reproducible from a frozen vocab
             file.
techniques:
  - byte-level BPE: vocab is HashMap<Vec<u8>, Rank> (rank = merge priority, lower merged first);
    decode is HashMap<Rank, Vec<u8>>
  - greedy lowest-rank merge on parts: Vec<(start, rank)>; O(mn) linear-scan-for-min for pieces
    <100 bytes, BinaryHeap<Merge> O(m log n) for large pieces (_byte_pair_merge_large)
  - Rank::MAX sentinel marks unmergeable pairs; get_rank closure looks up
    piece[parts[i].0..parts[i+3].0] slices in the encoder map
  - fixed unicode-property regex pre-tokenization (fancy_regex), e.g. cl100k splits on \p{L}++
    runs, \p{N}{1,3}+ digit groups, contraction suffixes, whitespace runs; bounds merges and
    guarantees determinism
  - compact .tiktoken vocab format: one line = base64(token_bytes) + ' ' + ascii(rank) + newline;
    loaded into the map and SHA-256 verified against expected_hash
  - special tokens in a separate special_tokens_encoder map plus separate regex so merges never
    cross sentinel boundaries
  - FxHashMap over std HashMap (5-10% win); thread-local cloned regexes indexed by thread-id to
    avoid lock contention; no SIMD, GIL released for parallelism
  - the token set itself acts as the cache (no separate memoization); hot subwords are already
    vocab entries
nest steals: Add a byte-level BPE tokenizer in nest-runtime::bm25 to replace the word-segmented tokenizer (the
             documented CJK/Thai gap): BPE pre-split plus merge yields language-agnostic subword
             terms while keeping bm25's required alphabetical/deterministic term order. Ship the
             frozen vocab as a new optional additive section (reserve a v1 id in 0x09+, e.g.
             bpe_vocab) encoded exactly as tiktoken's base64(bytes)+rank lines, then
             zstd-compressed via nest's existing non-embedding codec path; stays single-file,
             offline, per-section SHA-256 checksummed. The merge loop is pure-safe Rust (parts Vec
             + sentinel, no panics, thiserror on missing vocab), ~150-200 loc so it fits the
             300-line rule, and seedless-deterministic so it preserves reproducible builds.
             Strategically, also expose a nest-cli/nest-format codec that stores chunks_canonical
             as BPE token-ID streams (a denser integer code than raw UTF-8) feeding a future
             entropy-coded text section; content_hash stays stable because it hashes decoded
             canonical bytes, not the encoding.
compression: High and on-mission. BPE is compression by construction: it turns multi-byte UTF-8 runs into
             single small integers, and a BPE token stream is a strictly denser, dictionary-coded
             representation that downstream zstd/entropy coding compresses further. It is the
             natural front-end for an extreme text-compression pipeline (BPE IDs to rANS/range
             coder), and being byte-level plus deterministic it respects nest's lossless,
             reproducible, language-agnostic guarantees. Directly closes the known CJK/Thai bm25
             tokenizer gap.
perf:        3-6x faster than HuggingFace tokenizers on 1GB GPT-2 text. Pure scalar Rust, no SIMD. Two merge
             paths: linear O(mn) for short pieces (<100 bytes, common after regex split),
             BinaryHeap O(m log n) for rare long pieces. FxHashMap and thread-local regex clones
             are the main micro-opts; parallelism comes from releasing the Python GIL, not rayon.
             Memory is dominated by the vocab map (cl100k ~100k entries, o200k ~200k entries); the
             .tiktoken file is a few MB of base64 lines, very zstd-friendly.
license:     MIT License

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] OpenNMT/Tokenizer
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A fast C++ (with Python pyonmttok bindings) reversible tokenization library that combines
             Unicode-aware pre-segmentation with BPE/SentencePiece subword encoding, reversible
             joiner/spacer markup, and vocabulary-restricted recursive OOV splitting.
innovation:  Fully reversible, lossless tokenization: every segmentation decision (joins, spaces, case, script
             changes) is encoded as in-band modifier characters (joiner U+FFED, spacer U+2581,
             case markups, protected-region brackets) so the original text reconstructs
             byte-exactly from the token stream. This separates a reversible surface encoding from
             the compression model, exactly the property nest needs for a tokenizer-backed
             compression codec.
techniques:
  - BPE merge table as two hash maps: _codes (concatenated pair string -> merge rank/priority,
    lower rank applied earlier) for O(1) priority lookup, and _codes_reverse (pair -> its two
    constituent tokens) to undo merges
  - Greedy min-rank merge loop: precompute scores for all adjacent pairs (rank or INT_MAX),
    repeatedly merge lowest-rank pair, incrementally update only the two neighbor scores, stop
    when best_score==INT_MAX
  - Vocabulary-restricted recursive_split: OOV tokens decomposed by reversing BPE merges via
    _codes_reverse until every piece is in-vocab or atomic, giving a deterministic bounded token
    vocabulary
  - Reversible joiner/spacer in-band markup (U+FFED joiner, U+2581 spacer) for lossless
    detokenization without storing original spans separately
  - case_feature / case_markup: factor casing out as a separate channel (C/L/U/M/N tags or soft
    uppercase-region markers) so the subword model sees lowercase only, shrinking effective vocab
  - Protected sequences (U+FF9F brackets) and placeholders that bypass tokenization entirely for
    exact passthrough of opaque spans
  - Unicode-type pre-segmentation (aggressive mode) via ICU: split on script/character-type change
    and digit boundaries, deterministic and language-aware
  - BPE-dropout: stochastically skip merges during training for subword regularization
nest steals: Adapt the reversible-encoding plus BPE-merge-table pattern into nest-format as a new additive
             encoding id (in the reserved 4-255 space) for the chunks_canonical(text) section:
             ship a frozen deterministic BPE merge table (two flat little-endian arrays mirroring
             _codes and _codes_reverse, repr(C)) plus a token-id stream, replacing raw/zstd text
             bytes with subword token ids that zstd then compresses better (smaller alphabet,
             repeated merges). The reversible joiner/spacer markup is the key piece: it lets nest
             reconstruct exact canonical text from token ids, preserving content_hash stability
             and chunks_original_spans round-tripping. The vocabulary-restricted recursive_split
             gives a deterministic, seed-free, bounded token vocabulary satisfying nest's
             determinism constraint. Separately, case_feature/case_markup factoring is stealable
             for nest-runtime bm25::Bm25Index reversible case normalization, and the
             Unicode-type/script-change pre-segmenter is a concrete fix for the known bm25
             CJK/Thai word-segmentation gap. Keep C++/ICU out: reimplement the merge loop in safe
             Rust (no panic, typed thiserror) in a sub-300-line module under
             crates/nest-format/src/encoding/.
compression: Directly relevant. Subword tokenization is an entropy-reduction front-end: replacing UTF-8 byte
             runs with token ids over a small learned vocabulary shrinks the symbol alphabet and
             exposes long repeated merges, so a downstream zstd pass on token ids compresses text
             notably better than zstd on raw bytes, while reversible markup keeps it lossless and
             content_hash-stable. The vocabulary cap bounds dictionary size for tiny-preset goals.
             This is a complementary codec layer ahead of nest's existing zstd path for the text
             sections only (never embeddings).
perf:        C++11 core with ICU for Unicode; encoding is O(chars x merges) per word with no word->subword
             cache (computed fresh per request, so a Rust port should add a word-keyed LRU cache).
             Hash-map lookups O(1); merge loop updates neighbor scores incrementally rather than
             recomputing all pairs. Single-threaded per token; Python overhead amortized via the
             pyonmttok C extension. No SIMD in the tokenizer hot path (branchy string work), so
             nest gains nothing for its SIMD dot-product path here, only for the build-time text
             codec.
license:     MIT

# category: model-runtime  (3 repos)
# focus: inference runtimes and tensor libraries for on-device embedding

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MUST] ggerganov/ggml
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A dependency-free C/C++ tensor library for on-device inference whose real innovation is its
             block-quantization codec family (legacy Qn_0, K-quants with double-quantized
             super-block scales, and i-quants with shared codebook grids) packed into the
             mmap-aligned GGUF on-disk format and dequantized straight from disk by AVX2/NEON
             kernels.
innovation:  Hierarchical "double quantization" inside fixed-size super-blocks: a tensor is split into
             256-value super-blocks, each holding one or two fp16 master factors (d for scale,
             dmin for offset), and within that, eight 32-value sub-blocks whose own scale and min
             are stored as 6-bit integers relative to the master factors. This piecewise-affine
             scheme captures both global and local dynamic range at ~4.5 bits/weight (Q4_K)
             instead of fp16's 16, with metadata overhead amortized across 256 values, and every
             block is a self-contained repr(C) record dequantizable in isolation directly off
             mmap'd memory.
techniques:
  - Q4_K super-block: struct{ fp16 d; fp16 dmin; uint8 scales[12] (6-bit packed scales+mins for 8
    subblocks); uint8 qs[128] (4-bit nibble quants) } over 256 values = 4.5 bpw
  - Double-quantization: per-32 sub-block scale/min stored as 6-bit ints reconstructed as
    d*scale_idx and dmin*min_idx, so only two fp16 master values per 256 weights
  - block_q8_0: struct{ fp16 d; int8 qs[32] } symmetric per-32 int8 with one fp16 scale = direct
    upgrade to nest's flat int8 codec
  - block_q6_K split-bit packing: ql[128] low 4 bits, qh[64] high 2 bits, int8 scales[16], one
    fp16 d (6.5625 bpw)
  - i-quants (IQ2_XXS, 2.0625 bpw): vector codewords indexed into shared lookup grids
    (iq2xxs_grid, 64-bit packed entries) so weights cost log2(grid) bits not raw bits
  - GGUF format: magic 0x46554747, u32 version, tensor-info table (name,dims,type,u64 offset),
    every tensor 32-byte aligned for SIMD-loadable mmap, little-endian default
  - Block-local self-containment: no cross-block state, enabling zero-allocation streaming dequant
    and random access to any super-block from disk
  - Importance-matrix (imatrix) quantization: error minimized against per-channel activation
    importance not raw MSE, so bits go where they matter
nest steals: Steal the K-quant super-block scheme as a new ENCODING ID (format reserves ids 4-255 inside v1, so
             this is additive, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump) applied ONLY to the embeddings section
             in nest-format (crates/nest-format/src/encoding.rs plus a new block-codec module
             under the 333-line cap). Today nest's hottest, largest section uses flat float16 or
             per-vector int8 and is deliberately left out of zstd because it is scored from mmap
             on the hot path. A Q4_K-style codec (256-dim super-blocks: fp16 d/dmin + 6-bit
             subblock scales + 4-bit quants) cuts embeddings from 16 bpw to ~4.5 bpw, ~3.5x shrink
             of the dominant section, while preserving the invariant: each repr(C) little-endian
             super-block is self-contained and dequantized inline by the existing nest-runtime
             SIMD dispatcher (AVX2/NEON/scalar) during the dot product, so vectors stay scored
             directly from mmap with no decompress-to-heap step. The mandatory exact-cosine rerank
             in nest-runtime's search path stays ground truth since the codec only changes
             candidate-vector storage; pair it with a 'nano' preset (Q4_K embeddings + zstd text +
             hnsw) targeting a size ratio well under tiny's 0.283. Determinism holds: fix
             super-block size at 256, quantization is a pure function of input (no seed), emit
             blocks in tensor order so same chunks+model fingerprint give byte-identical files.
             block_q8_0 is the lower-risk first step: a drop-in higher-quality replacement for
             nest's int8 codec (one fp16 scale per 32 dims vs per-vector), shippable immediately.
             Note: K-quants require the quantized dim be a multiple of 256 (768=3*256, 1024=4*256
             are fine; 384 is not, so fall back to block-32 Q8_0 for non-multiples).
compression: Directly central to the north star. Embeddings are nest's largest section and the only one
             excluded from compression; K-quant/i-quant block codecs are the proven way to take
             that section from 16 bpw to 4.5 (Q4_K) or ~2 (IQ2_XXS) while keeping it scoreable in
             place off mmap. This is the single biggest available win on the 'compress more than
             everyone' axis without touching the frozen layout, since it slots in as an additive
             encoding id. The 6-bit-scale double-quantization trick is the key insight: spend
             metadata bits hierarchically so per-subblock precision is nearly free across 256
             values.
perf:        Blocks are designed for zero runtime allocation and SIMD: Q4_0/Q8_0 use 32-value blocks aligned
             for one AVX2 256-bit / NEON 128-bit load; K-quants use 256-value super-blocks so
             dequant-and-dot fuses without spilling. GGUF mandates per-tensor 32-byte alignment so
             mmap'd block arrays start on SIMD boundaries. Dequant is a multiply-add against the
             fp16 master scale, cheap relative to the dot product, so the codec adds little
             hot-path cost. Constraint: K-quants need dim a multiple of 256 else fall back to
             block-32 Q8_0.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] microsoft/onnxruntime
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        Cross-platform ML inference runtime whose CPU math library (MLAS) executes weight-only quantized
             matmuls via the MatMulNBits operator: block-wise sub-byte (int4/int8) weights with
             separate per-block scale/zero-point tensors, fused dequant-and-dot SIMD kernels
             (SQNBitGemm) dispatched per CPU feature level.
innovation:  Block-wise (group-wise) weight quantization: instead of one scale per whole vector, the
             contraction axis is cut into fixed-size blocks (e.g. 16/32/64/128 elements), each
             block gets its own f16 scale (and optional zero-point), and sub-byte codes are
             bit-packed (two int4 nibbles per byte) into a [N, k_blocks, blob_size] layout. The
             decisive companion idea is the FUSED kernel: SQNBitGemm unpacks nibbles, applies the
             per-block scale, and accumulates the dot product inside one SIMD pass, so the
             dequantized weights never materialize in memory. Accuracy stays high at int4 because
             each small block tracks local dynamic range, killing the single-outlier problem that
             ruins per-vector scaling.
techniques:
  - Block-wise / group-wise quantization: one scale per K-block (block_size power-of-2, >=16), not
    per whole vector
  - Sub-byte bit-packing: two int4 codes packed per byte, blob_size = block_size/2; generalizes
    via blob_size = block_size/packed_size
  - Separate parallel tensors: packed weights, _scales (one f16 per block), optional _zero_points;
    dequant = (q - zp) * scale
  - [N, k_blocks, blob_size] block-major layout so each block's codes are contiguous for streaming
    SIMD unpack
  - MLAS SQNBitGemm fused dequantize+dot kernel: nibble unpack + scale + MAC in one SIMD pass, no
    materialized fp weights
  - Runtime CPU feature dispatch ladder: scalar -> NEON/dotprod -> AVX2+FMA -> AVX512-VNNI,
    picking widest kernel
  - accuracy_level knob: accumulate dot products in fp32/fp16/bf16/int8 to trade recall vs speed
  - Symmetric (int4, implicit zp=0) vs asymmetric (uint4, explicit zp) modes; RTN/GPTQ/HQQ
    scale-selection calibration
nest steals: nest-format: add a new int4 embeddings encoding (an additive encoding id in the RESERVED 4-255
             range, leaving the FROZEN v1 header untouched) modeled on
             crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs. Today int8.rs uses scale_kind=0 = ONE f32
             scale per whole vector; introduce scale_kind=1 = block-wise: split each dim-length
             row into fixed blocks (e.g. 64), store one f16 scale per block (reuse the existing
             float16 codec), and bit-pack two int4 codes per byte in a block-contiguous [n,
             dim/block, block/2] layout. This same block-wise scheme should ALSO be retrofitted
             onto the existing int8 path as scale_kind=1 to recover accuracy int8 currently loses
             to per-vector outliers. nest-runtime: add a fused dequant+dot kernel beside
             crates/nest-runtime/src/simd/{neon,avx2,scalar}.rs that streams nibbles, applies the
             per-block f16 scale, and accumulates cosine straight off mmap, never expanding to f32
             in RAM, mirroring SQNBitGemm. Determinism holds because round-to-nearest-even max-abs
             per block is fully reproducible; split pack/unpack from the kernel to stay under the
             300-line cap. The mandatory exact-cosine rerank in search.rs already absorbs
             quantization error, so int4 can drive the candidate stage while ground-truth scores
             stay real cosine. This unlocks a new "nano" preset roughly halving the tiny preset's
             0.283 embedding footprint (int4 = 4 bits + ~0.25 bits/dim f16 scale at block 64 vs
             int8's 8 bits/dim) -- the single biggest remaining lever on nest's
             compress-more-than-everyone north star, since embeddings are the one section that
             CANNOT be zstd-compressed.
compression: Directly central. Embeddings are nest's largest incompressible section (zstd is banned on the hot
             path), so the only way to shrink them is better quantization. Block-wise int4 roughly
             halves bytes-per-dim vs the current int8 codec (~4.25 vs 8 bits/dim including the
             per-block f16 scale) while the per-block scale plus mandatory exact-cosine rerank
             protect recall. Pushing the tiny/nano preset below the 0.283 size ratio is the most
             concrete near-term win on the 'compress data more than everyone' goal.
perf:        SQNBitGemm fusion is the perf crux: nibble unpack + per-block scale + multiply-accumulate happen
             in one SIMD pass so dequantized weights never hit memory, keeping the matmul
             memory-bandwidth bound. MLAS dispatches at runtime across a feature ladder
             (NEON/dotprod, AVX2+FMA+F16C, AVX512-BW/VNNI, AMX) -- the int8 VNNI dot path
             (vpdpbusd) does 4 int8 MACs per lane and maps cleanly onto nest's simd/mod.rs
             dispatcher. Block size trades footprint vs accuracy (smaller block = more scales =
             bigger but more accurate); block_size 64-128 is the typical sweet spot. f16 scales
             (not f32) keep scale overhead near 0.25 bits/dim at block 64.
license:     MIT

[HIGH] onnx/onnx
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        ONNX is a protobuf-defined interchange format for ML graphs whose TensorProto/external-data
             subsystem is a battle-tested spec for serializing quantized tensors
             (int4/int8/float8/MX microscaling) as raw, page-aligned, mmap-friendly byte blobs
             referenced by location/offset/length.
innovation:  The MX microscaling block-quant scheme: shared 8-bit power-of-two block exponent (FLOAT8E8M0) over
             sub-byte (int4/fp4) packed elements, decoupling a cheap shared scale from per-element
             mantissa for near-int8 quality at int4 size; standardized on disk as raw_data
             nibble-packing plus page-aligned mmap-able external byte ranges.
techniques:
  - MX microscaling: 32-element blocks share one FLOAT8E8M0 power-of-two scale, per-element
    int4/fp4 mantissa, 2-stage dequant
  - int4/uint4 packed 2 nibbles/byte two's-complement; bf16/fp8 bit-cast to unsigned before
    serialize
  - raw_data little-endian native blob as single bulk serialization path bypassing protobuf
    overhead
  - External data: (location, offset, length, SHA1 checksum) referencing a flat weight sidecar by
    byte range
  - Page-aligned offsets (4KB / 64KB Windows) via round-up formula to enable zero-copy mmap of
    initializers
  - size_threshold gate: only tensors >= threshold spill to raw/external, small ones inline
  - Opset/version gating: new dtype force-upgrades consumer opset so readers know required
    capability
  - Named float8 variant zoo (E4M3FN, E5M2, FNUZ) as explicit encodings, not one lossy float8
nest steals: Two additive grafts in nest-format/src/encoding/ (use a reserved encoding id 4-255, no v2 bump).
             (1) New block-scaled sub-byte embedding codec generalizing int8.rs: replace its
             per-vector f32 scale with int4 nibbles packed 2/byte plus a shared power-of-two u8
             (E8M0-style) scale per 32-dim block; ~halves embeddings vs int8 while block scales
             recover int4-flat recall, pushing the 'tiny' preset below 0.283. Keep raw (never zstd
             embeddings) and fuse block dequant (single exponent add/shift) into the SIMD dot
             kernel in nest-runtime/src/simd before f32 accumulate, under AVX2/NEON. (2) Don't
             copy ONNX external-data (nest's single-file 64-byte-aligned section table in
             reader/parse.rs already beats the sidecar model); instead steal the size_threshold
             gate idea into writer/encoding_choice.rs so tiny sections skip block-quant overhead,
             and keep the per-section SHA-256 checksum discipline that mirrors ONNX's per-tensor
             checksum.
compression: Directly central. MX block-scaling + int4 packing is the single highest-leverage path to push the
             embeddings section (the dominant byte cost in a vector DB) below int8 without the
             recall collapse of flat int4. It targets the exact 'compress more than everyone'
             north star and the 'tiny' preset, is implementable as an additive encoding id within
             frozen v1, and keeps embeddings raw/SIMD-scored from mmap. The float8 variant
             taxonomy (E4M3 vs E5M2 vs E8M0-scale) is the proven design space to copy rather than
             reinvent.
perf:        raw_data + page-aligned external offsets exist specifically for zero-copy mmap of large weights
             (nest already does this with 64-byte SECTION_ALIGNMENT, single-file). E8M0
             block-scale dequant is one exponent add/shift per block (32 elems), cheap to fuse
             into an AVX2/NEON dot-product kernel before f32 accumulation. int4 packing halves
             embedding bytes vs int8; block scaling restores the recall flat-int4 loses. No native
             NumPy dtype for int4/fp8 (uses ml_dtypes), so the packing/unpacking is hand-rolled
             bit ops -- nest would implement the same in Rust.
license:     Apache-2.0
