--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] langchain-ai/langchain
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python/JS LLM-orchestration framework whose retrieval layer contributes two reusable,
             format-agnostic ideas: a content-hash record-manager indexing API for incremental
             dedup/sync of a vector store, and a content-hash-keyed embedding cache namespaced by
             model fingerprint.
innovation:  The "record manager" indexing API: every derived document carries a deterministic hash of
             (canonical content + metadata), plus a source_id and write timestamp. Re-indexing
             computes hashes, skips any hash already present (dedup), deletes stale hashes that
             share a touched source_id (incremental cleanup), and can scope cleanup to seen
             sources (scoped_full). This turns a write-once vector store into an idempotent,
             incrementally-syncable one keyed purely on content hashes, with no re-embed of
             unchanged content. It pairs with CacheBackedEmbeddings, which keys the embedding
             KV-cache on hash(text) prefixed by a model-name namespace so a model change
             invalidates cleanly.
techniques:
  - Record-manager dedup: store per-chunk content_hash + source_id + write_time;
    skip-if-hash-present, delete-stale-by-source_id
  - Three cleanup policies: incremental (delete superseded chunks of touched sources
    continuously), full (delete anything not in this run's universe), scoped_full (in-memory set
    of seen source_ids)
  - source_id_key grouping so all chunks derived from one source document update/delete atomically
  - force_update flag to invalidate the index on embedding-model change without changing content
  - CacheBackedEmbeddings: cache key = namespace(model fingerprint) + hex(SHA-256(text)); only
    cache misses hit the embedder
  - Configurable key encoder (sha1/blake2b/sha256/sha512) trading collision-resistance vs speed
  - SemanticChunker: embed sentences, split where cosine distance between consecutive embeddings
    exceeds a percentile/stddev/IQR/gradient breakpoint
  - RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter: ordered separator hierarchy [paragraph,line,word,char]
    recursive descent until under chunk_size, then greedy merge with bounded overlap
nest steals: nest-format already computes content_hash (decoded canonical sections, stable across encodings)
             and per-chunk chunk_ids; this is exactly LangChain's record-manager key. Add an
             optional section (reserved id 0x09+, additive within v1) holding a sorted manifest of
             {chunk_content_hash -> source_id, original_span_ref} so a future `nest update`/`nest
             merge` in nest-cli can do incremental edits: re-embed ONLY chunks whose content_hash
             is absent, copy embeddings/spans for unchanged hashes byte-for-byte from the old
             mmap, and drop chunks whose source_id was touched but hash vanished. This delivers
             the mission's missing real-time edit while preserving determinism (sorted hash order
             = byte-identical output for identical inputs) and zero-cost dedup. Mirror
             CacheBackedEmbeddings as a deterministic on-disk python-side cache in nest-python
             keyed by namespace(model fingerprint already in provenance) +
             SHA-256(chunk_canonical_text) so rebuilds and cross-corpus dedup never re-embed
             identical text. For compression: identical-chunk dedup across files plus span-deduped
             storage shrinks multi-document/whole-repo .nest containers (north-star use case) by
             collapsing repeated boilerplate to one physical chunk+embedding referenced by many
             spans.
compression: Indirect but real: content-hash dedup collapses repeated/boilerplate chunks (and their fp32
             embeddings, the largest section) to one physical copy referenced by many spans -- the
             biggest lever when packing whole repos/datasets/databases into one container. The
             embedding cache avoids recompute, not bytes, but cross-file hash dedup is a genuine
             size win for multi-document containers. Semantic chunking yields more self-similar
             chunks that zstd compresses better in non-embedding sections.
perf:        Record manager is normally SQLite-backed (SQLRecordManager) but the algorithm is just a hash set +
             source_id index -- nest can implement it as a sorted in-file manifest scanned at
             build time, no DB, no socket, offline by construction. Hash cost is one SHA-256 per
             chunk (nest already does this for content_hash, near-zero marginal cost). LangChain's
             incremental cleanup has O(redundant) cost when a source's chunks span batches; nest
             builds the whole corpus in-process so a single batch avoids it. Time-based cleanup
             races are irrelevant since nest builds are single-shot and deterministic, not
             clock-driven.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] langchain-ai/langgraph
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Pregel/BSP graph-execution runtime for stateful LLM agents whose durable-execution layer
             persists state as immutable, append-only checkpoints with content-deduplicated
             channel blobs keyed by per-channel version numbers.
innovation:  Version-keyed blob deduplication for append-only state history: each checkpoint stores only a tiny
             channel_versions int-map plus references; the actual serialized channel values live
             in a separate blobs table keyed by (channel_name, version). A new checkpoint that
             touches one channel bumps only that channel's version and writes one new blob; all
             unchanged channels keep pointing at existing blob bytes. This stores a full
             mutation/edit history with near-zero duplication of unchanged state, instead of
             rewriting the whole snapshot per step.
techniques:
  - Pregel/BSP super-step loop: Plan (select actors whose subscribed channels changed) -> Execute
    (run nodes in parallel, writes invisible until step end) -> Update (apply reducers) ->
    Checkpoint; transactional/atomic per super-step
  - Channel abstraction with typed update functions: LastValue (single value/step),
    BinaryOperatorAggregate (associative reducer applied eagerly at write time), Topic (pubsub
    accumulate/dedup), DeltaChannel (reducer applied lazily on reconstruction from persisted
    writes)
  - Immutable append-only checkpoint store: INSERT per super-step keyed by composite (thread_id,
    checkpoint_id); never UPDATE in place; enables time-travel and resume
  - channel_versions monotonic version map + get_next_version() (default integer sequence from 1,
    overridable) used as the dedup key for blobs
  - Separate blobs table mapping (channel, version) -> serialized bytes so unchanged channels are
    referenced, not copied, across checkpoints
  - Pending/task-level writes: outputs of completed nodes in a failed super-step are persisted as
    task writes so resume skips already-done work (fault-tolerant partial progress)
  - JsonPlusSerializer: ormsgpack primary binary encoding with extended-JSON fallback for complex
    types (pydantic, datetime, enums); pluggable SerializerProtocol, optional EncryptedSerializer
    (AES)
  - Security lesson: CWE-502 RCE via constructor-style deserialization in the JSON fallback path;
    fixed with a strict module/class allow-list (LANGGRAPH_STRICT_MSGPACK)
nest steals: Adapt the (channel,version)->blob dedup model into nest-format to unlock nest's "real-time edit"
             north-star without breaking the FROZEN v1 single-file/deterministic guarantees.
             Concretely: add an OPTIONAL additive section (reserved id 0x09+) that is an
             append-only edit log, where each logical chunk/embedding is content-addressed by its
             existing per-section SHA-256 (nest already has section checksum + content_hash). A
             new edit appends only new/changed chunk and embedding blobs; unchanged chunks are
             referenced by their existing content hash rather than recopied. The current
             chunk_ids/chunks_canonical/embeddings sections become the version-0 base, and a small
             version map (analogous to channel_versions, repr(C) LE-u64 ids) records which blob
             hash is live per chunk. This gives nest incremental/versioned containers and cheap
             snapshots while keeping deterministic byte-identical rebuilds, and it compounds
             compression: dedup-before-encode means zstd/int8/f16 only ever encode the delta.
             nest-runtime's reader resolves the live version map at mmap time so SIMD dot products
             still run straight off the latest blobs with no copy. Borrow the get_next_version()
             integer-sequence determinism (seeded/monotonic) to keep the additive log
             reproducible, and adopt the allow-list deserialization lesson: nest must never
             deserialize arbitrary types from a container (typed thiserror, fixed section decoders
             only), avoiding LangGraph's CWE-502 class of bug by construction.
compression: High. Version-keyed content-addressed blob dedup is a compression primitive: storing edit/version
             history of a corpus by referencing unchanged content-hashed chunks instead of
             recopying them is exactly the structural (cross-snapshot) dedup nest needs to
             "compress more than everyone" once it supports real-time edit and whole-repo/database
             ingestion, where most of a new version is unchanged. It composes with nest's existing
             zstd/int8/f16 codecs (dedup first, then encode the delta) and reuses nest's existing
             SHA-256 content_hash as the dedup key.
perf:        Checkpoint store is INSERT-only (append-only), trading write amplification of metadata for
             immutability and time-travel; channel blobs are written once per version and shared,
             so steady-state writes are O(changed channels) not O(state size). Serialization uses
             ormsgpack (C, fast) with orjson fast paths. No SIMD/quantization in LangGraph itself
             (it is a Python orchestration layer, not a vector engine); the transferable value is
             purely the on-disk dedup/versioning data model, not any kernel. Known operational
             pain point in their Postgres saver is checkpoint bloat from append-only writes, which
             nest avoids by keeping the log inside one mmap-friendly additive section rather than
             per-row DB inserts.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] run-llama/llama_index
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python RAG/agent data framework whose core abstraction is the Node (a chunk with typed
             relationships, metadata, and a content/text hash id) wired into a swappable
             StorageContext (docstore + index_store + vector_store), with retrieval strategies
             including hierarchical multi-resolution chunking, auto-merging retrieval, and
             reciprocal-rank fusion hybrid search.
innovation:  The HierarchicalNodeParser + AutoMergingRetriever pair: documents are parsed into a
             multi-resolution tree of chunks (e.g. chunk_sizes [2048,512,128] tokens) where every
             child stores a PARENT relationship to a coarser node; only leaf nodes are
             embedded/searched, and at query time the retriever counts how many sibling leaves
             under a common parent were hit -- if a majority threshold is crossed it recursively
             replaces those leaves with the single larger parent node. This decouples
             embedding/search granularity (fine) from returned/materialized granularity (coarse);
             the parent-child relationship graph is the load-bearing data structure.
techniques:
  - HierarchicalNodeParser: one document -> N levels of chunks (2048/512/128 tokens) with explicit
    child->parent references
  - Node relationship enum: SOURCE / PARENT / CHILD / PREVIOUS / NEXT links forming a chunk DAG
    via RelatedNodeInfo (target node_id + hash)
  - AutoMergingRetriever majority-threshold merge: if > threshold of a parent's children are
    retrieved, recursively collapse children into the parent node
  - Leaf-only embedding: only finest chunks are vector-indexed; coarser nodes live in the docstore
    and are fetched by id, not embedded
  - Reciprocal-rank fusion (RECIPROCAL_RANK mode, k=60.0, score += 1/(k+rank+1)) to merge vector +
    BM25 lists without an external model
  - Content-hash node ids (id = hash of text) giving stable dedup keys across retriever lists
    during fusion
  - StorageContext split into docstore / index_store / vector_store so relationship graph and
    embeddings persist independently
  - fsspec-backed JSON persistence so the same index serializes to local FS, S3, R2 unchanged
nest steals: Add an optional RESERVED section (id 0x09+, additive within format v1) named chunk_hierarchy to
             nest-format/src/sections/: a repr(C), little-endian array of (child_chunk_id u32 ->
             parent_chunk_id u32, level u8) edges plus per-parent child-count, hashed as its own
             integrity surface. The python builder emits the tree; only leaf chunks get rows in
             embeddings/hnsw_index, matching nest's existing leaf-search model. In
             nest-runtime/src/search.rs, add a post-rerank auto-merge pass: after the mandatory
             exact-cosine rerank, count retrieved leaves per parent and, above a deterministic
             majority threshold, swap them for the parent before materialize() resolves
             chunks_original_spans. This reuses chunk_ids + chunks_original_spans already in the
             frozen format and stays offline/deterministic. Compression angle: parent canonical
             text need NOT be stored -- a parent's chunks_canonical is reconstructed by
             concatenating its children's chunks_original_spans against the source, so the
             hierarchy gives coarse-context retrieval at ~9 bytes/edge of graph and zero extra
             text bytes, directly serving 'compress more than everyone' while adding
             multi-resolution retrieval. Do NOT re-steal RRF: nest already has it in
             crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25/fusion.rs (k=60).
compression: High and non-obvious: the parent-child edge table is ~9 bytes per edge, and because a parent's
             text is fully derivable from its children's existing chunks_original_spans, the
             multi-resolution hierarchy adds coarse-context retrieval with near-zero additional
             text/embedding bytes (no parent text stored, no parent embedding stored). It turns
             one stored leaf layer into multiple retrievable resolutions -- improving
             recall-context per byte rather than spending bytes, a pure structural win for the
             compression north star.
perf:        The auto-merge pass is O(retrieved_k) hashmap counting after rerank, negligible vs the SIMD dot
             products; no extra disk reads since parent text is reconstructed from already-mmapped
             spans. Leaf-only embedding keeps the embeddings section (the hot mmap-scored part
             nest never compresses) strictly minimal: coarser context costs no embedding rows.
             llama_index itself is pure Python and not perf-relevant -- the value is the algorithm
             and data model, not its implementation.
license:     MIT

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] run-llama/semtools
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust CLI for document parsing (LlamaParse API) plus local, API-free semantic line search built
             on model2vec static embeddings (potion-multilingual-128M, distilled from bge-m3),
             simsimd SIMD cosine kernels, and an embedded qdrant-edge IVF_PQ index with a
             persistent on-disk workspace embedding cache.
innovation:  Replacing a neural embedding model with a precomputed static token-vector lookup table
             (model2vec/POTION): distill a transformer into per-token vectors via PCA + frequency
             weighting, then embed any text by mean-pooling its token vectors. This makes
             embedding generation pure table lookup + averaging (no matmul, no GPU, no network,
             fully deterministic), exactly the offline/sovereign/reproducible property nest needs
             to own its embedding step.
techniques:
  - model2vec static embeddings: forward-pass vocab through sentence transformer (bge-m3),
    mean-pool output positions per token, PCA to 256 dims, Zipf weighting using vocab rank as
    frequency proxy (IDF-like downweighting of frequent tokens)
  - POTION/Tokenlearn post-training: re-regularize with PCA + SIF weighting w=1e-3/(1e-3+proba)
    for accuracy boost over plain Zipf
  - Uncontextualized inference: embed = mean of constituent token vectors (single gather +
    average, no attention, no forward pass at query time)
  - simsimd 6.5.1 SIMD cosine/dot kernels (AVX2/NEON/SVE) for similarity scoring
  - qdrant-edge embedded IVF_PQ: inverted-file coarse quantization + product quantization of
    residuals, serverless in-process ANN
  - persistent workspace embedding cache in ~/.semtools/workspaces/ via serde serialization with
    automatic re-embed when source documents change
  - per-line search with configurable context window (--n-lines) and --max-distance cosine
    threshold instead of fixed-size chunking
nest steals: Two distinct steals. (1) nest-python (or a tiny nest-runtime module): vendor a model2vec-style
             static embedder as an offline, deterministic, zero-dependency embedding backend. A
             potion token-vector table (vocab x 256 f32, nest-encoded as int8+zstd) ships inside
             or beside the .nest file; provenance.model_fingerprint becomes the table hash.
             Embedding = tokenize + gather + mean-pool, giving byte-identical builds with no
             network and no PyTorch, matching nest's deterministic/reproducible/sovereign
             guarantees and removing a heavy external dependency. (2) nest-format +
             nest-runtime/ann: add IVF-PQ as a new optional encoding tier for the embeddings
             section (new reserved encoding id 4-255 and/or section id 0x09+, additive within v1).
             Product quantization (m subvectors, 256-centroid k-means codebook per subspace, 1
             byte/subvector) compresses 256-d f32 ~16-32x vs raw and far below the current int8.
             Because nest already mandates an exact-cosine rerank after ANN, PQ distances are only
             a candidate funnel and the returned score stays real cosine, satisfying
             search_contract; seed k-means to keep it deterministic like the seeded-hnsw rule.
compression: Very high. The IVF-PQ/product-quantization tier directly advances the compress-more-than-everyone
             north star: PQ encodes a 256-d float vector in ~8-16 bytes (m x 1 byte) vs 256 bytes
             raw f32 or 64 bytes int8, a 16-32x cut on the embeddings section (usually the
             largest). With nest's mandatory exact rerank, recall stays high while stored vectors
             shrink dramatically, going deeper than the tiny preset's int8. Separately, the
             static-embedding table is itself highly compressible (int8+zstd on a vocab x 256
             matrix) and replaces a multi-GB neural model with a few-MB lookup table, shrinking
             the whole embedding toolchain footprint.
perf:        model2vec inference is reportedly up to ~500x faster than the source transformer with a ~15-50x
             smaller model; embedding is bandwidth-bound table gather + mean, ideal for CPU-only
             offline use. potion-multilingual-128M: 256 dims, 101 languages, ~90.9% of LaBSE
             quality, distilled from bge-m3. simsimd 6.5.1 ships AVX2/NEON/SVE cosine kernels
             aligning with nest's AVX2/NEON/scalar dispatcher (could cross-check or replace
             hand-rolled SIMD). IVF_PQ keeps the ANN index small and CPU-resident for serverless
             search. Caveat: like nest's bm25, model2vec tokenization quality on CJK/Thai depends
             on the underlying tokenizer (bge-m3 sentencepiece gives decent multilingual
             coverage).
license:     MIT (run-llama/semtools and model2vec-rs); potion-multilingual-128M weights MIT (MinishLab);
             simsimd Apache-2.0; qdrant-edge Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] infiniflow/ragflow (+ sibling engine infiniflow/infinity, Apache-2.0)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        RAGFlow is a layout-aware RAG engine (DeepDoc parser + template chunking) backed by the Infinity
             AI-native database, which serves a four-way hybrid search (dense vector via HNSW+LVQ,
             sparse vector, BM25 full-text, and ColBERT tensor/late-interaction) fused by RRF /
             weighted-sum / tensor reranking.
innovation:  Two-stage Local Vector Quantization (LVQ) for HNSW: instead of plain per-vector affine int8, first
             subtract the corpus mean and quantize the mean-centered residual (stage 1, used for
             graph construction and traversal), then quantize the second-order residual of that
             difference (stage 2, used only in the final similarity refinement). Because every
             vector is offset by the same global mean and scaled per-vector, the nearest-neighbor
             ordering is preserved, so the HNSW graph can be built and traversed entirely on the
             compressed stage-1 codes -- no full-precision vectors kept in RAM -- cutting memory
             bandwidth and footprint while recall stays intact. Complementary idea: ColBERT
             multi-vector reranking with binary quantization, where each float becomes one bit
             (1/32 size) and MaxSim is computed with Hamming distance, also ranking-preserving.
techniques:
  - LVQ: subtract global corpus mean, then per-vector scalar-quantize the residual; build/traverse
    HNSW on stage-1 codes only
  - Two-stage residual quantization: stage-1 codes for traversal, stage-2 residual codes for
    final-score refinement
  - ColBERT late-interaction: per-token multi-vector (tensor) doc representation, MaxSim = sum
    over query tokens of max token-to-token similarity
  - Binary quantization of tensors: 1 bit/float (1/32 size), MaxSim via Hamming distance,
    ranking-preserving
  - Tensor-array chunking for >128-token docs: split, one tensor per chunk, aggregate MaxSim
    across chunks
  - Three fusion modes: RRF (rank-based, k=60), Weighted Sum (score-normalized), and
    tensor/ColBERT rerank as finer verification
  - EMVB tensor index (ColBERTv2 improvement): centroid pre-filtering + SIMD-accelerated MaxSim
    used as a retriever not just a reranker
nest steals: Three concrete steals. (1) LVQ in nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs + nest-runtime/src/ann + simd:
             today int8.rs does plain per-vector affine quant (scale=max_abs/127, no mean) and
             ann/mod.rs keeps a full f32 `vectors: Vec<f32>` snapshot in RAM for HNSW. Add a new
             additive embeddings encoding id (e.g. 4, RESERVED-safe within v1) int8_lvq: store one
             global f32 mean[dim] in the payload prefix, then per-vector scale + i8 residual
             codes, plus an optional stage-2 residual blob. Then ann/build.rs and ann/search.rs
             traverse on the i8 codes (reuse the existing dot_f32_i8 SIMD kernel) instead of the
             f32 snapshot, deleting the `vectors: Vec<f32>` RAM cost and shrinking the tiny preset
             below 0.283 while keeping recall via the exact-cosine rerank that already runs. (2)
             Weighted-sum fusion: bm25/fusion.rs only has rrf_union; add
             weighted_sum_union(a_scores, b_scores, alpha) with min-max score normalization as a
             second hybrid mode behind a search_contract flag (~30 deterministic lines, no new
             section). (3) ColBERT tensor rerank as a new optional section id 0x09 (tensor_index)
             + a nest-runtime rerank stage: store per-chunk multi-vectors binary-quantized (1
             bit/float), compute MaxSim with Hamming-popcount as an optional final reranker after
             the mandatory exact-cosine pass, unlocking offline token-level semantic reranking
             with no GPU.
compression: Directly central. LVQ is a strictly better embedding codec than nest's current int8:
             mean-centering + per-vector residual scale preserves NN order so the HNSW graph runs
             on compressed codes, eliminating the full-f32 in-RAM snapshot and pushing the tiny
             preset's 0.283 size ratio lower at equal recall@10 (0.992). Binary tensor quant is a
             32x compressor for a new modality (multi-vector reranking) that is itself
             ranking-preserving via Hamming MaxSim. Both fit nest's
             compress-more-than-everyone-while-staying-offline/deterministic/single-file north
             star.
perf:        Infinity benchmarks: HNSW+LVQ ~0.1ms query latency, 15K+ QPS on million-scale vectors; BM25 ~1ms /
             12K+ QPS over 33M docs. LVQ win is reduced memory bandwidth (smaller codes streamed
             during graph walk) plus random-access friendliness suited to graph search. ColBERT
             rerank gives ~100x throughput of cross-encoder rerankers at comparable nDCG;
             binary-quantized MaxSim via Hamming/popcount maps to popcnt (x86_64) / vcnt (NEON),
             both already in nest's simd dispatcher. EMVB adds centroid pre-filter + SIMD MaxSim.
             Practical reranking range is top-100; top-1000 gives no nDCG gain at big latency
             cost.
license:     Apache-2.0 (both ragflow and infinity) -- permissive, safe to vendor/learn from for nest.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] supermemoryai/supermemory
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A cloud-native (Cloudflare Workers + pgvector/D1) "memory engine" for LLMs that unifies RAG, user
             profiles, and a temporally-aware knowledge graph behind one API, using Matryoshka
             (MRL) truncatable embeddings, AST-aware (cAST/tree-sitter) code chunking, and RRF
             hybrid search with cross-encoder reranking under ~200ms.
innovation:  Matryoshka Representation Learning (MRL): one embedding model emits vectors whose dimensions are
             ordered by importance, so a 3072-dim vector can be truncated to a 256/512-dim prefix
             (then L2-renormalized) and still preserve most recall. This turns embedding
             dimensionality into a graceful, model-backed lossy compression knob and enables
             coarse-to-fine two-pass search (cheap truncated dims for candidate recall, full dims
             only to rerank survivors).
techniques:
  - Matryoshka Representation Learning: order-by-importance dims, truncate to prefix (e.g.
    3072->512->256), then L2-renormalize the slice so cosine stays calibrated in the smaller
    subspace
  - Coarse-to-fine two-pass retrieval: recall top-N (e.g. 200) with truncated low-dim vectors,
    then rerank that shortlist with full-dim exact cosine
  - Post-truncation lightweight scalar quantization of the renormalized slice (stacks on top of
    dim reduction)
  - AST-aware chunking (cAST): tree-sitter parses source into an AST and splits on
    function/class/method boundaries, line-based fallback for unsupported languages
  - Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) for vector+keyword hybrid: score = sum 1/(k+rank), k=60; ~91%
    recall@10 before any reranker
  - Optional ColBERT/cross-encoder rerank only on top 20-50 candidates, added after benchmarking
    shows RRF alone misses recall target
  - Eval rigor: hard negatives (500 same-repo distractor files) + IoU>=0.3 relevance threshold to
    avoid trivially-easy retrieval benchmarks
  - Temporal layer: fact contradiction-resolution (overwrite stale facts) and recency-decay
    deprioritization over the knowledge graph
nest steals: MRL is the single biggest steal and maps cleanly onto nest's frozen-but-additive format. (1)
             nest-format: add an MRL-aware dimension layout to the embeddings section. Store the
             full f32/f16 vectors AND record a manifest field `mrl_prefix_dims` (e.g.
             [256,512,full]); because dims are importance-ordered, a truncated prefix is just the
             first K contiguous floats already on disk, so an MRL-trained model lets the `tiny`
             preset drop to a 256-dim int8 prefix for the hot scan with near-zero recall loss.
             This is additive (new optional manifest field / reuses existing embeddings section
             bytes, no NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump) and deterministic. (2) nest-runtime: add a
             coarse-to-fine path in search.rs alongside the existing exact-rerank contract. Pass 1
             runs the SIMD dot-product (AVX2/NEON) over the truncated prefix straight off mmap to
             gather candidates; Pass 2 reuses nest's MANDATORY exact-cosine rerank over the
             full-dim vectors. This fits nest's existing 'ann proxy then real cosine rerank'
             contract perfectly and cuts the hot-loop bytes-touched by 6-12x with no new index
             type. (3) The L2-renormalize-the-slice trick is the load-bearing correctness detail:
             store a per-prefix renorm scale (or renormalize at materialize time) so truncated
             cosine stays honest. Secondary steal: AST-aware chunking belongs in nest-python's
             builder as a tree-sitter-backed chunker so chunks_canonical respects code structure
             (better chunk_ids/spans), with line-based fallback. RRF is already nest's hybrid
             model, so it is reference-only confirmation that k=60 and exact rerank are the right
             defaults.
compression: Directly on-mission. MRL prefix-truncation is a graceful, model-supported lossy compressor for the
             embeddings section, which is nest's largest and the only section that cannot be
             zstd-compressed (it is scored from mmap on the hot path). It is orthogonal to and
             stacks with nest's existing f16/int8 quantization: dim-reduction (3072->256 ~= 12x)
             times int8 (4x) compounds, and the two-pass coarse search means the smaller prefix is
             what the hot SIMD loop actually reads, so it improves BOTH size ratio (toward beating
             the 0.283 tiny preset) AND search throughput while the mandatory full-dim exact
             rerank preserves real-cosine ground truth.
perf:        Supermemory reports end-to-end retrieve+rerank routinely under 200ms using MRL truncation plus
             lightweight quantization; RRF alone reaches ~91% recall@10. For nest: a 256-dim int8
             prefix vs a 3072-dim f32 full vector is ~48x fewer bytes touched per candidate in
             Pass 1, which is pure win for cache-resident SIMD dot products off mmap (AVX2/NEON).
             The key footgun is that truncated prefixes occupy a smaller subspace and
             inflate/jitter cosine, so each slice must be L2-renormalized (scale by 1/||prefix||)
             before scoring; nest should bake this into the writer or materialize path to keep
             determinism.
license:     MIT (supermemory main repo and code-chunk are MIT-licensed); MRL itself is a published technique
             (Kusupati et al.), not encumbered, and tree-sitter is MIT.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] neo4j/neo4j-graphrag-python
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python GraphRAG orchestration library that builds a lexical+entity knowledge graph from text/PDF
             (chunk -> entity -> relation) via schema-guided LLM extraction, then serves it
             through vector, hybrid (vector+fulltext), graph-traversal-Cypher, and text2cypher
             retrievers backed by Neo4j's HNSW vector index and Lucene fulltext index.
innovation:  The "lexical graph" overlay: every chunk becomes a node wired with typed edges (NEXT_CHUNK for
             sequential context, FROM_DOCUMENT to source, FROM_CHUNK/PART_OF_CHUNK to extracted
             entities), so retrieval can expand a vector hit into its neighborhood (prior/next
             chunks, co-mentioned entities) instead of returning isolated chunks. This is the
             minimal, deterministic graph layer that turns a flat chunk store into a navigable
             graph -- exactly what nest needs to add "graph search" without a graph DB.
techniques:
  - Lexical graph: Document->Chunk->Entity nodes with typed edges NEXT_CHUNK, FROM_DOCUMENT,
    FROM_CHUNK, PART_OF_CHUNK (an adjacency overlay over existing chunk_ids)
  - Hybrid fusion via UNION of vector + fulltext branches with max-score normalization (n.score /
    index_max_score) then per-node max() aggregation (the default RRF/'naive' ranker)
  - Linear ranker: final = alpha*norm_vector + (1-alpha)*norm_fulltext, alpha in [0,1] -- one
    tunable weight instead of RRF
  - VectorCypherRetriever pattern: run ANN first, then a parameterized graph-traversal query over
    the (node, score) seeds to pull connected context
  - Schema-guided LLM entity/relation extraction with allowed node/rel/triplet patterns and
    structured Pydantic/JSON output for validation
  - FixedSizeSplitter with chunk_size/chunk_overlap and 'approximate' boundary mode that avoids
    cutting words mid-token
  - Entity resolution tiers: exact-property match, RapidFuzz Levenshtein fuzzy match,
    spaCy-embedding cosine semantic match
  - effective_search_ratio: over-fetch top_k*ratio ANN candidates then trim to recover recall lost
    to approximation (mirrors nest's exact-rerank over-fetch)
nest steals: Three concrete steals. (1) nest-format: add a RESERVED optional section graph_edges (next free id
             0x09) holding a deterministic CSR/adjacency list over chunk_ids -- src=chunk_id u32,
             dst=chunk_id u32, edge_type u8 (0=NEXT_CHUNK, 1=FROM_DOCUMENT, 2=ENTITY_LINK). All
             integers LE unsigned, repr(C), built python-side, sorted by (src,edge_type,dst) for
             byte-identical reproducibility; gets its own per-section SHA-256 and feeds
             content_hash. Additive-only, stays single-file/offline. (2) nest-runtime: a small
             graph::Adjacency reader (mmap, no panic, ~150 loc under the 300-line cap) plus a
             'search-graph' path that seeds from exact-cosine top-k then does a bounded BFS over
             NEXT_CHUNK/ENTITY_LINK edges and re-runs exact cosine rerank on the expanded set so
             returned scores stay real cosine, never an ann proxy. (3) nest-runtime fusion:
             augment the current bm25/hnsw RRF with max-score-normalization + an optional
             linear-alpha ranker (one f32 weight) for a deterministic, tunable vector/text blend.
             nest-cli adds 'search-graph' as a 9th subcommand. Entity extraction stays in
             nest-python build tooling, out of the frozen rust core.
compression: Indirect but real. The lexical-graph adjacency is a compact integer overlay (CSR of u32 chunk ids
             + u8 edge type), highly compressible with zstd as a non-embedding section, and it
             lets nest reconstruct cross-chunk context WITHOUT duplicating overlapping text --
             drop chunk_overlap redundancy from chunks_canonical and recover neighbor context via
             NEXT_CHUNK edges, shrinking the largest text section. The linear/max-norm fusion adds
             zero storage. Entity dedup (fuzzy/exact) collapses repeated mentions, reducing stored
             entity strings. Net: unlocks graph-search and removes overlap redundancy while
             keeping single-file/offline/deterministic guarantees.
perf:        Fusion is pure arithmetic over already-fetched top_k sets (negligible). Graph expansion is a
             bounded BFS over a mmap'd CSR adjacency -- O(seeds * fanout * depth), no hot-path
             allocations if depth/fanout are capped; the cost driver is the exact-cosine rerank
             over the expanded candidate set, which reuses nest's existing SIMD dot products
             (AVX2/NEON/scalar). The over-fetch ratio trades a few extra ANN candidates for
             recall, matching nest's existing exact-rerank-after-ANN design.
license:     Apache-2.0

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] FalkorDB/GraphRAG-SDK
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python GraphRAG orchestration SDK that does schema-guided LLM entity/relation extraction into a
             FalkorDB knowledge graph (a Redis module whose engine stores every relation type as a
             GraphBLAS sparse adjacency matrix and executes Cypher traversals as sparse linear
             algebra), then answers via multi-path retrieval (vector + full-text + text-to-Cypher
             + relationship expansion) merged by ranked fusion and a final local cosine rerank.
innovation:  Represent the graph as typed sparse adjacency matrices (CSC) and turn graph traversal into sparse
             matrix multiplication.
techniques:
  - Typed sparse adjacency matrices: one CSC (compressed-sparse-column) matrix per relation type +
    one type-agnostic global adjacency matrix; node labels as diagonal identity matrices
  - Traversal as semiring matrix multiplication (GraphBLAS): k-hop reachability = matrix power,
    pattern join = A * B * transpose(A); planner compiles Cypher to a matrix expression
  - 3D sparse tensor (src x dst x edge_id) so a cell holds either a single relationship-id scalar
    or a vector of edge-ids for parallel multi-edges between the same pair
  - Copy-on-write versioned/delta matrix: writes accumulate in a delta overlay, flushed/fused into
    the base matrix in batch; enables MVCC snapshot isolation (concurrent reads on snapshots,
    writes serialized through a single mpsc channel)
  - Schema-guided / ontology-constrained extraction: a small ontology (3-7 node types, 5-15
    relations) bounds open-world LLM extraction into a closed, queryable schema
  - Multi-path hybrid retrieval with ranked result merging: vector cosine + DB full-text +
    text-to-Cypher + relationship expansion, candidates fused then reranked
  - Local cosine rerank as the final, non-LLM scoring stage so the returned score is true cosine,
    decoupled from expensive model calls
  - Incremental graph sync: SHA-256 per-document change detection driving idempotent
    apply_changes(added/modified/deleted) via graph merge instead of full rebuild, with MENTIONS
    edges linking entities back to source chunks for provenance
nest steals: See above: section 0x09 graph_adjacency (CSC, per-type matrices) in nest-format; pure-Rust
             deterministic SpMV/SpGEMM k-hop traversal in nest-runtime reusing the SIMD
             dispatcher; expose as a search path fused into the existing RRF + mandatory
             exact-cosine-rerank contract; new nest-cli subcommand (e.g. search-graph) and pyo3
             binding. Avoids vendoring GraphBLAS/Redis; preserves
             offline/single-file/deterministic/additive-format constraints and <300 LOC/file.
compression: High and direct. CSC/CSR sparse-matrix storage IS a compression scheme for graphs: storing only
             nonzeros as (indptr,indices) is O(edges) not O(nodes^2), and for a sparse relation
             graph this is dramatically smaller than an edge-list-of-strings. Combined with nest's
             existing zstd codec on non-embedding sections, the indices arrays (monotone within
             each column) are highly delta+varint+zstd compressible, letting nest carry a whole
             relationship graph in kilobytes and advance the 'compress AND serve
             graphs/databases/repos' mission. The delta-matrix/copy-on-write idea also hints at
             how nest could later support real-time edit without rewriting the frozen base
             (overlay deltas), another north-star goal.
perf:        SpMV/SpGEMM over CSC is bandwidth-bound and extremely cache-friendly vs pointer-chasing; the same
             memory-locality argument nest already uses for mmap'd SIMD dot products applies to
             CSC column dot products, so the existing AVX2/NEON dispatcher can be reused for
             boolean/weighted SpMV. FalkorDB's full GraphBLAS (SuiteSparse) is a large C
             dependency and offline-hostile (Redis module); nest should NOT vendor it -- a minimal
             pure-Rust CSC matvec (tens of lines) covers k-hop traversal without the dependency,
             threading, or MVCC machinery. MVCC/mpsc write serialization is irrelevant to nest's
             frozen, read-only, single-process mmap model and should be skipped.
license:     GraphRAG-SDK is MIT (FalkorDB org). FalkorDB engine core is dual-licensed Server Side Public
             License (SSPL) / commercial -- engine NOT vendorable; only the algorithmic ideas
             (CSC, traversal-as-matmul) are free to reimplement. SuiteSparse:GraphBLAS is
             Apache-2.0 but a heavy C dep to avoid.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] weaviate/recipes
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A collection of ~92% Jupyter notebooks demonstrating Weaviate features and integrations (RAG,
             agents, hybrid/multi-vector search, multi-tenancy) whose only deeply transferable
             systems content is the family of vector-quantization codecs the recipes exercise:
             Product Quantization (PQ), Scalar (SQ), Binary (BQ), and the newest 8-bit Rotational
             Quantization (RQ), all paired with an exact-vector rescoring step.
innovation:  Rotational Quantization: a seeded Fast Walsh-Hadamard rotation before 8-bit scalar quantization
             gaussianizes the vector so a single short quantization interval works for all
             dimensions, giving ~99% recall at 4x compression where naive int8 loses recall, all
             deterministically and with a SIMD integer-dot hot path plus exact rescoring.
techniques:
  - Fast Walsh-Hadamard Transform (FWHT) as O(D log D) pseudo-random orthogonal rotation: 3 rounds
    of blocked FWHT + random sign flips + element swaps, replacing an O(D^2) random orthogonal
    matrix
  - Per-dimension 8-bit scalar quantization into 256 evenly-spaced levels between a stored min/max
    interval, stored as [u8*dim, f32 lo, f32 hi, f32 squared_norm]
  - Asymmetric/expanded inner-product estimate: expand q.x into 4 terms where the dominant term is
    an integer byte-vector dot product done with SIMD (~2.3x faster than f32)
  - Exact-vector rescoring: ANN returns top-m on quantized distance, then recompute exact distance
    on uncompressed vectors for those m to erase recall loss
  - Product Quantization: split D into segments, k-means 256 centroids per segment (8
    bits/segment) into a codebook, Asymmetric Distance Computation via precomputed per-segment
    lookup tables, sparse Fisher-Yates training-set sampling
  - Binary Quantization: 1 bit per dim (sign only), 32x compression, Hamming-distance prefilter
    then rescore
  - Scalar Quantization with data-trained per-dimension buckets rather than a single global scale
nest steals: Adapt RQ to upgrade nest-format's int8 codec (crates/nest-format/src/encoding/int8.rs, which today
             does naive per-vector max-abs scalar quant) into a rotated-int8 codec. Concretely:
             (1) add a deterministic seeded FWHT rotation in nest-format encoding (new helper,
             well under the 300-line cap) applied at build time in nest-python/build_fn.rs before
             quantization; (2) store the rotation seed + per-vector or per-section min/max
             interval in the int8 payload header (the format is additive: bump
             INT8_PAYLOAD_VERSION or add a new scale_kind, NO NEST_FORMAT_VERSION bump, no new
             encoding id needed since it stays encoding=3); (3) at query time in nest-runtime,
             rotate the query with the same seeded FWHT, run the existing SIMD i8 dot kernel over
             mmap, then let the ALREADY-MANDATORY exact cosine rerank in search.rs (score_subset
             on ANN/hybrid candidates) recover full recall against unrotated f32 ground truth.
             This is pure additive change confined to one codec file plus a rotation helper, fully
             deterministic (seeded), offline, single-file. It directly improves the 'tiny' preset:
             same int8/4x footprint but recall jumps toward 0.99+ instead of degrading on
             outlier-heavy embeddings, and could let nest push toward sub-4x (with PQ next)
             without a server. PQ/BQ are the natural follow-on as new encoding ids (4-255 reserved
             range) for even smaller files.
compression: Directly central. nest's north star is 'compress data more than everyone' while serving from mmap.
             RQ is a near-drop-in win: it makes the existing int8/4x path nearly lossless (99%
             recall@10) by adding only a deterministic O(D log D) rotation and a tiny header, and
             it keeps the SIMD-on-mmap hot path (rotation happens at build time on data, only the
             query is rotated at search time). PQ (compute lookup tables, store 8-bit codes per
             segment, ~8-16x) and BQ (1 bit/dim, 32x) chart nest's roadmap to far smaller files;
             both fit nest's mandatory-exact-rerank model because nest already always reranks
             ANN/hybrid candidates against true cosine, which is exactly the rescoring step these
             codecs require for accuracy.
perf:        RQ rotation ~7us for 1536-dim (O(D log D) FWHT). Quantized int8 SIMD distance ~2.3x faster than
             f32; end-to-end 15-50% faster without rescore, 5-42% faster with rescore in
             high-recall ranges. 1M x 1536 OpenAI embeddings: 1465 MB (RQ) vs 5859 MB (f32) = 4x.
             BQ ~97% memory reduction (32x), PQ ~85% (split-segment codebook), SQ ~75%. All
             quantizers store uncompressed vectors alongside for rescoring (a tradeoff nest avoids
             since it keeps f32/canonical separately and reranks from them).
license:     BSD-3-Clause (weaviate/recipes repo); underlying Weaviate engine is BSD-3-Clause. Techniques (FWHT
             rotation, PQ, BQ, SQ) are public algorithms, not patented by Weaviate; safe to
             reimplement.

[MEDIUM] deepset-ai/haystack
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python LLM/RAG orchestration framework whose pipelines are serializable component DAGs (now with
             cycles), with a pure-Python InMemoryDocumentStore that holds an
             incrementally-maintained BM25 inverted index (BM25Okapi/BM25L/BM25Plus) and does
             dense retrieval via a single numpy matmul, persisting everything to a single JSON
             file.
innovation:  The incrementally-maintained, in-memory BM25 inverted index (mutable corpus stats updated per
             write/delete) is the transferable idea: it turns nest's frozen bm25 build into
             something that supports live edits.
techniques:
  - Incremental BM25 statistics: per-doc Counter(token)->freq + doc_len stored alongside doc;
    global _freq_vocab_for_idf (df map) and running _avg_doc_len updated on every write/delete
    instead of full reindex
  - Three BM25 scoring variants behind one interface: BM25Okapi vs BM25L vs BM25Plus, where L/Plus
    add a delta term (+delta in numerator/idf) to stop long-document over-penalization; tunable
    k1/b/epsilon
  - Configurable tokenizer as a single regex (r'(?u)\b\w\w+\b') captured in config, making
    tokenization a serialized, reproducible parameter
  - Dense retrieval as one BLAS call: stack all doc embeddings into a 2D array, scores =
    np.dot(query, embeddings.T) then top-k - identical math to nest's SIMD dot-product fan-out
  - to_dict/from_dict component contract: every component (incl. document store, retriever)
    serializes to a plain dict of construction params (e.g. store IP+db name, not the live
    client), enabling deterministic rebuild
  - Single-file persistence: save_to_disk/load_from_disk dump the whole store (config + documents
    + stats) to one JSON file
  - Pipeline-as-data: the entire orchestration graph (components + typed input/output connections)
    serializes to YAML and round-trips, with deserialize callbacks for component rewriting
nest steals: Steal the incremental BM25 maintenance model for nest-runtime crates/nest-runtime/src/bm25.rs:
             keep the on-disk bm25_index(0x08) as the frozen cold start, but add an in-memory
             delta layer holding per-chunk term-frequency maps, a global df map, and a running
             avg_doc_len so live edits (insert/delete chunk) update scores without a full Python
             rebuild - this directly unlocks the north-star "real-time edit + fast search" without
             breaking the frozen container (the persisted 0x08 stays additive/deterministic,
             alphabetical term order preserved). Secondly, adapt the BM25L/BM25Plus
             delta-smoothing formula into the existing Bm25Index scoring path as a config flag in
             search_contract (nest-format) so recall on long/heterogeneous chunks improves; keep
             alphabetical term order for determinism. Thirdly, mirror the to_dict/from_dict
             contract as nest's reproducibility surface: the search_contract section already
             fingerprints config, and Haystack confirms that capturing tokenizer-regex + bm25
             params + similarity-fn as serialized scalars is exactly what makes builds replayable
             - validates nest's content_hash design. Low compression relevance: this is
             index/orchestration plumbing, not an entropy coder; nest already beats Haystack on
             storage (Haystack stores raw JSON + full-precision f32 embeddings with zero
             quantization, whereas nest has zstd/f16/int8/hnsw).
compression: Low/indirect. Haystack does no compression or quantization at all (save_to_disk is plain JSON,
             embeddings kept as full-precision f32 in a numpy array), so it offers nothing for the
             'compress more than everyone' goal directly - nest is already far ahead here. Its
             relevance is to the adjacent north-star pillars (real-time edit, fast BM25/hybrid
             search), not to the entropy/quantization frontier.
perf:        Dense retrieval is a single numpy.dot (BLAS) over the full stacked embedding matrix then top-k -
             O(N*d) brute force, no ANN in the in-memory store (ANN is delegated to external
             vector DBs); fine at small N, does not scale, and is exactly the brute-force path
             nest already replaces with SIMD + HNSW. BM25 scoring is pure-Python over Counters
             (slow vs a compiled Rust scorer). No SIMD, no mmap, no quantization.
             Whole-store-in-RAM model; persistence is a full JSON rewrite, not append/mmap, so it
             has no zero-copy or partial-load story.
license:     Apache-2.0

[MEDIUM] langgenius/dify
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        Dify is a Python LLM-app orchestration platform whose RAG subsystem (api/core/rag) is a modular
             multi-strategy retrieval pipeline: pluggable vector stores, an "economical"
             jieba+TF-IDF inverted keyword index, and a hybrid retriever that fuses semantic and
             keyword results via either a neural reranker or a transparent weighted-score formula.
innovation:  The "weight_rerank" fusion: instead of always needing a neural cross-encoder, Dify computes final
             = w_vec*cosine(query_emb, chunk_emb) + w_kw*tfidf_cosine(query_tokens, chunk_tokens),
             where the keyword side is a self-contained TF-IDF-cosine (IDF = log((1+N)/(1+df)) +
             1, TF*IDF vectors, cosine between query and chunk term vectors) and the vector side
             is raw cosine. It is a deterministic, dependency-free, two-knob alternative to a
             black-box reranker that still yields one comparable score per chunk.
techniques:
  - weighted-score hybrid fusion: final = w_vec*cosine + w_kw*tfidf_cosine, two tunable weights
  - TF-IDF-cosine keyword reranker: smoothed IDF log((1+N)/(1+df))+1, TF*IDF vectors, query/chunk
    cosine, fully deterministic and neural-free
  - economical inverted keyword index: keyword->set(chunk_ids) table built by jieba TF-IDF top-k
    extraction + subtoken expansion + stopword filtering
  - two-tier quality modes: 'economical' (inverted keyword index, no embeddings) vs 'high-quality'
    (embeddings+ANN), a build-time size/cost switch
  - RerankRunner factory: RERANKING_MODEL vs WEIGHTED_SCORE behind one interface; community RRF
    1/(k+rank) as a scale-free rank fusion
  - pluggable vector-store abstraction: uniform retrieval interface across 16+ backends decoupling
    index format from query path
  - TopK dynamically bounded by the consumer context budget instead of a fixed constant
  - jieba (CJK-aware) tokenization for the keyword side
nest steals: Adapt weighted-score fusion into nest-runtime's search path as a third hybrid mode beside RRF.
             nest today fuses bm25::Bm25Index and ann::HnswIndex via reciprocal-rank fusion then
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank; add a `weighted` fusion in
             crates/nest-runtime/src/search computing final = w_vec*exact_cosine +
             w_kw*normalized_bm25 with two deterministic config weights, giving an interpretable
             blended score. The exact-cosine rerank invariant still holds because cosine is one
             term, and it stays offline/deterministic (no neural reranker, no socket). Separately,
             mirror Dify's economical/high-quality split as a nest-cli + nest-format preset: an
             "economical" build shipping ONLY chunks + bm25_index(0x08) (no embeddings, no HNSW),
             zstd on non-embedding sections, for keyword-amenable corpora. The keyword->chunk_ids
             inverted-table layout is also a reference for making bm25_index(0x08)
             self-describing. All additive: existing section ids, frozen v1 layout untouched.
compression: Indirect but real. Biggest lever is the "economical" build: an embeddings-free keyword-only .nest
             (chunks + bm25_index(0x08), zstd) would beat the tiny preset's 0.283 ratio for
             keyword-amenable corpora because int8 embeddings dominate file size, while the
             TF-IDF/BM25 inverted table is tiny and zstd-friendly. The weighted-score fusion adds
             no bytes but keeps that lighter index recall-competitive. RRF (1/(k+rank)) is also a
             config-only, storage-free fusion improvement.
perf:        Fusion is a handful of float ops per candidate, run after ANN/BM25 gather, so negligible added
             latency on nest's hot path; the vector term IS exact cosine, so it reuses nest's SIMD
             dot-product kernels (AVX2/NEON/scalar). Dify's jieba keyword tokenizer is CJK-aware,
             directly relevant to nest's known gap (word-segmented BM25 tokenizer degrades on
             CJK/Thai); nest could adopt a jieba-style or unicode-segmentation tokenizer, but must
             pick an offline, small-footprint, deterministic segmenter to preserve reproducibility
             (jieba's dictionary is large).
license:     Modified Apache License 2.0 (Dify Open Source License: Apache 2.0 plus conditions on
             multi-tenant/SaaS commercial use and logo/branding). The math (TF-IDF cosine,
             weighted fusion, RRF) is not protectable, so a clean-room Rust reimplementation is
             fine.

[MEDIUM] langroid/langroid
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python multi-agent LLM framework whose DocChatAgent implements a RAG retrieval pipeline that
             fuses semantic + BM25 + fuzzy rankings via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (c=60), then
             applies deterministic post-rank reordering (diversity, lost-in-middle periphery) and
             optional cross-encoder/LLM relevance extraction.
innovation:  Rank-only Reciprocal Rank Fusion as the hybrid-search combiner: scores from heterogeneous
             retrievers are discarded and replaced by 1/(rank_i + 60) summed across methods.
             Because it consumes only ordinal rank, it fuses score spaces that are not comparable
             (cosine vs BM25 vs edit-distance) with zero normalization, zero tuning, and is fully
             deterministic given deterministic input rankings -- exactly what nest already
             guarantees for its hnsw and bm25 indices.
techniques:
  - Reciprocal Rank Fusion: combined = sum_methods 1/(rank + c), c=60.0, sort desc; rank-only so
    no score normalization needed
  - Three-way fusion of semantic(vector) + BM25Okapi + fuzzy(thefuzz partial_ratio) rankings
    rather than the usual two-way
  - rank_to_periphery: deterministic anti-'lost-in-middle' reorder splitting by index into odds +
    reversed(evens) so top hits land at both list ends (1..9 => 1 3 5 7 9 8 6 4 2)
  - rank_with_diversity: greedy MMR-style selection picking the passage with min mean cosine
    similarity to already-selected set
  - n_neighbor_chunks context-window expansion via stored window_ids metadata: re-stitch adjacent
    original chunks around a hit
  - cross-encoder rerank with logistic squashing 1/(1+exp(-score)) using ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2
    (mutually exclusive with RRF)
  - RelevanceExtractorAgent: LLM extracts verbatim relevant sentences by numbering, returns
    NO_ANSWER when nothing matches (post-retrieval filter)
nest steals: nest-runtime/search: nest already runs hnsw + bm25 and fuses bm25 via reciprocal-rank fusion
             before the mandatory exact-cosine rerank. Steal two concrete upgrades. (1) Generalize
             the RRF fuser in the hybrid path to a rank-only sum_methods 1/(rank+60) over N>=2
             ordered lists with c exposed in the search_contract section of nest-format (additive,
             no version bump), so a future third signal (fuzzy/edit-distance term match, helping
             the known CJK/thai bm25 gap) drops in without touching the codec; keep determinism
             via the existing alphabetical/seeded ordering. (2) Add rank_to_periphery as a
             pure-integer, deterministic final reorder in nest-runtime::search::materialize (odds
             + reversed(evens)) -- ~15 lines, no ML, no float, panic-free, well under the 300-line
             file cap -- improving downstream LLM answer quality on the returned chunk list at
             zero index/storage cost. The n_neighbor_chunks idea maps directly onto nest's
             chunks_original_spans: store left/right neighbor chunk_ids so a hit can
             re-materialize its original window from the mmap.
compression: Low-to-none for raw byte compression: langroid has no on-disk format, entropy coder, or
             quantization -- it is an orchestration layer that outsources storage to
             Qdrant/LanceDB/Chroma. Indirect relevance: the n_neighbor_chunks/window_ids pattern
             argues for storing only neighbor chunk_id pointers (a few varints) instead of
             duplicating overlapping context text, which trims redundancy in chunks_canonical and
             supports nest's 'compress more than everyone' goal by deduplicating chunk overlap.
perf:        All steal-worthy pieces are cheap: RRF is O(total_hits) integer/float adds; rank_to_periphery is
             an O(k) index permutation with no allocation beyond the output vec; both run after
             the hot-path exact-cosine rerank, so they add negligible latency and are
             SIMD-irrelevant. langroid itself is pure Python with per-method latency dominated by
             network calls to external vector DBs and LLM relevance extraction (the
             relevance_extractor adds an LLM round-trip per passage and is the main latency sink,
             defaultable to None) -- nest gains the math, not the runtime cost.
license:     MIT

[MEDIUM] QuivrHQ/quivr (+ companion QuivrHQ/MegaParse)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        An opinionated, YAML-configured LangGraph RAG engine (quivr-core) paired with MegaParse, a
             "lossless" document-to-markdown ingestion tool that combines layout models with
             vision-LLM table reconstruction, plus a deterministic iterative token-budget
             context-reduction algorithm.
innovation:  The MegaParse ingestion contract: normalize any source document (PDF/DOCX/PPTX/XLSX) into a single
             canonical, hierarchy-preserving markdown representation BEFORE chunking/embedding, by
             routing each region through a layout model (~90% table segmentation) and falling back
             to a vision LLM that crops hard tables and reconstructs them as markdown. The
             canonical text is the thing you chunk, hash, and serve -- decoupling messy source
             bytes from a stable normalized representation.
techniques:
  - MegaParse two-tier table extraction: layout-model segmentation (unstructured/doctr) for ~90%
    of tables, vision-LLM (gpt-4o/claude-3.5) crop-and-reconstruct fallback for complex tables ->
    markdown
  - Hierarchy-aware partition->chunk: split on document-format semantic units (headers, TOC,
    sections) first, only fall back to text-splitting (\n\n) when an element exceeds max chunk
    size (the 'unstructured' chunking model)
  - Deterministic combine_documents: assign sequential metadata['index']=0,1,2... to chunks so
    retrieved fragments can be re-ordered and mapped back to source position
  - retrieve_full_documents_context: rank by similarity score + chunk frequency, then reconstruct
    full-document context up to the highest chunk index per knowledge item (chunk->full-doc
    expansion)
  - reduce_rag_context: iterative arithmetic context fitting with an 85% security factor -- drop
    oldest chat-history pairs first, then drop chunks from the longest task, recomputing token
    count until it fits max_context_tokens
  - dynamic_retrieve: saturation-driven retrieval that multiplies k/top_n per iteration (max 3)
    until enough relevance-thresholded chunks are found
  - ContextualCompressionRetriever pattern: base vector retrieval ALWAYS followed by a pluggable
    reranker (Cohere/Jina) with IdempotentCompressor no-op fallback
  - YAML-declared RetrievalConfig/WorkflowConfig/NodeConfig as a portable, declarative description
    of the whole search pipeline
nest steals: nest-python ingestion + nest-format: adopt a MegaParse-style 'canonicalization-before-chunk' stage
             so PDF/DOCX/PPTX are first normalized to deterministic markdown, then that normalized
             text becomes chunks_canonical(text) while raw source byte spans feed
             chunks_original_spans -- this directly strengthens nest's content_hash STABILITY
             guarantee (hash the normalized canonical form, not the encoding-dependent source) and
             makes the 'compress and serve PDF/parquet/datasets' north-star tractable. The
             chunk->full-doc index reconstruction
             (combine_documents/retrieve_full_documents_context) maps cleanly onto nest's
             chunks_original_spans + nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation reopen: store the
             sequential chunk index and parent-doc id so a hit can rehydrate the surrounding
             original span. The reduce_rag_context 85%-factor iterative trimming is a tiny
             deterministic, panic-free pure-integer algorithm that belongs in nest-runtime
             materialize as a 'fit retrieved chunks into a token/byte budget' helper
             (drop-lowest-score-first, recompute, stop when under budget) -- offline, no LLM call,
             fully reproducible. The ContextualCompressionRetriever 'always rerank' pattern
             validates nest's existing mandatory exact-cosine rerank as the correct architecture.
compression: Indirect but real: MegaParse's normalize-to-canonical-markdown step is a precondition for extreme
             compression of heterogeneous formats -- you cannot deduplicate/zstd/entropy-code PDF
             or parquet effectively until you have lifted them into a single normalized
             text/structure representation. Canonicalizing before hashing also makes content_hash
             stable across source encodings (already a nest goal) and exposes far more
             cross-document redundancy for zstd on non-embedding sections. The chunk->full-doc
             index lets nest store one canonical body once and reference spans rather than
             duplicating overlapping chunk text. No new entropy coder or quantization scheme is
             offered here -- this repo is pure Python RAG glue, not a codec.
perf:        Pure-Python, langgraph/asyncio orchestration; no SIMD, no custom on-disk format, vectorstores are
             delegated to PGVector or Faiss. Heavy paths (vision-LLM table reconstruction,
             Cohere/Jina rerank) are network/GPU-bound external API calls -- antithetical to
             nest's offline-by-construction constraint, so only the ALGORITHMS (budget trimming,
             chunk indexing, hierarchy chunking) are stealable, not the runtime. dynamic_retrieve
             caps at 3 iterations; reduce_rag_context is O(n) per trim pass over chunks. No
             memory-mapping, no zero-copy; everything is in-RAM Python objects.
license:     Quivr: Apache-2.0. MegaParse: Apache-2.0 (with the caveat that MegaParse Vision and the
             unstructured/llama-parse backends pull in their own external model/API dependencies).

[MEDIUM] Mintplex-Labs/anything-llm
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Node.js/Express provider-agnostic RAG + agent platform that ingests documents, chunks them with
             an embedder-aware TextSplitter, caches embeddings deterministically by UUID v5
             content hash, and queries one of ~10 pluggable vector DBs (default LanceDB/Arrow)
             with cosine distance normalized to a 0-1 similarity and optional native reranking.
innovation:  A deterministic content-addressed embedding cache keyed by chunk content + embedder identity (UUID
             v5), which makes re-ingestion skip already-computed vectors.
techniques:
  - UUID v5 namespaced-hash cache keys over chunk text + embedder identity, stored as per-file
    JSON vectors
  - Embedder-aware TextSplitter sizing chunks to min(user setting, model max token length) with
    char overlap
  - Self-describing chunks via <document_metadata> headers (title/published/source) prepended
    before embedding
  - distanceToSimilarity: normalize cosine/L2 distances to a uniform 0-1 similarity
  - Retrieve-then-rerank with NativeEmbeddingReranker for precision over raw ANN recall
  - Single storage directory that fully reconstructs all state (sqlite + lancedb + vector-cache +
    documents JSON)
  - Common VectorDatabase interface abstracting ~10 backends
  - Agent Intelligent Skill Selection to prune tools and cut token usage up to 80%
nest steals: Build a deterministic embedding cache in nest-python's builder backed by nest-format hashing: key
             = SHA-256(canonical_chunk_text || model_fingerprint), value = stored f32 embedding.
             On rebuild the builder reuses cached vectors instead of re-running the model, giving
             fast incremental rebuilds while keeping byte-identical output (preserves the
             reproducible-build contract). Also adopt distanceToSimilarity-style explicit 0-1
             normalization of the exact-cosine rerank score in nest-runtime/search.rs so returned
             scores are uniform across raw/f16/int8 encodings, and formalize per-chunk provenance
             headers tying chunks_original_spans + provenance into nest://content_hash/chunk_id
             citations in nest-format/sections.
compression: Low-to-indirect. anything-llm does no compression or quantization (it stores raw f32 vectors via
             LanceDB/Arrow and delegates everything to external DBs), so it offers nothing for
             nest's extreme-compression north star directly. Its value is build-time/operational:
             the deterministic embedding cache removes recompute cost during the encode/quantize
             loop, which is what makes aggressive re-encoding experiments (int8, f16, future
             codecs) cheap to iterate. It validates nest's content-hash determinism as a cache
             primitive, not as a size reducer.
perf:        All-JS/Node runtime; no SIMD, no quantization, no on-disk compression of its own. LanceDB
             (Rust/Arrow columnar, mmap-friendly) provides the fast local path and is reported
             "blazing fast on low-end hardware" with ~100% of users on it. Embedding cache is the
             main perf lever -- it eliminates redundant embedding API/model calls. Vectors stored
             as raw f32, so memory/storage scales linearly with corpus and dimension (no
             float16/int8 like nest).
license:     MIT
