[MEDIUM] KaiDMML/FakeNewsNet
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A fake-news research data repository that ships only minimal CSV manifests (id, news_url, title,
             tab-separated tweet_ids) plus per-object JSON files, and reconstructs the full
             multi-GB corpus (news content, tweets, retweets, user profiles, social graph) on
             demand via a rate-limit-coordinated parallel download harness.
innovation:  Dehydrated-dataset / manifest-plus-pointers pattern with dedup-by-reference: ship a tiny canonical
             ID+URL manifest and store every heavy object once in a shared, content-addressed pool
             that records reference by natural key.
techniques:
  - Manifest CSV with stable string IDs decoupling identity from payload
  - Tab-separated ID reference lists as foreign keys into a shared object pool
  - Content-addressed-by-natural-key per-object JSON files (<tweet_id>.json, <user_id>.json)
    stored once, referenced many times
  - Two-layer split: canonical shareable manifest vs reconstructable hydrated payload
  - Flask key-broker coordinating rate-limited parallel reconstruction across workers
  - Factory-dispatched resumable partial collection driven by config.json
  - newspaper3k HTML-to-canonical-JSON article normalization (text/images/publish date)
nest steals: nest-format gets an additive RESERVED section (0x09+) for external source refs (stable id + url)
             so a .nest can be a dehydrated manifest that deterministically re-hydrates chunks;
             nest-python builder gets a pre-zstd chunk-dedup pass that stores duplicate canonical
             spans once and emits pointer indices in chunk_ids; the tweet_ids->object-pool model
             becomes the data layout for a future deterministic graph_edges section in
             nest-runtime for offline graph search.
compression: High conceptually, indirect in practice. FakeNewsNet itself uses no entropy coder or quantization
             - its "compression" is structural: dedup-by-reference (each user/tweet stored once,
             referenced by ID) and dehydration (ship the recipe, not the corpus). This is exactly
             the lever nest needs for the 'compress whole datasets/repos more than everyone' goal:
             deduplicate identical chunks before zstd, and offer a manifest mode where a .nest
             references external sources instead of inlining them. Both shrink the container
             without touching the embedding hot path.
perf:        No SIMD, no entropy coding, no on-disk binary format - pure filesystem-of-JSON plus CSV.
             Performance characteristic worth noting is the parallel-reconstruction harness: a
             Flask key-broker fronts num_process workers to amortize external API rate limits, a
             useful pattern for nest-cli batch-build throughput but irrelevant to the mmap/SIMD
             serving path. Dedup magnitude depends on object sharing (users/tweets recur heavily
             across news items), so structural savings are corpus-dependent, not guaranteed.
license:     Repository is research/academic; code under an open-source license (no SPDX header prominently
             shown), but the underlying tweet/news payloads are NOT redistributable (Twitter ToS +
             publisher copyright) - which is precisely why the dehydration pattern exists. Treat
             as reference-only for ideas, not vendored code.

[MEDIUM] jghm-f/FACTCK.BR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A 1,309-row UTF-8 TSV corpus of Brazilian Portuguese fact-checks (9 columns) harvested from agency
             RSS feeds by parsing schema.org ClaimReview JSON-LD, where each claim carries a
             normalizable truthfulness rating (ratingValue / bestRating + human-readable
             alternativeName label).</one_liner>
             <parameter name="core_innovation">The schema.org ClaimReview/reviewRating data model as a
             portable, normalized "truth verdict": a claim is represented by claimReviewed (text),
             a numeric ratingValue on a per-agency scale with bestRating as the scale maximum, and
             an alternativeName string label (e.g. "falso", "verdadeiro"). Dividing
             ratingValue/bestRating yields a source-agnostic truthfulness score in [0,1] that lets
             you fuse incompatible agency label vocabularies into one comparable axis. The
             harvesting is itself a clean ETL: pull JSON-LD (application/ld+json) out of
             fact-check pages, map reviewRating fields directly to columns, drop exact-row
             duplicates with URL as implicit primary key.
innovation:  The schema.org ClaimReview/reviewRating data model as a portable normalized truth verdict,
             harvested from JSON-LD, where ratingValue/bestRating yields a source-agnostic
             truthfulness score in [0,1] that unifies incompatible per-agency label vocabularies.
techniques:
  - schema.org ClaimReview JSON-LD extraction from <script type=application/ld+json> tags via
    feedparser + BeautifulSoup findAll
  - reviewRating triple: ratingValue (numeric), bestRating (scale max), alternateName (text label)
    -> normalized truth score = ratingValue/bestRating in [0,1]
  - field-to-column direct mapping (url, author.url, datePublished, claimReviewed,
    reviewBody/description fallback, ratingValue, bestRating, alternateName, itemReviewed.@type)
  - text normalization: re_char() strips non-ASCII except PT accented chars, saxutils.unescape()
    for HTML entities
  - exact-row drop_duplicates() dedup with URL as primary key
  - UTF-8 TSV (tab-separated) flat-table on-disk format, 9 columns x 1309 rows
nest steals: This is a truth-feature / provenance pattern, not a compression codec, so it lands in nest-format
             provenance + the demo corpus pipeline rather than the codec layer. Two concrete
             adaptations: (1) Extend the provenance section schema in nest-format
             (crates/nest-format/src/sections, additive within v1 since provenance is already
             required) to carry an optional per-chunk verdict record {rating_value:u16 LE,
             best_rating:u16 LE, label_id:u8} mirroring reviewRating, with label strings interned
             in a small dictionary, so a chunk that is a fact-checked claim ships its normalized
             truthfulness alongside its content_hash citation. (2) In the python builder
             (nest-python / build pipeline that ships the PT-BR fake-news demo corpus), add a
             ClaimReview JSON-LD ingester that maps reviewRating -> the new provenance fields and
             computes ratingValue/bestRating as a deterministic normalized score. This directly
             enriches nest's existing demo domain (it already ships PT-BR fake-news datasets) and
             gives nest a real "truth feature": nest://content_hash/chunk_id citations can then
             return both the exact byte span AND its agency verdict, deterministically. Because
             labels are dictionary-interned u8 ids and the verdict struct is tiny repr(C) LE
             integers, it also compresses well under the existing zstd path on non-embedding
             sections.
compression: Low for raw byte-compression: the dataset is a 1,309-row TSV with no entropy coder, quantization,
             or codec to steal. Its compression-adjacent value is schema compactness: representing
             a verdict as ratingValue/bestRating/label_id (a few LE integers + a dictionary id)
             instead of free-text labels is a small domain-specific dictionary-coding win, and
             storing labels as interned u8 ids rather than repeated UTF-8 strings reduces
             provenance-section size before zstd. So it is relevant only as a tiny
             structured-field encoding pattern for the provenance section, not as a general
             compression technique.
perf:        No performance characteristics of interest: pure-Python offline ETL (feedparser + BeautifulSoup +
             requests), no SIMD, no on-disk index, no large-scale data. The TSV is tiny (~1,309
             rows). Only the score-normalization formula and the verdict-field layout transfer;
             nothing about throughput or memory is relevant to nest's hot path (and nothing here
             touches embeddings, which nest never compresses on the scoring path).
license:     MIT (dataset code); paper publication rights licensed to ACM (DOI 10.1145/3323503.3361698)

[MEDIUM] roneysco/Fake.br-Corpus
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A labeled fact-checking dataset (not software): 7,200 length-aligned Brazilian-Portuguese news
             articles (3,600 fake / 3,600 true) shipped as numbered plain-text files plus a fixed
             25-field line-by-line linguistic-metadata schema per article and a
             stopword/diacritic-normalized preprocessed CSV variant.
innovation:  The pairwise size-normalization protocol: every fake article is matched to a true counterpart on
             the same topic, and the longer text in each pair is truncated (by word count) to the
             shorter one's length, producing a corpus where length cannot leak the label. This
             controlled-pair construction is the transferable methodological idea, not any
             compression or indexing technique.
techniques:
  - Length-aligned fake/true pairing: longer member of each pair truncated by word count to remove
    length-as-label leakage (size_normalized_texts/)
  - Fixed 25-field line-by-line metadata schema per doc: author, link, category, date, then 21
    numeric linguistic features
  - Hand-crafted truth features: pausality (punctuation count / sentence count), emotiveness
    (affective-term ratio), type-token diversity, spelling-error percentage, avg sentence/word
    length, POS counts (verbs, subjunctive/imperative, modal, pronouns)
  - Three parallel corpus variants from one source: full_texts (raw), size_normalized_texts
    (truncated), preprocessed (stopword-removed + accent-folded CSV)
  - Numbered filename convention (NNNN.txt / NNNN-meta.txt) as the join key aligning a doc, its
    label folder, and its metadata
  - Empirical baseline: SVM + bag-of-words reaches ~89% F-measure; the 21 hand-crafted features
    add almost nothing over BOW
nest steals: Two concrete uses. (1) Demo corpus: nest already ships PT-BR fake-news data; this is the canonical
             aligned source. Build a tiny-preset .nest (zstd/int8/hnsw) over the 7,200 docs so
             chunks_canonical holds raw text, embeddings hold sentence-transformer vectors, and
             provenance records label+category+source-url per chunk -- a flagship offline
             single-file fact-check demo at ~0.283 size ratio. (2) Truth features as provenance:
             the 25-field metadata maps into nest-format's provenance section (additive, no format
             bump -- provenance is an existing v1 section whose meaning is unchanged). Store
             pausality/emotiveness/spelling-error-rate as per-chunk numeric provenance fields,
             surface them via nest-cli inspect/stats so a nest://content_hash/chunk_id citation
             shows credibility metadata next to the text span. Features are cheap deterministic
             single-pass counts (regex/POS over canonical text), computable at build time in
             nest-python with no model, fitting offline/deterministic constraints.
compression: Low to none. This repo contributes zero compression machinery -- no entropy coder, quantization,
             or on-disk format; data is uncompressed numbered .txt plus CSV. Indirect relevance:
             the size-normalization (truncate-to-shorter-pair) is a domain-specific length-cap
             heuristic, and the corpus is a realistic payload to benchmark nest's tiny preset
             (int8 + zstd-on-text + hnsw) and content_hash stability. The truth features are
             metadata to carry, not bytes to shrink.
perf:        Static dataset, no runtime. Small: 7,200 short-to-medium PT-BR articles (fake avg ~216 tokens,
             true avg ~1,268 tokens), on the order of a few MB of text, trivially mmappable and
             fully resident; ideal small fixture for deterministic-build and recall@10 regression
             tests. The 21 linguistic features are O(n) single-pass token/punctuation counters, no
             SIMD or heavy compute needed.
license:     Academic/research use; corpus released with PROPOR 2018 (Monteiro et al.) and Expert Systems with
             Applications 2020 (Silva et al.). No explicit OSS license file in repo -- treat as
             research-use, cite the papers; verify before redistributing inside a shipped .nest
             demo.

[MEDIUM] sfu-discourse-lab/MisInfoText
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A curated corpus-of-corpora that scrapes fact-checking sites (Snopes, BuzzFeed, PolitiFact,
             Emergent) and stores each article as a row carrying the original news text, the
             checked claim, the source URL, the fact-checker's native veracity rating, and a
             hand-normalized truth label projected onto a common ordinal scale.
innovation:  A dual-label veracity normalization schema preserving both original and normalized truth labels
             per record.
techniques:
  - Per-record dual labels: original_label (verbatim fact-checker string) + normalized_label
    (mapped ordinal), so the projection is lossless w.r.t. provenance
  - Heterogeneous-scale unification: collapse 2/4/5/6-way fact-checker taxonomies onto one
    canonical ordinal {false, mostly-false, mixture, mostly-true, true} with optional binary
    {fake, real} reduction
  - Provenance triple per row: source fact-checker id + original article URL + checked claim
    string, enabling reopen/re-verification
  - Out-of-scale rating handling: non-veracity Snopes ratings (Outdated, Miscaptioned, Satire)
    routed to explicit side categories rather than forced onto the truth axis
  - Two-stage curation: automatic scraper crawl then manual annotator alignment verification of
    claim<->article<->label (Figure Eight crowd platform for larger sets)
  - CSV-as-interchange: flat columns (claim, text, url, original_label, normalized_label) zipped
    per collection
nest steals: nest-format: add an OPTIONAL truth/veracity metadata section (reserve a v1-additive section id in
             the 0x09+ space) that attaches, per chunk_id, a small fixed record {source_id:u16 LE,
             original_label_idx:u8, normalized_ordinal:u8 (0..=4 false..true), claim_chunk_ref,
             source_url provenance ref}. All integers little-endian unsigned, repr(C),
             deterministic. Store the source-label <-> ordinal mapping table once in the
             provenance/manifest section so normalization is reproducible and byte-stable (same
             mapping => same bytes). This pairs cleanly with the existing
             chunks_canonical/chunks_original_spans split: original_label is the "original span,"
             normalized_ordinal is the "canonical" derived value. nest-runtime: expose a label
             filter/facet on the search path (post-rerank predicate on normalized_ordinal, e.g.
             return only false<=1 claims) and let cite emit nest://content_hash/chunk_id pointing
             at the exact checked claim. nest-cli: extend stats to histogram the ordinal
             distribution. Compression relevance: the ordinal is a u8 (or 3 bits) and source_id is
             small-cardinality, so the truth column is a textbook target for the int8/zstd path
             already in nest -- dictionary-encode source labels and bit-pack the 5-level ordinal,
             turning verbose fact-checker strings ("Pants on Fire!") into ~3 bits while keeping
             the verbatim string recoverable via the mapping table; this is real per-section
             compression on the PT-BR fake-news demo corpus nest already ships.
compression: Direct: veracity labels are low-cardinality categoricals. The dual-label idea lets nest store a
             verbose source string once in a manifest mapping table, then represent each row's
             truth value as a 3-bit ordinal + small source_id, bit-packed and zstd'd on the
             non-embedding path. Verbose label text collapses to a few bits per chunk while
             staying losslessly recoverable, a clean win for the demo corpus and for the broader
             "compress datasets" north star.
perf:        No runtime/codec engineering here -- this is a data resource, not a system. Volumes are tiny by
             nest standards (largest published collections ~1.3k-12.8k rows; CSV-zipped). No SIMD,
             no on-disk binary format, no index. The only perf-relevant fact is that the truth
             column is extremely low entropy (<=6 distinct values), so it costs near-zero space
             under nest's existing int8/zstd encodings and adds negligible scoring cost as a
             post-rerank facet predicate.
license:     Not clearly stated in README; datasets aggregate third-party fact-checker content
             (Snopes/PolitiFact/BuzzFeed/Emergent) under their respective terms plus
             academic-citation requests (Asr & Taboada 2019, Big Data & Society). Treat as
             reference/citation-ware, not a vendorable code license -- do not bundle the data,
             only adopt the schema idea.

[LOW] yuxiaw/OpenFactCheck
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Python fact-checking/factuality-evaluation framework that consolidates RARR, FacTool, and
             FactCheckGPT into a YAML-configured solver DAG (claim_processor -> retriever ->
             verifier) passing a single FactCheckerState dict, plus four human-annotated benchmark
             datasets (FactQA 6,480 examples, FactBench claims with True/False/Unknown labels).
innovation:  Declarative pipeline-by-name-matching: solvers register via @Solver.register(name, input_name,
             output_name) and are chained purely by string-matching one stage's output_name to the
             next stage's input_name in a YAML config, carrying all state in one mutable
             FactCheckerState bag with a success_flag. The pipeline topology is data, not code, so
             heterogeneous stages from different systems compose without recompilation - this is
             the Fairseq plugin pattern applied to a verification DAG.
techniques:
  - @Solver.register(name, input_name, output_name) decorator into a SOLVER_REGISTRY plus
    YAML-declared stage order; chaining is validated by string-matching output_name->input_name
  - Single FactCheckerState dict threaded through all stages with a boolean success_flag gating
    progression (message-passing contract)
  - BM25 over an offline Wikipedia dump for evidence retrieval (vs paid Serper/SerpAPI web search)
    - explicit offline tier
  - Atomic-claim decomposition: NLTK sentence split then LLM atomic-claim extraction +
    decontextualization
  - True/False/Unknown ternary verdict taxonomy with per-claim labels aggregated to a
    response-level 'percentage true claims' (FactScore-style) score
  - FactBench claim schema: question, domain (482), topic (4740), atomic_claims[], per-claim
    factual_labels[], response_level_label
  - Checker evaluation by precision/recall/F1 per label plus latency-hours and itemized per-claim
    cost ($/claim) leaderboard
nest steals: nest-runtime search_contract section + nest-cli: adopt the register-by-name output/input
             string-matching as a data-encoded retrieval-stage graph in the already-required
             search_contract section (no format bump, additive, deterministic, offline);
             separately, nest-python demo corpus + provenance/citation: adopt the FactBench
             claim+evidence+True/False/Unknown schema and percentage-true aggregation, mapping
             evidence passages onto nest://content_hash/chunk_id citations.
compression: n/a - OpenFactCheck contributes nothing to extreme compression. It has no entropy coder, no
             quantization, no on-disk binary format, no SIMD; data lives in plain JSON/dict
             structures and HF datasets. Its BM25-over-Wikipedia offline tier merely validates
             nest's existing bm25_index(0x08) and offline-by-construction stance. Value to nest is
             the demo/truth-feature domain (fact-check datasets + ternary verdict +
             citation-as-evidence) and a declarative-pipeline engineering pattern, not byte
             reduction.
perf:        Pure Python, no SIMD or vectorization. Reported latencies are pipeline-level and dominated by LLM
             API calls (FacTool ~0.49h, FactCheckGPT ~7.67h over a benchmark run), not
             data-structure throughput. BM25 retrieval is the only local compute path. Cost is
             tracked per atomic claim (~$0.02/claim) - a useful precedent for nest emitting
             per-query cost/latency telemetry in benchmark/stats subcommands, but nothing
             performance-transferable to the mmap hot path.
license:     Apache-2.0 (per repo); permissive, compatible with vendoring schema/dataset ideas. Datasets
             aggregate third-party benchmarks (FacTool-QA, FELM-WK, Factcheck-Bench, HaluEval,
             FreshQA, SelfAware) each under their own upstream licenses - check before
             redistributing in a .nest demo corpus.

[LOW] Gabriel-Lino-Garcia/FakeRecogna
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A balanced PT-BR fake-news corpus of 11,902 labeled samples (5,951 fake / 5,951 real) shipped as a
             single XLSX with an 8-column metadata schema (Title, Sub-title, News, Category,
             Author, Date, URL, Class), crawled and aggregated from 6 fact-check agencies and 4
             reputable news portals.
innovation:  The transferable idea is a per-record structured-provenance schema for fact-checking data: every
             sample carries not just text + a binary truth label (Class 0/1) but the full origin
             chain (source agency/portal, original URL, publication Date, Category), with
             engineered class balance and source diversity to avoid the leakage bias where fake
             and real come from disjoint stylistic distributions. Fakes are aggregated across 6
             independent fact-checkers, making the corpus a multi-source consensus rather than a
             single-feed scrape.
techniques:
  - 8-column flat metadata schema per record (title, subtitle, body, category, author, date, url,
    class) in a single XLSX container
  - Binary truth label encoding Class in {0=fake, 1=real} as a first-class column
  - Multi-agency aggregation: 6 fact-check sources merged into one fake-news pool with source
    attribution preserved
  - Class balancing by downsampling 100k+ real samples to 5,951 to match the fake count
  - Fixed 6-category partition (Health, Politics, Entertainment, Brazil, Science, World) from
    journal sections
  - Source-decoupling anti-bias design: fake and real drawn from unrelated outlets so they are not
    topic-paired
  - Classical baseline: TF-IDF/bag-of-words features feeding Naive Bayes, SVM, Optimum-Path
    Forest, plus a CNN
nest steals: nest steals the data model, not an algorithm. (1) nest-python build path: ingest the XLSX demo
             corpus, mapping News body to chunks_canonical(text) and (Title, Category, Author,
             Date, URL, Class, source-agency) into the provenance section as per-chunk structured
             metadata, giving the existing PT-BR demo corpus a real truth-label field. (2)
             nest-format provenance: adopt the origin-chain shape (source + url + date + category
             + truth-class) as the canonical fact-check provenance record, kept additive on
             existing provenance bytes (reserve a new section id 0x09+ only if a typed truth field
             is wanted; never bump NEST_FORMAT_VERSION). The original URL maps onto nest's
             citation model so nest://content_hash/chunk_id resolves back to the upstream article.
             (3) nest-cli search-text: Category and Class become filterable demo facets; the
             low-cardinality enums compress to near-zero under existing zstd.
compression: Low/indirect. The repo ships no codec, quantizer, or index -- it is raw XLSX text. Relevance is
             twofold: (a) the low-cardinality enum columns (Class 2 values, Category 6 values, ~10
             source agencies) are highly compressible by nest's existing
             zstd-on-non-embedding-sections path and serve as a clean fixture to validate metadata
             compression; (b) the 11,902-sample PT-BR body text is a realistic real-world corpus
             to benchmark the tiny preset (zstd/int8/hnsw, 0.283 size ratio, recall@10 0.992) on
             the exact misinformation domain nest demos, rather than synthetic data. No new
             compression technique is transferable from here.
perf:        No runtime, SIMD, or throughput characteristics -- it is a static ~11.9k-row XLSX (single file, a
             few MB). The only perf-adjacent note is that its 8-column metadata is tiny and
             enum-heavy, so it adds negligible bytes to a .nest provenance section and negligible
             cost to mmap/scan.
license:     Not clearly stated in the README; the dataset accompanies an academic paper (Garcia, Afonso, Papa;
             BRACIS/PROPOR 2022, LNCS/LNAI 13208). Treat as research-use; verify upstream license
             before redistributing inside a .nest demo container.

[LOW] viniciuszani/portuguese-fake-news-classifiers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Poetry/Makefile-orchestrated PT-BR fake-news ML benchmark that converts the Fake.Br and
             FakeTrue.Br corpora into Parquet, then trains/tunes logreg+TF-IDF, MLP+TF-IDF,
             BiLSTM, and BERTimbau (Portuguese BERT) classifiers via Optuna with

innovation:  Deterministic, length-debiasing "size-normalized pair truncation": for each aligned true/fake news

             reproducible corpus transform that yields a fairer, smaller, paired dataset -
             applicable as a deterministic preprocessing/normalization step for nest's demo
             corpus.
techniques:
  - Size-normalized truncation: per true/fake pair, truncate longer text to shorter text's word
    count (deterministic length-debiasing)
  - 25-field per-article linguistic/stylometric metadata schema: POS counts (verbs, nouns,
    adjectives, modal verbs, pronoun persons), pausality, char/sentence/word averages,
    spelling-error %, emotiveness, diversity
  - Parquet conversion of raw CSV/txt corpora as a single columnar prep step (make data_prep)
  - TF-IDF n-gram vectorization with tunable ngram_upper/min_df/max_df sparse text features
  - BERTimbau (Portuguese BERT) contextual embeddings as strong baseline; HuggingFace artifact
    publishing for reproducible model identity
  - Optuna study-based hyperparameter search persisted to a SQL backend (replayable trials)
  - Aligned true/fake corpus design (Fake.Br: 2000+2000 paired articles) enabling paired
    evaluation
nest steals: Two concrete steals. (1) In nest-python's demo-corpus builder (the PT-BR fake-news dataset that
             ships with nest), add an optional deterministic "pair-normalize" preprocessing pass
             mirroring size_normalized_texts: word-count-truncate aligned pairs before chunking.
             nest builds are already deterministic (same chunks + model fingerprint =>
             byte-identical file), so this transform is reproducible and shrinks the corpus,
             improving the demo's compression ratio honestly without touching the FROZEN format.
             (2) The 25-field stylometric metadata schema is an ideal payload for a future
             "truth-features" sidecar: encode it as a fixed repr(C), little-endian, per-chunk
             struct array stored inside the existing provenance section (no new section id, stays
             additive within v1). These are cheap-to-compute, int/float-quantizable features
             (int8/float16 codecs already exist) giving nest a sovereign credibility signal
             co-located with each chunk and citable via nest://content_hash/chunk_id. Neither
             touches nest-runtime's hot path or the exact-cosine search contract.
compression: Indirect. Size-normalized truncation is a lossy-but-deterministic corpus transform that
             legitimately reduces demo-corpus bytes (and embedding count), improving nest's
             headline size ratio on its shipped PT-BR fake-news dataset. The stylometric features
             are quantization-friendly (int8/float16) but are additive metadata, not a general
             entropy coder, so they add bytes rather than remove them. No new codec, graph layout,
             or SIMD kernel here that advances the "compress more than everyone" north star.
perf:        Pure Python/sklearn/PyTorch training pipeline; no SIMD, no custom on-disk format, no compression
             codec. BERTimbau inference is the throughput bottleneck. Parquet (likely Snappy/zstd
             column compression) is the only storage-efficiency mechanism. Nothing
             performance-relevant transfers to nest's mmap+SIMD runtime; the value is entirely in
             the corpus-prep and metadata-schema layer.
license:     Repository license not confirmed from README; underlying Fake.Br corpus is research-released and
             FACTCK.BR is MIT. Treat the classifiers repo license as unknown/unverified before
             vendoring any code; the size-normalization and metadata schema are method ideas (not
             copyrightable) safe to reimplement.

[LOW] fabriziosalmi/UglyFeed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A pure-Python RSS pipeline that fetches feeds, deduplicates and clusters near-duplicate articles
             via TF-IDF/cosine + agglomerative clustering, LLM-rewrites each cluster, and scores
             output with a ~30-metric text-quality evaluation suite
             (BLEU/ROUGE-L/METEOR/CIDEr/NIST, edit/Levenshtein/Hamming, TF-IDF & BoW & LSA cosine,
             plus readability indices) merged into a single weighted Ugly Score.
innovation:  A deterministic, dependency-light recipe for collapsing a corpus of near-duplicate documents into
             representative clusters: build a sparse vectorizer (tfidf/count/hashing with
             configurable ngram_range, min_df/max_df), compute a dense cosine-similarity matrix,
             convert to a precomputed distance matrix (1 - sim, clamped to >=0), then run
             AgglomerativeClustering(metric=precomputed, linkage=average, distance_threshold=0.66,
             n_clusters=None). Cluster cohesion is reported as the mean pairwise intra-cluster
             similarity. This is a pre-storage dedup/segmentation primitive that directly reduces
             how many documents you must store at all.
techniques:
  - TF-IDF/Count/Hashing vectorizer with ngram_range, max_df=0.85, min_df=0.01 to bound vocabulary
  - Cosine-similarity matrix -> precomputed distance (1-sim clamped >=0) feeding
    AgglomerativeClustering(linkage=average, distance_threshold, n_clusters=None) for
    threshold-driven near-duplicate grouping
  - Alternative clustering backends behind one config key:
    DBSCAN(metric=precomputed,eps,min_samples) and KMeans
  - Mean intra-cluster pairwise similarity as a per-group cohesion/confidence score stored with
    the group
  - Exact-dedup pre-pass keyed on (content, link) tuple-set before expensive vectorization
  - Reference-free readability/lexical battery: Gunning-Fog, SMOG, Coleman-Liau, ARI, type-token
    ratio, lexical density, entropy
  - Reference-based similarity battery: BLEU-n via Counter n-gram intersection, ROUGE-L/LCS,
    METEOR, CIDEr, NIST, Jaccard/Dice/Overlap, WER
  - Normalize-then-weighted-sum aggregation of ~30 heterogeneous metrics into one scalar
    (per-metric max-value normalization to 0-100, fixed weight vector)
nest steals: Two targeted lifts. (1) A nest-python build-time dedup/cluster pass (Python is the builder, so
             sklearn-class deps are acceptable): before embedding/encoding, run
             TF-IDF-or-embedding cosine + agglomerative threshold clustering to detect
             near-duplicate chunks; keep one canonical chunk per cluster and record collapsed
             members as additive provenance metadata, so chunks_canonical shrinks at the source.
             Pure pre-compression leverage (fewer chunks = fewer embeddings, the dominant byte
             cost) and stays deterministic if you fix linkage=average plus a sorted tie-break,
             matching nest reproducible-build rule. (2) For nest-runtime bm25 path, this repo
             confirms the TF-IDF/cosine-over-sparse-vectors recipe and the precomputed-distance
             trick; nest already does mandatory exact cosine rerank, so the reusable bit is the
             cluster cohesion score (mean intra-group similarity) as a cheap storable confidence
             signal for a future graph-search/dedup section (reserved section id 0x09+). The
             ~30-metric eval suite is reference material for a nest-cli benchmark/quality-report
             surface and for scoring the shipped PT-BR fake-news demo corpus, not core-format
             code.
compression: Indirect but real: agglomerative near-duplicate clustering is a dedup primitive that can run at
             build time in nest-python to drop redundant chunks before embeddings are generated,
             attacking the single largest byte cost (per-chunk f32/f16/int8 vectors) at its
             source. It compresses corpus cardinality, orthogonal to and multiplicative with nest
             per-section codecs (zstd/f16/int8). The metric suite itself compresses nothing.
perf:        O(n^2) memory/time: materializes a dense n-by-n cosine-similarity and distance matrix with
             precomputed-metric agglomerative clustering, so it does not scale past a few thousand
             docs per batch (fine for RSS, a hard wall for whole databases). KMeans/agglomerative
             call vectors.toarray(), densifying the sparse TF-IDF matrix. For nest, any borrowed
             dedup pass must be blocked/batched or swapped to an ANN-neighbor graph (nest already
             ships HnswIndex) to avoid quadratic blowup. Pure Python/sklearn/nltk/spacy stack, no
             SIMD, no native kernels worth importing into the Rust core.
license:     AGPL-3.0 (copyleft; vendoring source into a permissive core is incompatible, so reimplement the
             algorithm cleanly rather than copying code).

[REFERENCE] Cartus/Automated-Fact-Checking-Resources
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A curated, MIT-licensed "awesome list" / paper-and-dataset link index companion to two survey

innovation:  The transferable artifact is not code (there is none) but a precise, widely-adopted decomposition
             of fact-checking into four pipeline stages -- claim detection/check-worthiness ->
             evidence retrieval -> verdict prediction (SUPPORTS / REFUTES / NOT-ENOUGH-INFO) ->

             threads). This is the schema nest needs to model "truth features" and a

techniques:
  - Four-stage AFC pipeline: claim detection/check-worthiness -> evidence retrieval -> verdict
    prediction -> justification
  - Three-label veracity schema (SUPPORTS / REFUTES / NOT ENOUGH INFO) from FEVER, with NEI as an
    explicit abstention class
  - Evidence-granularity taxonomy: sentence vs document vs table vs multi-hop chain vs
    conversation thread
  - Retrieve-then-verify framing where retrieval+verification are jointly 'factual verification'
    (maps onto nest's ANN-then-exact-rerank search contract)
  - Provenance-as-evidence pattern: claims bound to specific Wikipedia sentence ids/spans
    (analogous to nest's nest://content_hash/chunk_id span citations)
  - Multi-hop reasoning chains (HoVer/FEVEROUS) as ordered evidence sets -> motivates a future
    graph-search section linking chunk_ids
  - Check-worthiness as an importance-ranking/triage scoring stage before retrieval
nest steals: No crate steals code (this repo has none and is irrelevant to the FROZEN format). It informs three
             things. (1) nest-cli + demo docs: shape the PT-BR fake-news demo around the
             four-stage pipeline so a 'fact-check' is expressed purely as nest primitives --
             search-ann/search-text retrieve candidate evidence chunks, exact cosine rerank ranks
             them, and a citation nest://content_hash/chunk_id binds the verdict to an exact byte
             span (the provenance section already exists; this gives it semantic purpose). (2)
             provenance section schema (nest-format/src/sections): document a convention for a
             per-chunk 'claim/evidence role' plus a three-value veracity label
             (SUPPORTS/REFUTES/NEI) carried as provenance metadata key-values, staying
             additive-only (no new section id, no version bump). (3) roadmap only: multi-hop
             evidence chains are the concrete use-case justifying the mission's planned 'graph
             search' over chunk_ids, but this must wait for a reserved section id 0x09+ and is out
             of scope for v1. It unlocks a credible, citable fact-checking narrative without
             touching the frozen container or the compression path.
compression: n/a -- this is a link list with zero compression, encoding, quantization, or indexing
             implementation. It contributes nothing to the 'compress more than everyone' north
             star. Its only relevance is domain modeling for the demo corpus and the truth-feature
             narrative, which is orthogonal to the codec and on-disk format.
perf:        No runtime, no benchmarks, no throughput/SIMD/memory characteristics -- it is static markdown.
             Performance n/a. Only operational caveat: any dataset linked here that nest ships
             must be license-checked individually, independent of nest's own posture.
license:     MIT for the repository markdown; underlying datasets carry their own individual licenses (e.g.
             FEVER/FEVEROUS are Wikipedia-derived CC BY-SA share-alike) and must be license-vetted
             per-dataset before shipping any of them in a .nest demo corpus.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[HIGH] PyO3/pyo3
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        PyO3 is a macro-driven Rust<->CPython FFI binding layer that compiles a single cdylib extension
             module against Python's stable ABI (abi3 / Py_LIMITED_API) and enforces GIL-correct,
             refcount-correct interop at compile time via lifetime-tagged smart pointers
             (Python<'py>, Bound<'py,T>, Py<T>).
innovation:  The "stable ABI as a build feature, not a source macro" model: opting into abi3-pyXY via a Cargo
             feature makes the linker emit only Py_LIMITED_API symbols, so ONE compiled .so/.pyd
             is loadable by every CPython >= XY without recompilation -- decoupling the binary
             artifact from the interpreter patch version. Paired with this is the zero-copy borrow
             discipline: Bound<'py,T> is morally (Py<T>, Python<'py>) and the buffer protocol /
             PyBuffer<T> let Rust read a Python array's backing memory in place rather than
             marshalling element-by-element.
techniques:
  - abi3 / Py_LIMITED_API: compile against the limited C-API subset so symbols resolve on any
    future CPython >= floor (lowest abi3-pyXY feature wins); single wheel per OS/arch
  - abi3t (PEP 803): opaque-PyObject stable-ABI variant loadable on BOTH GIL and free-threaded
    builds, forward-compatible to all future 3.X
  - Bound<'py,T> smart pointer = Py<T> handle + Python<'py> GIL-proof token; drops the Python
    refcount immediately on Drop (no GILPool surprise)
  - Py<T> as a Send+Sync, GIL-independent Arc-like handle for storing Python objects in Rust
    structs without a lifetime param
  - buffer protocol via PyBuffer<f32> / rust-numpy PyReadonlyArray1: borrow a contiguous
    numpy/array backing store as &[f32] with zero copy
  - macro codegen: #[pymodule]/#[pyclass]/#[pymethods]/#[pyfunction] generate the limited-API
    wrapper shims, hiding Py_LIMITED_API entirely
  - FromPyObject / IntoPyObject trait-based bidirectional marshalling at the FFI boundary
  - Python::detach (formerly allow_threads): release the GIL around a long Rust compute so other
    threads run; requires unbinding to Py<T>
nest steals: nest-python already does the right baseline (PyO3 0.28, crate-type=cdylib,
             features=[\"abi3-py312\"], Bound API in nest_file.rs/lib.rs), so the steal is two
             concrete upgrades, not adoption. (1) ZERO-COPY QUERY INGESTION on the hot path:
             nest_file.rs:21-24/34-37/47-56 currently do query.extract::<Vec<f32>>(), a full heap
             copy of every query embedding (which arrives from the model as a numpy float32 array)
             before the SIMD dot-product runs. Replace with PyBuffer<f32> (pure-pyo3, no numpy
             dep, abi3 buffer API is available since py3.11 so py312 floor is fine) or an optional
             rust-numpy PyReadonlyArray1 feature, borrowing the array's backing memory as &[f32]
             passed straight to nest-runtime's simd dispatcher -- eliminates one alloc+memcpy per
             search, complementing the already zero-copy mmap read of stored embeddings. (2)
             DISTRIBUTION: wire maturin + pyproject.toml (PyPI/maturin is a listed known gap)
             producing ONE cp312-abi3 manylinux/macos/windows wheel that runs on 3.12/3.13/3.14+,
             and add the abi3t feature path so the single wheel also loads on free-threaded builds
             -- this is the entire \"portable, single-file, install-anywhere\" leg of the north
             star for the python-builds half. Both respect hard constraints: no socket, no panic
             (keep PyValueError mapping of typed thiserror errors), determinism untouched, and
             nest-format/nest-runtime stay pure Rust with PyO3 confined to nest-python.
compression: Indirect. abi3/abi3t and the macro layer do not compress bytes; their relevance is to the
             \"portable, cheap, light, install-anywhere\" axis of the north star -- one tiny wheel
             serving every Python on every OS keeps the distribution surface minimal. The directly
             load-bearing compression-adjacent idea is the zero-copy buffer-protocol borrow: it
             keeps the search hot path allocation-free end-to-end (mmap'd compressed/quantized
             embeddings in, borrowed query in), so the int8/f16/zstd compression wins in
             nest-format are not paid back as marshalling overhead at the python boundary.
perf:        Query path today incurs one Vec<f32> alloc + element marshalling copy per search call
             (nest_file.rs:21-24); PyBuffer/PyReadonlyArray borrow removes it, leaving only the
             mmap SIMD kernel cost. abi3 has a small, documented runtime cost vs version-specific
             builds (cannot inline against a known struct layout) -- acceptable for nest since the
             hot loop is in pure-Rust nest-runtime, not across the FFI boundary. GIL note: nest
             searches are read-only against an mmap, so wrapping the nest-runtime compute in
             Python::detach would let concurrent Python threads issue parallel searches against
             one open NestFile; deterministic because the file is immutable post-build. PyO3 0.28
             requires Rust >= 1.83 (nest is edition 2024 so already on a newer toolchain).
license:     Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual-licensed; permissive, compatible with vendoring/linking)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] clap-rs/clap
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Rust CLI argument parser with a dual builder/derive API, a modular feature-gated crate split
             (clap_builder/clap_derive/clap_lex/clap_complete/clap_mangen), Jaro-distance typo
             correction, and a runtime "ask the binary" dynamic shell-completion protocol.
innovation:  The binary, not a static script, is the source of truth for valid inputs at runtime.
techniques:
  - strsim::jaro did_you_mean at 0.7 cutoff, candidates sorted ascending by similarity
  - CompleteEnv env-var re-exec completion protocol (COMPLETE/_CLAP_COMPLETE_INDEX/_CLAP_IFS)
  - CompletionCandidate type decoupling generation from per-shell rendering
  - feature-gated crate split (clap re-exports clap_builder) for binary-size minimization
  - clap_lex OsStr-aware lossless arg tokenizer
  - derive layer compiling to the same Command/Arg builder model
  - ArgValueCompleter per-arg dynamic candidate closures
nest steals: nest-cli gets Jaro did_you_mean over its 8 subcommand names (clap's `suggestions` feature,
             deterministic). Extend the same Jaro scorer over real container chunk_ids (mmapped by
             nest-runtime) so a missed `nest cite` suggests the nearest valid id without altering
             the frozen format. Adopt CompleteEnv-style re-exec completion that reads the actual
             .nest file for content_hash/chunk_id/preset completion, staying offline and
             single-file. Mirror clap's feature-gated crate split to keep hnsw/bm25/pyo3 opt-in
             for the smallest sovereign binary.
compression: n/a directly. clap is a CLI ergonomics/distribution-surface project with no entropy coder,
             quantizer, or on-disk codec to lift; it does not advance the
             compress-more-than-everyone north star. Its value to nest is purely the client
             surface (typo correction, dynamic completion, binary-size minimization), which
             improves the portable/clean/light pillars but not raw compression ratio.
perf:        Parsing is allocation-light; clap_lex avoids premature UTF-8 decoding (works on OsStr) and the
             builder model is the runtime data structure with no reflection. Binary size is
             controlled by opt-in cargo features rather than a monolith. No SIMD or hot-path
             numerics relevant to nest's scoring kernels; the Jaro suggestion path runs only on
             the error branch so its cost is irrelevant. CompleteEnv runs before app init to keep
             completion latency low, but a nest re-exec would still pay the process-start cost
             (cheap for the Rust binary, but note the known ~300-500ms python boot overhead must
             NOT be on this path -- completion must stay in the pure-rust nest-cli/nest-runtime
             layer).
license:     MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual-licensed)

[MEDIUM] weaviate/weaviate-python-client
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        The official Python client for Weaviate: a gRPC/protobuf v4 client where vectors are serialized as
             length-prefixed flat float32 byte blobs, batching is auto-tuned for throughput, and a
             VectorType enum tags single vs multi-vector layouts inside one wire field.
innovation:  Tag-then-pack vector serialization: a single bytes field carries an entire vector (or a flattened
             multi-vector matrix) as a contiguous little-endian float32 blob, prefixed only with
             the per-row dimension as a uint16, and disambiguated by a tiny VectorType enum
             (VECTOR_TYPE_SINGLE_FP32 / MULTI_FP32). One field, one struct.pack call, no
             per-element protobuf framing -- the dtype/layout lives in a side enum, not in the
             bytes. This is exactly the "type tag + raw contiguous payload, mmap/decode in one
             shot" discipline nest needs for its embeddings and named-vector sections.
techniques:
  - Vector wire encoding: struct.pack("{}f".format(n), *vec) -> one contiguous float32 blob, zero
    per-element framing
  - Multi-vector encoding: struct.pack("<H", row_dim) prefix + flattened float32 matrix; row width
    from the uint16, count from len(bytes)//(dim*4)
  - VectorType side-enum (SINGLE_FP32 / MULTI_FP32) tags layout/dtype out-of-band so the payload
    stays a raw blob -- additive room for future int8/fp16 tags
  - Symmetric _ByteOps decoder: struct.unpack(f"{len//UINT32_LEN}f") infers element count from
    byte length, no stored count needed
  - Three explicit batching policies: dynamic (auto-tunes batch size + concurrency for
    throughput), fixed_size, rate_limit -- a tuning taxonomy, not one magic knob
  - Context-manager batch lifecycle with auto-flush on __exit__ and early-abort on error threshold
    (number_errors)
  - Layered .proto schema (base -> batch/search_get -> weaviate) keeping wire structs additive and
    codegen-stable across grpcio/protobuf skew
  - Producer/consumer queue feeding gRPC unary-unary BatchObjects, decoupling Python
    object-creation rate from server request concurrency
nest steals: nest-python (the pyo3 builder) and nest-format encoding/sections: adopt the same tag-then-pack
             discipline for any future named/multi-vector embeddings section. Today nest stores a
             single embeddings matrix; when it adds named vectors (text + image per chunk), copy
             Weaviate's layout exactly -- a per-section dtype enum (already implied by nest's
             encoding ids f32/f16/int8) plus a uint16/uint32 little-endian row-dim prefix, then a
             contiguous blob the rust runtime mmaps straight into the SIMD dot-product kernel with
             zero per-element decode. nest already mandates repr(C), little-endian unsigned,
             contiguous-for-mmap; Weaviate validates that the flat-blob-plus-side-tag shape is
             correct and shows recovering element count from byte length (len//4) instead of
             storing a redundant count, saving header bytes. Separately, nest-python's bulk-build
             path should borrow the producer/consumer + auto-flush + error-threshold batch
             ergonomics so large-corpus builds stream chunks without holding everything in memory.
             Relevance to extreme compression: the side-enum-tags-dtype pattern is precisely what
             lets nest swap f32->f16->int8 per named-vector without touching byte layout or
             bumping NEST_FORMAT_VERSION, keeping the format additive while the codec gets
             smaller.
compression: Indirect. No entropy coder or quantization codec here -- vectors ship raw fp32, no compression on
             the embedding payload. The transferable compression-adjacent lesson is the
             side-enum-tags-dtype pattern: it makes per-vector dtype (fp32/fp16/int8) a 1-byte tag
             rather than a layout change, so nest can shrink embedding sections by swapping codecs
             without format churn. The dimension-as-uint16-prefix + count-from-byte-length trick
             also shaves redundant header bytes. Batching/throughput work is orthogonal to
             compression.
perf:        Single struct.pack per vector emits a contiguous fp32 blob with no per-element protobuf tag
             overhead -- the documented "key efficiency feature" of the gRPC path vs the old
             REST/JSON client. Decode side (_ByteOps) is a single struct.unpack over the whole
             buffer. Dynamic batching co-tunes batch size and concurrent request count to maximize
             ingest throughput while bounding memory (no unbounded batch growth). gRPC default
             port 50051, unary-unary RPCs. Note: this is a network client (REST+gRPC over sockets)
             -- nest is offline-by-construction and must NOT adopt the transport, only the
             byte-layout and batch-ergonomics ideas.
license:     BSD-3-Clause

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[MEDIUM] weaviate/weaviate-go-client
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A native Go client for Weaviate that layers a fluent builder-pattern API over dual GraphQL
             (precise field selection) and gRPC/protobuf transports, with the gRPC path packing
             dense vectors as raw little-endian float32 byte blobs rather than repeated proto
             float fields.
innovation:  On the gRPC wire path, vectors are not sent as `repeated float` (which costs ~5 bytes/element with
             protobuf varint+tag framing and forces per-element decode); they are packed into a
             single opaque `bytes` field carrying contiguous little-endian float32 (the
             `_Packing{bytes_, type_}` wrapper), with an enum tag discriminating 1D vectors, 2D
             multi-vectors, and quantized forms. This is exactly nest's hot-path principle: a
             vector section is one contiguous mmap-able byte run scored directly, never a
             decode-per-element structure.
techniques:
  - gRPC vector byte-packing: dense embeddings serialized as one contiguous little-endian float32
    `bytes` blob + a type enum (1D / 2D multivector / quantized), instead of `repeated float`
    proto fields
  - relativeScoreFusion: per-result-set min-max normalization (max->1, min->0, linear in between)
    of vector-distance and BM25 scores, then weighted sum -- an alternative to pure
    reciprocal-rank fusion
  - BM25 OR/AND operator with `minimumOrTokensMatch` (minimum_should_match): a tunable knob
    requiring N of the query tokens to be present before a doc is eligible
  - Builder-pattern argument unification: every search argument (NearVector, NearObject, Hybrid,
    BM25, filters) is one typed builder with `WithCertainty/WithLimit/WithTargetVectors/...`,
    replacing v2's stringly-typed GraphQL/filter construction
  - Named/target vectors: `WithTargetVectors` selects which of multiple per-object vector spaces
    to score against in a single request
  - Transport abstraction with graceful fallback: gRPC enabled when `GrpcConfig` present, else
    REST/HTTP; cloud auto-derives the gRPC endpoint from the REST URL
  - API-group partitioning of the surface (Batch/Data/GraphQL/Schema/Backup/...) so each
    subcommand is a small single-responsibility unit
nest steals: Two concrete steals. (1) Codec/format insight, nest-format encoding + nest-runtime/search.rs:
             weaviate's `bytes`-not-`repeated-float` packing validates nest's mmap-contiguous
             embedding layout and points at a cheap win -- when nest exposes vectors over any
             future bridge (nest-python pyo3 or a thin export), hand back the raw contiguous
             f32/f16/int8 slice + a 1-byte encoding-id discriminator (mirror of weaviate's type
             enum) so the consumer slices the mmap directly with zero per-element decode, matching
             nest's existing encoding ids (raw/zstd/float16/int8) without touching the frozen
             layout (additive: it is a read-side view, no new section). (2) Search semantics,
             nest-runtime/bm25 + search.rs: adopt `minimum_should_match`/OR-operator as a
             deterministic BM25 knob (alphabetical term order preserved, fully reproducible) and
             evaluate relativeScoreFusion (min-max normalized weighted sum) as a typed alternative
             to nest's current reciprocal-rank fusion in the hybrid preset -- both feed the SAME
             mandatory exact-cosine rerank, so ground-truth scoring is unchanged. Surface both as
             flags on nest-cli `search-text`/hybrid, mirroring weaviate's builder ergonomics with
             clap.
compression: Indirect but real: the gRPC `bytes`-packing trick is the wire-format twin of nest's core
             compression decision to keep embeddings as a raw contiguous mmap run scored directly
             (never zstd, never per-element proto framing). It confirms that for vectors the win
             is layout + quantization (int8/f16), not entropy coding, and that the read surface
             should expose the packed bytes + a small encoding tag so downstream tools never
             re-inflate. No new entropy coder, quantizer, or graph layout is offered here -- this
             is a bindings/ergonomics repo, so its compression contribution is validation of an
             existing nest choice plus a clean discriminator-tag pattern.
perf:        gRPC path is HTTP/2 + protobuf; the byte-packed vector blob avoids protobuf's per-element
             varint/tag overhead (~5 bytes/float for repeated) and per-element allocation, giving
             a measurably smaller, faster-to-decode payload than GraphQL JSON -- the stated
             motivation for the experimental gRPC Search being a drop-in replacement for GraphQL
             Get. Java v6 client switched embeddings to `float[]` (vs boxed) for GC-friendliness,
             a parallel lesson: keep vectors as flat primitive arrays. Go's net/http provides
             default connection pooling; client connection timeout defaults to 60s.
license:     BSD-3-Clause

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[LOW] weaviate/weaviate-helm
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
what:        A Helm chart (Go-templated Kubernetes YAML) that deploys the Weaviate vector DB as a StatefulSet,
             driven by a single layered values.yaml whose uniform per-module schema
             (enabled/repo/tag/registry/inferenceUrl/envconfig/resources) is compiled into
             ConfigMaps, Secrets, and an aggregated ENABLE_MODULES env var.
innovation:  The single-source-of-truth declarative config compiler: one flat values.yaml with a strictly
             uniform per-feature block schema, where every optional capability is a
             self-describing record carrying its own enable flag, version pin (tag), and key/value
             envconfig, and a helper layer (_helpers.tpl) deterministically folds the set of
             enabled blocks into one aggregated activation list (ENABLE_MODULES) plus generated
             secrets. The config schema IS the feature manifest; toggling a boolean adds/removes a
             whole subsystem with zero imperative wiring.
techniques:
  - Uniform per-feature config record schema: every optional subsystem
    (module/backup/offload/auth) shares identical fields {enabled, tag, registry, repo, envconfig,
    resources}, so the parser/validator is written once and reused for all features
  - Deterministic aggregation fold: enabled blocks are collected and emitted as one comma-joined
    activation string (ENABLE_MODULES) in declaration order, a reproducible reduce over the config
    set
  - Version pinning by 'tag' on every component with a documented warning that unpinned tags break
    reproducibility on chart upgrade
  - Dual-source config: inline 'env'/'envconfig' values vs 'envSecrets' references, with a fixed
    precedence rule (env overrides static blocks)
  - inferenceUrl indirection: a capability is satisfied locally (deploy a pod) OR by pointing at
    an external endpoint, selected by one field without changing the rest of the schema
  - enabled:false short-circuit: nested settings under a disabled block are ignored entirely,
    keeping config trees inert until activated
  - PVC naming as a pure function of {storage.name, replica-index} for stable predictable on-disk
    identity
  - Pinned distribution channel: chart published to a static GitHub Pages Helm repo, versioned
    releases tied to git tags
nest steals: Adapt the uniform-config-record + deterministic-fold pattern to nest-cli and the nest-format
             manifest. (1) nest-cli: replace ad-hoc preset flags with a single declarative build
             profile (TOML/YAML) where each section/codec is a uniform record {enabled, encoding,
             params} mirroring presets exact/compressed/tiny/hybrid; a helper folds enabled
             records into the section table the writer emits, so adding
             hnsw_index(0x07)/bm25_index(0x08) is one boolean, never imperative wiring -- an
             additive-format-friendly config surface nest currently lacks. (2) nest-format
             manifest: formalize the manifest as the single source of truth (each section a
             self-describing record carrying its encoding id + per-section checksum) that a
             deterministic fold turns into physical layout, reinforcing reproducible
             byte-identical builds. (3) nest-python maturin/PyPI gap: copy version-pinning-by-tag
             discipline and the static-repo distribution model (GitHub Pages Helm repo == a pinned
             wheel index) so profiles record exact model fingerprint + codec versions,
             guaranteeing same-input => same-file. (4) inferenceUrl indirection maps to nest's
             local-vs-external embedding source: one field selects 'embed locally' vs 'ingest
             precomputed embeddings' without schema change.
compression: Essentially none directly. This repo contains zero compression, quantization, entropy-coding,
             graph-layout, or SIMD content -- it is pure Kubernetes deployment YAML. The only
             oblique relevance to the compress-more-than-everyone north star is meta: a clean
             declarative build-profile schema (uniform codec records folded deterministically into
             the section table) makes it cheaper to add and combine new codecs/presets in nest-cli
             and keeps reproducible builds honest, indirectly accelerating compression
             experimentation. It does not move the compression ratio itself.
perf:        Not applicable -- no runtime/data-plane code; Helm is build-time template rendering. The only
             perf-adjacent design choice is StatefulSet podManagementPolicy=Parallel (all pods
             start simultaneously for Raft bootstrap), which is irrelevant to nest's offline
             single-file no-socket model.
license:     BSD-3-Clause (matches the main Weaviate project)
